# D5's Driver 2 — the GitHub Source Driver Implementation Plan

> **WRITTEN 2026-09-24. SITTING 1 — TASK 1, THE MEASUREMENTS — RAN ON 2026-09-24; SITTING 2 (TASKS 2 AND 3) IS NEXT.** Every premise held, and the measurements found that one rewrite of GitHub's `main` freezes the mirror's (F6) — corrected in Tasks 7, 9 and 11 — and that Task 13's check crosses its own line (F7). **Rich decided F7 the same day: the summary becomes STRUCTURED OUTPUT, and Task 13 is rewritten for it (Decisions 19 and 22); he also switched the chat model to `qwen3.5:4b`, with thinking off.** No sitting boundary moved. The `[M<n>]` blocks at the top of Tasks 2, 4, 5, 7, 8, 9, 11 and 13 are the corrections; [`spikes/d5-baseline/`](../spikes/d5-baseline/README.md) is the record. **Its three spec actions were approved by Rich and APPLIED the same day** (*Spec actions*, below): §20's push-time sentence as option (a), §21's inventory row, and §20's one unscoped token — the last *unless the conformance run says otherwise*. *What executing this plan found* is empty and is where every sitting's record goes.

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking. **Commit on `main`; no branch, no worktree, no push** — ORIENTATION §6 rule 9, which both of those skills will push you against.

**Goal:** A Manifest deployment can keep every application's source in a GitHub organisation instead of in bare repositories on the laptop. The control plane holds a GitHub App's private key in the master key's custody class and acts through short-lived installation tokens scoped to one repository. Every repository is private and kept private. A push reaches Manifest by an HMAC-verified webhook, and every commit Manifest learns of is scanned for secrets. `main` cannot be force-pushed or deleted. `Project` says where the code lives. Builds still run offline from a local mirror. **It is proved against a GitHub-compatible fake in a container, offline, and checked against real GitHub by an opt-in run.**

**Architecture:** D5's `SourceDriver` gets its second implementation, and **the build path goes behind the interface** for the first time. Today two callers read a laptop path straight off `RepoRef` (roadmap finding 2). They now ask `localGitDir(repo, commitSha)` for the commit, present in a bare repository on this machine. **The GitHub driver keeps a LOCAL MIRROR at exactly the path driver 1's bare repository has** (roadmap finding 1), so `build/` does not change, a mirrored commit builds with the network off (C1), and §13's *same source → same digest* holds. GitHub is the durable copy. The mirror is what builds, and it is fetched with a **non-forced** refspec, so a rewritten upstream `main` can never take away the history an approved release names. **One driver per control-plane process** (`MANIFEST_SOURCE_DRIVER`, default `local`). Each project records its provider, and an operation on a project the running driver did not create is refused rather than guessed at. **Webhooks reach the control plane the way the edge itself does** — `host.docker.internal:7100`, never through the edge. They are verified by HMAC before anything else, recorded once by delivery id, and processed off the request by one per-process queue. **Every advance of the mirror, whatever caused it, goes through one observer**, which is where history rewrites, a repository found public and secrets found in pushed commits become Events. The fake is **our own**. It is a zero-dependency workspace package, run from TypeScript source by Node 22 in a container on the platform network. Three things its author did not write hold it honest: GitHub's own OpenAPI schemas, GitHub's published HMAC test vector, and the golden answers of the real App.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript on Node **24.12.0** (host) and **22.23.2** (`node:22-alpine`, the fake's container — measured 2026-09-24). Fastify **5.12.3** (installed, the only version in the store — *its own `fastify.version` constant says `5.12.2`*, which is where this line first came from; Task 1 `[M2]`, F1), Drizzle over Postgres 16, `zod/v4`, Vitest 2.1, TypeScript **5.9.3**, `git` **2.50.1** (Apple, host) and **2.49.1** (Alpine 3.22's package, in the fake). React 19 + Vite in `packages/console`, and `@manifest/contract` generated by `openapi-typescript` and driven through `openapi-fetch`. Ajv **8.20.0**, already in the store for `@manifest/mock`. **No new external package is installed.** `packages/github-fake` is a new *workspace* package with zero runtime dependencies. Linking it is `pnpm install --offline`, and Task 1 measures that before Task 4 relies on it. The JWT is signed with `node:crypto`; there is no `jsonwebtoken` and no Octokit.

**Spec:** [`../specs/2026-08-29-manifest-platform-design.md`](../specs/2026-08-29-manifest-platform-design.md). Read these sections: **D5** and **D14** in §4; **§20 *Git driver*** (all five bullets), *Key management*, *Credential classes* and the *Control map*'s confused-deputy and secret-exfiltration rows; **§12 *Supply chain*** (the secret gate is pattern-based and runs offline); **§21** *Platform inventory*, *Honest divergences* 8 (platform containers can reach the host, app containers cannot) and *Commands*; **§13 *Integrity of the gate*** (a release's commit must stay buildable); §5's module map (`source/  git provider drivers (local, github)`); §6's `Project`.

**Roadmap:** the section *D5's driver 2 — the GitHub source driver* in [`2026-08-29-plan-roadmap.md`](./2026-08-29-plan-roadmap.md). Its three findings are this plan's starting point: the builder does not use the source driver; the interface exists but the build path is not behind it; use two App registrations, not one. So does its rule: *anything that widens who can change a spec goes after the gate that inspects spec changes.* That gate is P6b, which is executed, and **this plan is the first thing that widens who can write `manifest.yaml`**. Write every control as though a hostile push will arrive at it.

**Predecessor:** [`2026-09-22-p6b-subsequent-releases.md`](./2026-09-22-p6b-subsequent-releases.md), executed 2026-09-23. **Its *What this plan does not build* is an input list for this plan**: F9 and F11–F15 are Tasks 13 and 14. F10 was fixed on 2026-09-24 in its own sitting and is **not** inherited. It is also the house style this plan follows.

**Successor:** the authoring API ([`2026-09-19-authoring-api-brief.md`](./2026-09-19-authoring-api-brief.md), §6; Rich's order, 2026-09-22), which writes through `SourceDriver.commitFiles`. **This plan gives that method its second implementation and its pre-push secret scan. The authoring API is what makes both load-bearing.**

## How this plan is to be executed — sittings, one per session

**One sitting per session, with a check-in at each boundary.** This pattern has carried every plan since P4a. It means a session limit never lands in the middle of a task. This plan commits after every task: a stop *between* tasks is recoverable, a stop *inside* one is not.

> **EIGHT SITTINGS — RICH'S SPLIT OF 2026-09-24** (ORIENTATION §8, *Decided*): 1 the measurements; 2 the build
> path behind `SourceDriver` and the App key's custody; 3 the fake; 4 the driver; 5 webhooks and enforced-private;
> 6 push-time secret scanning, `main` protected and the repository link; 7 P6b's inherited findings; 8 the
> acceptance. **This plan puts the conformance run (Task 6) in sitting 3**, because it is the fake's check. That
> placement makes sitting 3 the heaviest, so **if sitting 3 runs long, stop after Task 5 and sweep; Task 6 then
> opens sitting 4**, ahead of Task 7, with which it shares nothing. Sitting 6 is the other heavy one.

*The **Status** column records what a sitting made true, never how many findings it produced. That number lives once, in the roadmap's defect-rate table (Rich, 2026-09-20).*

| Sitting | Tasks | What it delivers | `pnpm test:docker` owed? | Status |

**THIS TABLE IS A SCHEDULE, NOT A CONTRACT.** Moving task boundaries is Task 1's job. **If a measurement breaks Rich's split, say so to Rich before sitting 2 and let him re-cut it. Task 15 stays alone and last**: it is this plan's own task, not something that happens after the last feature.

## Decided by Rich — build them, do not re-open them

**Each of these was answered by Rich from options with their costs stated. Do not re-ask any of them** (ORIENTATION §8, *Decided*).

- **The stand-in is a FAKE plus an opt-in REAL check** (2026-09-24). A small GitHub-compatible fake **in a container** carries the acceptance offline and in CI. It has App JWT → per-repository installation tokens, private repositories, git over HTTP and HMAC-signed webhooks, and it is the only way a webhook can reach a laptop at all. A short **opt-in** conformance run against a real GitHub App (`Manifest (local dev)`, on Rich's account, network on, at his yes) checks that the fake answers what GitHub answers. **UBC's own organisation stays on the external track.** *Rejected:* the fake alone (a fake written by the agent that writes the driver agrees with the driver — P3's false-green shape); a real org alone (the network and an external account on the acceptance path).
- **Eight sittings, split as the table above** (2026-09-24). *Offered:* seven (merging 6 and 7, putting the inheritance in the sitting most likely to run long) and ten (splitting the fake and the mirror).
- **The order after this plan: the authoring API, then the vulnerability database in the console** (2026-09-22 and 2026-09-24). This plan neither designs for the authoring API nor blocks it. It gives `commitFiles` a second implementation and a pre-push scan, and leaves the write surface to that plan.
- **F10 is fixed and NOT inherited** (`0aa1824`, 2026-09-24). **F9 and F11–F15 ARE inherited** — Tasks 13 and 14.
- **The approval summary is STRUCTURED OUTPUT** (2026-09-24, answering sitting 1's F7). The model fills a JSON schema — one `exposure` sentence per change, `path` an enum of the diff's own paths — through LiteLLM's `response_format`, with no field a verdict could go in; Task 13 is rewritten for it, and Decision 22 makes it the rule for every platform model call whose answer is read or shown. *Offered:* (a) keep the prose and narrow the check; (b) the check as written, withholding ~7 in 10; (c) a harder prompt.
- **The chat model is `qwen3.5:4b`, with thinking OFF, and a `-reasoning` name beside each chat name** (2026-09-24). `infra/litellm/config.yaml` maps `default-chat` and `default-chat-onprem` to `ollama_chat/qwen3.5:4b` with Ollama's own `think: false` pinned — first as `reasoning_effort: none`, replaced the same afternoon because a request's own `reasoning_effort` overrode it and emptied the answer; LiteLLM applies the mapping's `think` after a request's `reasoning_effort`, so the pin now holds. `default-chat-reasoning` and `default-chat-onprem-reasoning` are the same model with thinking ON, chosen by declaring them in `ai.models`. Without the pin it streams 0 content frames in 260 (S3 Evidence 9, re-measured), and `make verify` and `ai-path.docker.test.ts` go red. **Its two spec actions (§7's catalogue and reasoning paragraph, §21's "non-thinking" sentence) were approved and APPLIED the same day, after Rich read the wording** (the roadmap's *Spec actions raised by the chat-model switch*).
- **Carried from earlier plans, still binding:** D24's privileged four and the person-only class (§20); step-up for a production deploy and for an approval; the stored preview an approval binds; `SENSITIVE_FIELDS` as §7's list; an added CWL attribute waits for IAM and a removed one does not; a PIA never returns to `draft` automatically.

## What Rich does, and when

**Three things in this plan are Rich's hands, not an agent's.** Each is asked at the sitting that needs it, never assumed.

1. **The network, for the fake's image — sitting 3, Task 5.** The fake needs `git` in its image and there is no apk mirror (ORIENTATION §8), so `apk add git` runs when the image is built, which is `make seed`'s step 2 — the one step that needs the network (C1). **Ask Rich for the network, then run `docker compose -f infra/compose.yaml -p manifest --env-file .env --profile github build github-fake`** — the one image, not a whole re-seed, which also rebuilds every image, re-mirrors and re-warms (P6b sitting 3 chose the same narrowing for the vulnerability database). Measured on 2026-09-24: `apk add --no-cache git` in `alpine:3.22` installs git 2.49.1, 6.8 MiB, 28 packages.
2. **Registering `Manifest (local dev)` — any time before the conformance run's real leg (Task 6, sitting 3; again in sitting 8). Optional**: every sitting is green without it, and the conformance run says `SKIPPED — no real App configured` rather than failing. **The settings, exactly:**
   - **Create a FREE organisation** (github.com → your avatar → *Your organizations* → *New organization* → *Free*), named for this purpose, e.g. `<you>-manifest-dev`. **An organisation, not your personal account**: GitHub's own OpenAPI description marks `POST /user/repos` **`enabledForGitHubApps: false`** and `POST /orgs/{org}/repos` `true` (read 2026-09-24, description 1.1.4), so an installation token **cannot** create a repository on a personal account. A dedicated organisation also keeps the App's reach to repositories that exist only for this.
   - **In that organisation**: *Settings* → *Developer settings* → *GitHub Apps* → *New GitHub App*. Register it *under the organisation* so that *Only on this account* means the organisation.
     - **Name:** `Manifest (local dev)`. App names are unique across GitHub; if it is taken, `Manifest (local dev, <you>)`. **Homepage URL:** the organisation's page. **Callback URL**, **Setup URL**: empty. *Request user authorization (OAuth) during installation*: **off**. *Enable Device Flow*: **off**.
     - **Webhook: untick *Active*.** This laptop has no public address, so GitHub could not deliver to it. The fake carries every webhook in this plan, and GitHub's published HMAC test vector checks its signing.
     - **Repository permissions: *Administration* — Read and write** (create, change visibility, protect a branch, delete). ***Contents* — Read and write** (git fetch and push). ***Metadata* — Read-only** (mandatory). **Everything else: No access. No organisation or account permissions. Subscribe to no events.**
     - **Where can this GitHub App be installed: *Only on this account*.**
   - **Create it**, and note the **App ID** on its page.
   - ***Private keys* → *Generate a private key***. A `.pem` downloads. **Move it into the master key's custody and nowhere else:** `mv ~/Downloads/<name>.<date>.private-key.pem infra/secrets/github-app.pem && chmod 600 infra/secrets/github-app.pem`. `infra/secrets/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:48`, and `git check-ignore -v infra/secrets/github-app.pem` answers that line — checked 2026-09-24). **Never paste the key into a chat, `.env` or a ticket.**
   - ***Install App*** → the organisation → ***All repositories***. Note the **installation id**, the number at the end of the page's URL (`…/settings/installations/<id>`).
   - **Write `infra/secrets/github-conformance.json`** with `{"appId": <id>, "installationId": <id>, "org": "<org>"}`, then `chmod 600`. The key's path is fixed: `infra/secrets/github-app.pem`.
   - **To revoke:** delete the key or the App on the same page. **This App must never be installed on UBC's organisation.** A key mints tokens for EVERY installation of its App, and a laptop key that could reach production repositories is exactly what the roadmap's finding 3 (*two App registrations, not one*) exists to make impossible by construction.
3. **The network, and a yes, for the conformance run's real leg — sitting 3 (if the App exists) and sitting 8.** It creates two private repositories named `mf-conformance-<timestamp>-a` and `-b` in that organisation, pushes one commit to one of them, and deletes both. **That is an outward action on a real account, so it runs only at Rich's word, each time.** And the clicked half of the acceptance in sitting 8: a person clicks, the agent reads the screens, and **Rich types every password** (§4: the Chrome extension will not).

## Read this first — what this plan knows that the roadmap and ORIENTATION §7e do not

**Read from the code at `e038685` and measured on this machine on 2026-09-24, while this plan was written.** The raw output of every measurement below is in the writing session's scratchpad. Task 1 re-runs each one marked *(T1: M<n>)* into `spikes/d5-baseline/`, where it becomes the record. **A plan is a hypothesis.** P6b's acceptance alone found three of its own plan's predictions wrong.

1. **GITEA AND FORGEJO DO NOT IMPLEMENT GITHUB APPS, SO THE FAKE IS OUR OWN.** Gitea's published OpenAPI document (`https://gitea.com/swagger.v1.json`, version `1.27.0+dev-954-g1f3981a301`, **342 paths**) and Forgejo's (`https://codeberg.org/swagger.v1.json`, `16.0.0-dev-753-6bcc6da0+gitea-1.22.0`, **326 paths**) have **zero** paths matching `/app`, `installation` or `access_token`. Forgejo v15 added repository-scoped *user* access tokens, which is a different model. *(T1: M3 — re-read the two documents.)*
2. **GIT OVER HTTP NEEDS NOTHING BUT CORE GIT.** A 40-line Node server that answers `GET …/info/refs?service=` with a pkt-line service header, then spawns `git upload-pack` / `git receive-pack --stateless-rpc`, served a push, a 3 MB push and a clone from the host's git 2.50.1. Alpine's `git` 2.49.1 answers `--stateless-rpc --advertise-refs` for both services inside a container. **Alpine's `git` package ships no `git-http-backend`** (`/usr/libexec/git-core/git-http-backend: No such file or directory`), and the fake does not need one. *(T1: M4.)*
3. **A TOKEN PASSED TO GIT ON THE COMMAND LINE OR IN THE URL LEAKS INTO THE ERROR MESSAGE `local-driver.ts` ALREADY BUILDS.** That file's `git()` wraps every failure as `` `git ${args[0]} failed: ${String(error)}` ``, and `String(error)` from `execFile` begins `Command failed: git <every argument>`. **Measured with a canary token against a port nothing listens on:** `-c http.extraHeader=Authorization: Basic <b64>` → the message contains the base64 of the token; `http://x-access-token:<token>@host/…` → the message contains the token itself; **`GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=1 GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0=http.extraHeader GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0=…` in the environment → the message contains neither, and the same form authenticates a real push.** And `SourceError`'s message goes ON THE WIRE, because `api/errors.ts:639-650` answers every `SourceError` as `409 { code, message }`. **So the obvious ways to hand git a token publish it to the API client.** *(T1: M5.)*
4. **THE BUILD'S SECRET GATE MISSES GITHUB'S NEW INSTALLATION-TOKEN FORMAT, AND THIS PLAN PUTS THOSE TOKENS INTO CIRCULATION.** GitHub's REST description (1.1.4, read 2026-09-24) says of `POST /app/installations/{installation_id}/access_tokens`: *"Starting April 27, 2026, GitHub began a staged rollout of a stateless format (`ghs_APPID_JWT`) to all newly minted GitHub App installation tokens… If your application expects or relies on installation tokens being exactly 40 characters long, it may not handle this new token format correctly."* Measured against `build/gates.ts:42-58`'s patterns: a classic 40-character `ghs_…` token is caught by *a GitHub token*. **A `ghs_1234567_eyJ…` token of 460 characters is caught by NEITHER pattern.** *a GitHub token* needs 36 alphanumerics after `ghs_` and meets `_` after seven. *a JSON Web Token* begins `\beyJ`, and there is no word boundary between `_` and `e`. A bare JWT is still caught. *(T1: M6.)*
5. **THE WEBHOOK CAN TAKE THE EDGE'S OWN ROUTE TO THE CONTROL PLANE, AND THE EDGE STAYS AS IT IS.** The edge reaches the control plane at `host.docker.internal:7100` (`infra/caddy/Caddyfile:69`), and the control plane binds `127.0.0.1` (`src/index.ts:368`). **Measured with a throwaway listener on `127.0.0.1:7197`:** a container on `manifest-platform` reached it through `host.docker.internal`, both with Docker Desktop's own name and with `--add-host host.docker.internal:host-gateway`. The same container, asking the edge's console site at `10.89.0.10`, got **`403`**, which is the `@outside` refusal (`Caddyfile:64`). And the console site forwards only `/v1/*` and `/auth/*` (`Caddyfile:68`), so nothing arriving through the edge can reach a `/webhooks/…` path at all. S6 measured that app networks cannot reach the host. *(T1: M7.)*
6. **HMAC's OBVIOUS NODE IMPLEMENTATION CRASHES ON AN ABSENT SIGNATURE.** `crypto.timingSafeEqual(Buffer.from(header ?? ''), expected)` → **throws `ERR_CRYPTO_TIMING_SAFE_EQUAL_LENGTH`** for an absent, empty or short header, which is a `500`, not a refusal. The codebase's pattern is `a.length === b.length && timingSafeEqual(a, b)` (`tokens/token.ts:83`, `identity/session.ts:119`, `runtime/docker/registry-auth.ts:117`). **GitHub's published test vector**: the secret `It's a Secret to Everybody` over the payload `Hello, World!` gives `sha256=757107ea0eb2509fc211221cce984b8a37570b6d7586c22c46f4379c8b043e17`. That value was computed here and matches `docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/validating-webhook-deliveries`, which also says `X-Hub-Signature` (SHA-1) *"is only included for legacy purposes"*. **This is the 2026-09-24 lesson (ORIENTATION §7e) with its mechanism measured**: a check that verifies a signature that is PRESENT must also refuse one that is ABSENT. *(T1: M8.)*
7. **A RAW-BODY PARSER SCOPED TO ONE FASTIFY PLUGIN LEAVES EVERY OTHER ROUTE ALONE.** In Fastify 5.12.2, a child plugin that calls `removeContentTypeParser('application/json')` and then adds a `parseAs: 'buffer'` parser gave its own route a `Buffer`. A sibling `/v1/x` still got a parsed object, and still answered `413` at 2 MiB under the default 1 MiB limit, while the webhook route took 2 MiB under its own `bodyLimit`. A request with no content type answered `415` in **Fastify's own body**, because a bare test app has no error handler; in the real server `setErrorHandler` maps it. *(T1: M9.)*
8. **GITHUB'S OWN DESCRIPTION SETTLES THREE FACTS THIS PLAN WOULD OTHERWISE GUESS.** Downloaded 2026-09-24 from `github/rest-api-description` (`api.github.com.json`, 12,964,527 bytes, `info.version` 1.1.4, OpenAPI 3.0.3). **(a)** Creating a repository through an App works only in an organisation: `POST /orgs/{org}/repos` is `enabledForGitHubApps: true` and `POST /user/repos` is `false`. **(b)** *"Protected branches are available in public repositories with GitHub Free and GitHub Free for organizations, and in public and private repositories with GitHub Pro, GitHub Team, GitHub Enterprise Cloud, and GitHub Enterprise Server."* **So on a free organisation, a PRIVATE repository's `main` cannot be protected** — which is exactly what Rich's `Manifest (local dev)` organisation will be. **(c)** An installation token *"cannot be granted access to repositories that the installation was not granted access to"*, takes `repositories` or `repository_ids` (up to 500) and `permissions`, and expires after one hour. *(T1: M10.)*
9. **GITHUB'S OWN SCHEMAS CAN HOLD THE FAKE HONEST, OFFLINE.** The seven operations and three webhook payloads the fake serves pull **31 component schemas, 114,332 bytes**, out of the description. That is small enough to commit. Ajv 8.20.0 (already in the store) **honours OpenAPI 3.0's `nullable`**: `{type:'string', nullable:true}` accepts `null`. `full-repository` has **75 required fields**, `installation` 17, `integration` 11, `installation-token` 2, `webhook-push` 12 and `webhook-repository-publicized` 3. So the fake must generate every one of them, and a schema test is what says it did. *(T1: M11.)*
10. **THE FAKE CAN RUN FROM TYPESCRIPT SOURCE WITH NO BUILD STEP AND NO DEPENDENCY.** `node:22-alpine` is **22.23.2** (on this machine, and in `infra/images.txt`), and it strips erasable types unflagged. **It cannot resolve the repository's NodeNext `./x.js` specifier to `./x.ts`** (`ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`). A 12-line `node:module` `registerHooks` resolve hook, `--import`ed, fixes it in the container (22.23.2) and on the host (24.12.0). So the fake keeps house style (`.js` specifiers, the base tsconfig) and needs `erasableSyntaxOnly: true` (TypeScript 5.9.3 has it). *(T1: M12.)*
11. **A PLAIN `docker compose build` SKIPS A SERVICE BEHIND A PROFILE.** Measured with a two-service throwaway project and `--dry-run`: `compose build` built `always` only; `--profile github build` built both. **So `make seed`'s step 2 (`$COMPOSE build`, `infra/seed/seed.sh:14`) would never build the fake** unless it names the profile. *(T1: M13 — and measure what `compose down` does with a running profiled service, which was NOT measured.)*
12. **GIT ALREADY PROTECTS HISTORY THE WAY THIS PLAN NEEDS, ON BOTH SIDES.** On a bare repository, `receive.denyNonFastForwards true` and `receive.denyDeletes true` refused a force-push (`denying non-fast-forward refs/heads/main`) and a deletion (`denying ref deletion`). A **non-forced** fetch refspec `refs/heads/*:refs/heads/*` into a mirror, after the upstream `main` was rewritten, answered `! [rejected] main -> main (non-fast-forward)`, **exit 1, and the mirror kept its `main`**. A `pre-receive` hook that exits 1 refused a push to the mirror, and a fetch into the same mirror was unaffected, because hooks do not run on fetch. *(T1: M14.)*
13. **F9 REPRODUCES ON THE REAL MODEL, AND ITS SOURCE IS THE PROMPT'S OWN INSTRUCTION.** Measured by sending `summary.ts`'s `SYSTEM_PROMPT`, verbatim, ten times through LiteLLM (`default-chat-onprem` → `ministral-3:latest` on Ollama 0.34.4). The input was a leg-A-shaped diff (`sn` removed, one egress host added), its two security notes, `NullReviewer`'s reason and `COVERAGE_LIMIT`. **4 of 10 contained Markdown**: backticked field names twice, and a `**Verdict:**` line twice. **Those two `**Verdict:**` lines are invented verdicts**: *"This release is blocked by §7 and §20—it changes a sensitive field and widens egress without code review"* and *"This release changes sensitive fields and egress, so it requires an administrator's approval."* **1 of 10 called the coverage sentence *"the code reviewer's verdict"*.** Every one of the three appeared **after** `State the code reviewer's verdict exactly as given`. **And a regex for "a person decided" caught 0 of those 3**, so a prose check alone would not have caught F9. *(T1: M15 — re-run it, and run Task 13's prompt beside it.)* **→ Task 1 re-ran it (6 in 20 invented or misattributed) and found Task 13's prose check withholding 14 in 20, none a verdict (F7); Rich chose structured output the same day, and Task 13 is rewritten for it (Decisions 19 and 22).**
14. **`SourceDriver` HAS FOUR CALLERS THAT ASSUME A DIRECTORY, AND `RepoRef.url` HAS NONE.** `api/routes/builds.ts:107` hands `repositoryFor(slug).path` to the builder. `releases/approval.ts:571` hands it to the code reviewer (`NullReviewer` reads nothing, and a real reviewer would read a checkout). `api/routes/project-reads.ts:362` reads through `headCommit`/`readFile`. `api/routes/projects.ts:195` creates the repository. `grep -rn '\.url\b'` over `src/` finds **no reader of `RepoRef.url`** — the field the interface says *"a builder is handed"*. The builder is not handed it (roadmap finding 1). *(T1: M16.)*
15. **THE DEMOS WRITE STRAIGHT INTO THE BARE REPOSITORIES.** `scripts/demo.sh:85`, `scripts/demo-releases.sh:48,105-107,189` and `scripts/lib/proof-app.sh:76-86` `git clone`/`push` `.manifest/repos/<slug>.git`, and `scripts/lib/api.sh:60` deletes an orphan one. That is outside the contract, as signing in is (D23.8). **On driver 2 that path is the MIRROR**, and a push into it would put a commit in the mirror that GitHub never saw. So the mirror refuses every push (item 12's hook), and `make demo-github` pushes to the fake instead.
16. **EVERY `SourceError` IS `409`, AND ITS MESSAGE IS THE WIRE'S.** `api/errors.ts:639-650` maps the whole family to `409 { code, message }`. The registry (`api/error-codes.ts:330-334`) holds four `SOURCE_*` codes, and its comment says *"every one is 409"*. **A GitHub that cannot be reached is not a state conflict**, so this plan gives `SOURCE_UNREACHABLE` its own `503`. The registry scan finds codes as `new SourceError('LITERAL'` anywhere under `src/` (`error-codes.test.ts:66-71`), so every new code must be thrown as a literal.
17. **THE CONTRACT IS `1.1.0` WITH 43 OPERATIONS** (`api/contract/document.ts:31`; `jq` over `packages/contract/openapi.json`). **An additive change bumps the minor**, which the document's own comment requires (P6b Decision 15). The unversioned list (`api/unversioned.ts`) is written into the document too (P5a Task 6), so **the webhook route changes the document**, as do new error codes on an operation, new event types and new representation fields. *(T1: M17 — predict which task first moves `openapi.json`: Task 8.)*
18. **THE MASTER KEY'S CUSTODY IS A FILE AND A CONVENTION, WITH NO CHECK.** `infra/lib/ensure-master-key.sh` mints `infra/secrets/master.key` with `umask 077` and `chmod 600` in a `chmod 700` directory, called by `make up`. `make reset` keeps it. `loadMasterKeypair` (`secrets/store.ts`) reads it and **checks no permission**. §20 puts the App key *"in the same custody class"*, so this plan makes that one rule with two callers (Decision 5), rather than a new rule for the App key and none for the master key.
19. **`@fastify`'S CSRF AND IDEMPOTENCY HOOKS WOULD REFUSE EVERY GITHUB DELIVERY.** `api/server.ts`'s `preHandler` runs `assertSameOrigin` on every mutating route, and demands an `Idempotency-Key` of at least 8 characters, unless the route opts out through `config: { csrf: 'exempt', idempotency: 'exempt' }`. GitHub sends neither an `Origin` nor that header. The registry token realm opts out of idempotency for the identical reason (`api/routes/registry-token.ts`).
20. **P6b's `make demo-releases` IS THE ONLY `ci-acceptance` DEMO THAT WRITES GIT DIRECTLY**, and it reuses `launch-app`, a driver-1 project. **A GitHub-mode control plane would therefore refuse it** under Decision 3, which is why the acceptance runs every other demo on driver 1 and `make demo-github` on driver 2 (Decision 17).

---

## Decisions this plan makes, and why

**Twenty-two questions below Rich's line** — twenty-one made while the plan was written, and Decision 22 added on 2026-09-24 after sitting 1, when Rich chose structured output. Each one records what it rejected and what changing course would cost.

**Decision 1. The GitHub driver keeps a LOCAL MIRROR at driver 1's path, and `build/` does not change.** The mirror is a bare repository at `<reposRoot>/<slug>.git`, which is exactly where driver 1 keeps the repository itself. `localGitDir` makes sure the commit is present, and fetches it if it is not. It returns that directory, and the builder's `git --git-dir=<dir> archive` runs as it always has. *Rejected:* **an "export the tree at a commit" method downloading a tarball**, which is the cleaner abstraction and puts the network on the build path (roadmap finding 1). **A shallow mirror**, because a release's commit must stay buildable after GitHub moves on (§13). *Changing course* costs the build path's one caller, since the interface is already there.

**Decision 2. `RepoRef` loses `path` and `url`, and the build path asks for `localGitDir`.** `RepoRef` becomes `{ projectSlug, provider }`, and `SourceDriver` gains `localGitDir(repo, commitSha): Promise<LocalGitDir>`. **Removing `path` is what makes the seam real: the two readers become `tsc` errors rather than something to remember**, which is roadmap finding 2 closed by the compiler. `url` has no reader (*Read this first* 14). *Rejected:* **keeping `path` for driver 1's convenience**, because a public field that is meaningful for one implementation is the D5 leak, restated. *Changing course* is two fields.

**Decision 3. One driver per control-plane process; each project records its provider; a mismatch is refused.** `MANIFEST_SOURCE_DRIVER` is `local` (default) or `github`. `source_repositories` (migration, Task 8) records each project's provider, full name and web URL, and backfills `local` for the projects that exist. Every source operation on a project whose provider is not the running driver's answers **`409 SOURCE_PROVIDER_MISMATCH`** naming both. **A GitHub-mode control plane must never treat a driver-1 bare repository as a mirror**: it would push a laptop repository's history to GitHub, or fetch over it. *Rejected:* **a per-project choice of driver in the API**, which puts an infrastructure decision in a faculty client's hands and appears nowhere in the spec. **Dispatching per project across both drivers in one process**, which is a migration tool nobody has asked for. *The cost, accepted:* switching a laptop between drivers means restarting the control plane, and a project keeps the provider it was created with.

**Decision 4. An installation token is minted per repository and per purpose, lives only in memory, and never reaches an argument, a URL, a message or a row.** Fetching uses `contents: read` for that one repository. Pushing uses `contents: write` for that one repository. Changing visibility or protection uses `administration: write` for that one repository. Tokens are cached in memory, keyed by (repository, permissions), until five minutes before `expires_at`. They are handed to git only through `GIT_CONFIG_*` environment variables (*Read this first* 3). Every `SourceError` message the GitHub driver builds passes through a redactor that holds every live token. `credential.helper` is emptied and `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` is set, so macOS's keychain helper never stores or asks for one. ***The one exception is creating a repository***: a token cannot name a repository that does not exist yet. So `createRepository` mints an installation-wide token holding `administration: write` and nothing else, uses it for that one call and never caches it — which is Spec action 3. *Rejected:* **one installation-wide token for everything**, which is a token for every repository in the organisation. **Caching tokens in Postgres**, which puts a live credential in a backup. *Changing course* is the cache key.

**Decision 5. The App key's custody is the master key's, and ONE owner-only rule enforces both.** The App's private key is a PEM file in `infra/secrets/` — `github-fake-app.pem` for the fake, minted by `make up`, and `github-app.pem` for the real App, placed by Rich. It is mode `600` in a `700` directory, and it is never in `.env`, Postgres, git, an event or a log. `make reset` keeps it. **`assertOwnerOnly(path, code)` refuses a key file that its group or others can read or write, or that another user owns.** It is called by `loadAppKey` and by `loadMasterKeypair` both, so *"the same custody class"* is one rule with two callers. §20 says the key comes from Vault/KMS on UBC infrastructure. That is Phase 5's, and the loader is the one place it changes. *Rejected:* **a check for the App key only**, which makes the two keys different classes while the spec says they are one. **The key in `.env`**, which is readable by every process `make` starts. *Changing course* is one call site.

**Decision 6. The fake is our own, a zero-dependency workspace package, run from source in a container.** It lives in `packages/github-fake` (package name `@manifest/github-fake`), and its `Dockerfile` sits in the package, so the build context is only that directory. It uses Node built-ins alone (`http`, `crypto`, `child_process`, `fs`), plus the resolve hook from *Read this first* 10. The compose service `github-fake` is behind the **`github` profile**, so a developer on driver 1 never runs it. It publishes `127.0.0.1:7110` (free: `lsof` and a grep of the repository both found nothing on it), and keeps its state in the volume `manifest-github-fake-data`, which `make reset` destroys. **No hostname and no reserved label**: nothing in a container calls the fake (the builder uses the mirror, and the fake calls OUT for webhooks), so it needs no name under the zone. §7e's worry about `github.manifest.internal` does not arise. *Rejected:* **Gitea or Forgejo** (*Read this first* 1). **A host process like `manifest-mock`**, which Rich's decision rules out: *"a fake in a container"*. **`git http-backend` as CGI**, which Alpine's package does not ship (*Read this first* 2). **A build step into `dist/`**, which is an ordering problem in `make seed` for nothing the resolve hook does not already solve. *Changing course* is a Dockerfile.

**Decision 7. The fake is held honest by three things its author did not write.** The first is **GitHub's own schemas**: `packages/github-fake/conformance/github-schemas.json` is extracted from the description by a committed script in Task 1, and every response and webhook payload the fake produces is validated against it in the unit tier. The second is **GitHub's published HMAC test vector** (*Read this first* 6). The third is **`conformance/golden.json`**: the normalised answers of a fixed request script. It is written first from GitHub's documentation (`"source": "documentation"`), and replaced by the real App's answers on the first real run (`"source": "github.com <date>, App <id>"`). The fake's unit test compares the fake against it on every `pnpm test`. **A disagreement goes to the fake, never to the golden**, unless the real run shows the golden wrong. *Rejected:* **a fake checked only by the driver's own tests**, which is P3's false-green shape and the reason Rich rejected *the fake alone*. *Changing course* is one file.

**Decision 8. Webhooks reach the control plane directly at `http://host.docker.internal:7100/webhooks/github`, never through the edge, and the Caddyfile does not change.** The route is **unversioned**: it is not a resource, and GitHub, not a Manifest client, is its caller. It is listed in `api/unversioned.ts` with that reason, and `versioning.test.ts` forces the listing. It is **exempt from CSRF and from `Idempotency-Key`** (*Read this first* 19). It is registered in its own plugin with a **raw-buffer parser and a 5 MiB body limit** (*Read this first* 7). **It is registered in both modes**: in local mode it answers `404 WEBHOOKS_NOT_CONFIGURED` to everything, so `versioning.test.ts` is one list, and the acceptance can ask which mode it is talking to. **The security argument:** the route is reachable exactly by what can already reach the whole API on `127.0.0.1:7100` — host processes, and platform containers through `host.docker.internal`. App containers cannot reach it, because they reach neither the host (S6) nor, through the edge, anything but `/v1/*` and `/auth/*` — and those only from the gateway. So it adds no new reach, and HMAC is what authenticates a delivery. **5 MiB, not GitHub's 25 MB cap**, because the receiver reads only `repository.full_name`, `ref`, `before` and `after`. A `413` costs a webhook's latency and nothing else: the next pull-side sync scans the same commits (Decision 11). *Rejected:* **a route through the edge**, which the console site refuses and does not forward (*Read this first* 5), and which would need `@outside` weakened. **A second site such as `hooks.manifest.internal`**, which every app container could then reach through Caddy. *What stays open for UBC:* GitHub delivers to a PUBLIC URL, and production's ingress for it is Phase 5's topology question, named in *What this plan does not build*.

**Decision 9. The receiver verifies before it does anything else, and says which check failed.** The order is: body size (Fastify) → exactly one `X-Hub-Signature-256` → its shape → HMAC-SHA256 over the raw bytes, compared in constant time and only between equal lengths → **then** parse → **then** record the delivery id → **then** act. **Four refusals, four codes**, all `401`: `WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MISSING` (absent — including a delivery carrying only SHA-1's `X-Hub-Signature`), `WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MALFORMED` (not `sha256=` and 64 lowercase hex characters, or the header sent twice), `WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_INVALID` (well-formed and wrong), and `WEBHOOK_PAYLOAD_INVALID` (a correctly signed body that is not the JSON it claims to be — a `400`). **Distinct codes are the point**: with one shared code, deleting the absent-signature branch would leave every test green, which is exactly what node-saml's missing-signature acceptance did on 2026-09-24. *Rejected:* **one `401` code for all four**, for that reason. **Refusing without a body**, which D23.7 forbids.

**Decision 10. A delivery is recorded once, and processed off the request by one per-process queue.** Once verified, the delivery's `X-GitHub-Delivery` id is inserted into `webhook_deliveries` (migration, Task 9) with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. A repeat answers `200 { duplicate: true }` and changes nothing. A new one answers `202` and is queued on `deps.sourceSync`, **one job at a time across the process**, with `idle()` for tests — the retirer's and the build runner's shape. **GitHub times out a delivery after 10 seconds**, and a sync is a fetch, a scan and a validation. *Rejected:* **processing inline**, which turns a slow fetch into a failed delivery that GitHub redelivers. **A queue per repository**, which is concurrency nobody needs on one laptop. *Unmeasured, and recorded:* whether GitHub re-uses a delivery id when it redelivers. If it does not, a redelivery re-runs a sync, which is idempotent. **A push is synced, scanned and validated — never built.** Builds stay a request a person or an agent makes through the API, as the roadmap's D5 section requires (*builds stay pull-based*), so a push to GitHub can never start work that costs resources or lands a release on its own.

**Decision 11. Every advance of the mirror goes through ONE sync, and ONE observer reports what it found.** `sync(repo)` fetches `refs/heads/*:refs/heads/*` non-forced into the mirror (Decision 1). A ref the upstream rewrote is refused and reported (*Read this first* 12). The sync then reads the repository's visibility (Decision 12) and scans every new commit (Decision 14), and hands a `MirrorAdvance` to the **required** `SourceObserver`. That observer is built at boot with the database and the bus, and it publishes the Events. **Required, so boot cannot forget to wire it**: `createGithubSourceDriver` has no default. `headCommit`, `readFile` and `localGitDir` sync when they need a commit the mirror lacks, so a push that no webhook announced is scanned just the same. `refs/manifest/scanned/<branch>` moves only after the observer resolves, so a finding is reported at least once and never skipped. *Rejected:* **scanning only on a webhook**, which makes the scan depend on a delivery that a laptop's real App never gets. **Publishing events from `source/`**, which would give a git driver the database.

**Decision 12. Enforced private means created private, verified private, made private again, and not built while public.** `createRepository` sends `private: true` and **refuses the answer unless it reads `private: true` and `visibility: 'private'`**. Otherwise it deletes the repository and answers `409 SOURCE_REPOSITORY_NOT_PRIVATE`. Every sync reads `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}`. A repository found public is `PATCH`ed back to `private: true`, and the observer publishes `repository.visibility_enforced`. A `repository` webhook whose action is `publicized` triggers the same immediately. The last visibility read is kept in the mirror's own config (`manifest.visibility`). **`localGitDir` refuses a build while it reads `public`**, with `409 SOURCE_REPOSITORY_PUBLIC`, which holds offline too. *Rejected:* **detection alone**, which is not *"enforced"*. **Refusing to revert**, which leaves the leak open. *What cannot be enforced from here, and is recorded:* between a person flipping visibility on GitHub and Manifest's revert, the repository is public — seconds with a webhook, and until the next sync without one. **§20 already calls that leak immediate and irreversible.** The preventive control is the organisation's own policy (members may not make repositories public), and that is UBC's, on the external track.

**Decision 13. `main` is protected against rewriting and deletion, on both drivers, and nothing more.** Driver 1 sets `receive.denyNonFastForwards` and `receive.denyDeletes` on every bare repository, at creation and at boot (*Read this first* 12). Driver 2 `PUT`s branch protection on `main` at creation, with `allow_force_pushes: false` and `allow_deletions: false`, **no required reviews and no push restrictions**: faculty keep pushing `main`. **Where GitHub refuses it** — a private repository on a free organisation (*Read this first* 8) — the repository is recorded `mainProtected: false` with GitHub's own message, and the observer publishes `repository.protection_unavailable`, **never silently**. **And the mirror's non-forced fetch protects the history a release names on EITHER plan**, which is the property §13 actually needs. *Rejected:* **push restrictions so that only the App may push `main`**, which changes every faculty member's workflow and is not in the spec. D14's branch-scoped credential is a SANDBOX's, and sandboxes are S5's. **Required reviews**, which is a policy choice for UBC.

**Decision 14. Push-time secret scanning covers every path Manifest can see, with ONE list of rules.** `build/secret-patterns.ts` holds the patterns, **plus a new one for the stateless installation token** (*Read this first* 4). `build/gates.ts` imports it. The patterns are applied three ways. **(a) The control plane's own commits** — a seed, and the authoring API's commits in the next plan — are scanned before they leave, on both drivers, and refused with `409 SOURCE_SECRET_DETECTED`. **(b) A push to one of driver 1's bare repositories** is refused by a `pre-receive` hook. The driver renders that hook from the same list, installs it at creation and re-renders it at boot. **(c) A push made directly to GitHub** cannot be blocked from here, because GitHub.com runs no custom pre-receive hooks. Its commits are scanned when the mirror advances, and `repository.secret_detected` names the commit, the path, the line and the rule. **It never names the text.** And the build gate still refuses a tree that carries the secret. **The hook's loop is the one thing written twice** — plain JavaScript in the rendered file, TypeScript in `scanText`. **One test runs a shared corpus through both and demands identical findings.** *Rejected:* **the hook calling back into the control plane** over an internal route, which fails every push closed while the control plane is down, and needs the pusher's quarantine directory passed across a process boundary. **Scanning the tree at the new head only**, which misses a secret committed and deleted in the same push, and that secret is on GitHub for ever. **§20's bullet now says exactly this** (Spec action 1, option (a), approved and applied 2026-09-24).

**Decision 15. The repository link is `Project.repository`, a required field, and the contract becomes `1.2.0` once.** The link is `{ provider: 'local' | 'github', fullName, webUrl, mainProtected, protectionDetail }`. `webUrl` is `null` for driver 1: a laptop path is not an address, and publishing it would tell a client the host's filesystem layout. **The first task whose change reaches `openapi.json` bumps `CONTRACT_VERSION`, `openapi.json` and `packages/contract/package.json` to `1.2.0`, and covers every later additive change in this plan** — P6b's Decision 15, applied again, because nothing is published between tasks. Task 1 predicted that task is Task 8; **it measured Task 2** (`[M17]`, F8 — every route's `errors:` list is printed into the document, and Task 2 adds one). *Rejected:* **a bump per task**, because nothing is published between tasks. **`webUrl` as the bare repository's path**, for the reason above.

**Decision 16. An orphan repository is never adopted.** When `POST /orgs/{org}/repos` answers `422` because the name exists — `pnpm test` and `make reset` empty the tables and leave the fake's repositories behind, and on GitHub it can be anyone's — `createRepository` answers **`409 SOURCE_REPOSITORY_EXISTS`**, and the project is deleted as P4b's Decision 29 already does. **Adopting it could hand a new project another person's code and history.** Demos and tests clear it through the fake's own `/_fake/reset`, which GitHub does not have. On real GitHub an administrator removes it by hand. *Rejected:* **adopting it when empty**, because *empty* is a race.

**Decision 17. The acceptance is `make demo-github`, on its own project, against a control plane started on driver 2, and it asks the control plane which driver it runs.** Step 0 sends an unsigned `POST /webhooks/github`. `401 WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MISSING` means driver 2, and it is also the first negative control. `404 WEBHOOKS_NOT_CONFIGURED` means driver 1, and the demo stops with the restart command, **creating nothing**. That matters because no route deletes a project (P6a F5), so a `github-app` created on driver 1 would block the demo for ever. **Every other acceptance stays on driver 1** (*Read this first* 20), and `ci-acceptance`'s new step reports **`NOT RUN — the control plane runs driver 1`**, which is not a pass. *Rejected:* **a second control plane on another port**, which clobbers the platform's SP row exactly as the Docker tier does (and must restart it afterwards). **Making every demo driver-agnostic**, which is a rewrite of three demos to prove that a git host is a git host. *The breadth this trades, stated* (ORIENTATION §9, *the demo is the deliverable*): driver 2's acceptance deploys to STAGING, not production. The release and launch path is measured source-independent (the roadmap: `releases/`, `launch/` and `spec/` import nothing from `source/`), so a production launch on driver 2 would re-prove P6a over a different git host.

**Decision 18. Offline on driver 2 (C1): what the mirror has keeps working, and what it lacks answers `503`, never a stale answer.** Building a commit that is in the mirror needs no network. `headCommit`, a read of a commit that is not mirrored, and `createRepository` answer **`503 SOURCE_UNREACHABLE`**, naming the provider. **A stale `HEAD` presented as current would validate and build the wrong commit.** *Rejected:* **the mirror's head as a fallback**, for that reason.

**Decision 19. F9: the model no longer states the verdict — it fills a schema that has no place for one, and an answer that breaks the schema or decides something is withheld.** *(Rewritten 2026-09-24, after sitting 1's F7 and Rich's choice of structured output; the prose-and-regex design this decision first made is in the *Rejected* list below, with the measurement that retired it.)* The measurement (*Read this first* 13) puts every invented verdict after the instruction to state one, over a free-text answer. The verdict and the coverage limit are the snapshot's own fields (P6b Task 8), and the console already shows them beside the summary. **So the model is no longer given them.** It is handed the diff's facts as JSON — each change's `path`, `from`, `to` and `describeDiff`'s own `summary`, and the security notes — and it answers `{ changes: [{ path, exposure }] }`, **`path` an enum of this diff's own paths and the array exactly as long as the diff**, through LiteLLM's `response_format: json_schema` (Decision 22). An answer that does not parse, does not match, names a path twice or leaves one out, or has a sentence using decision vocabulary (`verdict`, `approv…`, `reject…`, `decid…`, `decision`, `recommend…` — **not** `blocked`, which F7 measured describing egress) is recorded as `summary: null`, `summarySource: 'withheld'` and `summaryWithheldBecause: <the rule>`, **so an administrator sees the facts instead of an invented verdict**. Backticks and asterisks are stripped from a sentence that passed, which is safe only because the decision words are refused first. **R4(d) — Rich's — is unchanged**: *the approval summary surfaces the reviewer's verdict*, and the SNAPSHOT surfaces it, in its own field. It was P6b's Decision 13 that had the MODEL state it, and this plan reverses that one decision on a measurement. *Rejected:* **the prose prompt plus `checkSummary` over the whole text** — this decision's first form: Task 1 measured it withholding 14 of 20 answers, none of which decided anything (F7). **Stripping Markdown from prose**, which turns `**Verdict:** This release is blocked` into `Verdict: This release is blocked`. **A prompt change alone**, which failed 3 in 10 under a prompt that already forbade it. **A retry**, which doubles a preview's worst-case latency. **Asking the model for the whole summary as one JSON string field**, which is prose inside JSON and keeps every failure.

**Decision 20. D14's sandbox credential is not built, and the property that makes it buildable is.** §20's fifth bullet is a SANDBOX's credential, and sandboxes wait on S5. **What this plan makes true is that an installation token never leaves the control plane**: not into an app's environment, a build context, an event, a log line, an argument or a wire message. Tests assert this with a canary token (Tasks 7 and 9). A branch-scoped credential for a sandbox can then only be issued by the control plane and forwarded under its own token, which is the only way it could be scoped at all, since GitHub cannot scope an installation token to a branch.

**Decision 21. The conformance run creates, pushes and deletes, and never makes anything public on real GitHub.** Its script (Task 6) runs fourteen steps against a target. **The two that need a publicised repository — the `publicized` webhook and the revert — run against the fake only**, because flipping a real repository public is an outward action this plan will not take even for an empty repository. Webhook delivery cannot be conformance-checked at all on a laptop, which has no public URL. GitHub's test vector checks the signing instead. *Rejected:* **a real publicise-and-revert**, for that reason.

**Decision 22. A model's answer that the platform reads or shows is STRUCTURED OUTPUT: a JSON schema in the request, validated on the way back.** *(Added 2026-09-24, when Rich chose structured output for Task 13.)* The request carries `response_format: { type: 'json_schema', json_schema: { name, strict: true, schema } }`, the schema derived from a zod definition with `z.toJSONSchema`, so **one definition is both what the model is asked for and what its answer is checked against**. LiteLLM 1.98.0 turns it into Ollama's `format: <schema>` (read in its adapter and measured, sitting 1's `[M15]` addendum), and into each hosted provider's own form. **The request gives the model FACTS, not instructions about decisions**, and the schema has no field for anything the model must not say. **A provider that cannot honour the schema fails safe**: `drop_params: true` drops the parameter, the model answers prose, the parse fails and the answer is withheld — loudly, with an operator line, never shown unconstrained. **A platform call uses a non-reasoning name** (`default-chat`, `default-chat-onprem`: Ollama's `think: false` pinned, the chat-model decision above), because reasoning tokens spend `max_tokens` before any JSON appears — never a `-reasoning` name. **In this plan the rule has exactly one caller, Task 13's summary** — it is the control plane's only model call (`grep -rn chat/completions src/` finds only `releases/summary.ts`, 2026-09-24). **The authoring API and a real code reviewer inherit it.** *Rejected:* **JSON mode alone** (`format: "json"` / `response_format: json_object`), which guarantees JSON but not its shape — without a schema the model invented its own keys 3 times in 3 (`structured-control.mjs`). **A per-deployment "small-model mode" switch**, which ties a per-MODEL property to the whole deployment; if a model ever needs a more explicit prompt than another, that is a per-model attribute in `infra/litellm/config.yaml`'s `model_info`, beside `max_classification` (D17), and it is not built here because every mapping today is the same model. *Changing course* is one request field.

## Global Constraints

Every task's requirements implicitly include this section. Values are copied verbatim from the spec, or from a dated measurement.

- **Four gates, all clean before every commit**, from the **repository root**: `pnpm test`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm format:check`. **Run `pnpm test` twice**, because a suite that is not repeatable has a state leak. Never run it with `--filter`. **The baseline is `pnpm test` 1742 passed in 123 files, `pnpm test:docker` 200 in 31, `make doctor` 19/0, `make verify` 55/0, and 24 migrations** (ORIENTATION §2, the F10 sitting, 2026-09-24) — **this plan's starting line, kept as written; the CURRENT baseline is always ORIENTATION §2's box.**
- **`pnpm test:docker`** (~20 min, needs `make up`, **fails rather than skips**) is owed by any change to `runtime/`, `routing/`, `services/`, `build/`, `releases/`, `identity/`, `sso/`, `secrets/`, `projects/`, `blueprints/`, `spec/`, `ai/`, `observability/`, `launch/`, `infra/`, or any `*.docker.test.ts` — **and, in this plan, `source/`**, because from Task 2 the build path runs through it. It restarts the edge, dropping every runtime route, truncates the tables and re-registers the platform's SP row. **Restart the control plane afterwards.** It regenerates seven dead app networks and one volume every time it runs. **S6's probe 14 needs the chat model resident** (P6b sitting 3, F17) — **`qwen3.5:4b` since 2026-09-24**, 3.4 GB on disk and about 4.3 GB loaded: warm it with `curl -s http://127.0.0.1:11434/api/generate -d '{"model":"qwen3.5:4b","prompt":"ok","stream":false,"think":false,"keep_alive":"30m","options":{"num_predict":1}}'`. A `000` there is that precondition before it is a regression.
- **`pnpm test -- <filter>` does not filter.** Run one file this way instead, from the repository root:
  - a unit file: `pnpm exec vitest run --project unit src/<path>`;
  - a packages file (the fake's too, from Task 4): `pnpm exec vitest run --project packages packages/<pkg>/src/<path>`;
  - a Docker file: `MANIFEST_TEST_DOCKER=1 pnpm exec vitest run --project docker src/<path>`.

  **Never run two Vitest processes at once**: they share a global setup that truncates the tables in both.
- **`pnpm test` TRUNCATES the control plane's tables** — so does one file, and so does `pnpm contract:write`. **Run anything that needs a demo's rows BEFORE any Vitest run.** A new table that references `projects` is emptied by the `CASCADE`, and is named in `db/testing.ts`'s `TABLES` anyway, as belt and braces (the file says why).
- **Vitest strips types; it does not check them.** `pnpm typecheck` is the only gate that sees a whole class of error. `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` is on, so fix an optional field with a conditional spread. **`packages/github-fake` also has `erasableSyntaxOnly`**: no `enum`, no `namespace`, **no constructor parameter properties** (`constructor(readonly code: string)` is exactly what `SourceError` does and the fake cannot).
- **A route change is three files, in order**: the definition, then `pnpm contract:write`, then `pnpm contract:generate`. **`packages/contract/openapi.json` is generated; never edit it.** The webhook route is NOT a contract route — it is a plugin like the registry token realm — **but it IS in the document**, through `api/unversioned.ts`.
- **A migration is `pnpm --filter @manifest/control-plane db:generate`, then READ what it wrote.** Drizzle writes the `audit.events` `CHECK` rewrite itself whenever `db/schema.ts`'s literal changes, and **appending that constraint by hand makes the migration fail to apply** (P6a `[M10]`). A data backfill is appended by hand, and the task says so. **Migration numbers in this plan are predictions** (0024 onwards); take what drizzle writes.
- **An EVENT TYPE is four edits and published surface**: `EVENT_TYPES` (`observability/events.ts`), its payload schema (`observability/event-schemas.ts`), the `CHECK` literal in `db/schema.ts` (drizzle then writes the migration), and the OpenAPI document's event schemas (the drift test reddens with no route added — P6a sitting 4). **A payload never carries a token, a secret's text, a laptop path or a GitHub error body verbatim.**
- **EVERY REFUSAL ASSERTS ITS CODE, NEVER ITS STATUS ALONE.** In this plan, `401` means `UNAUTHENTICATED`, `WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MISSING`, `WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MALFORMED` and `WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_INVALID`. `409` gains `SOURCE_PROVIDER_MISMATCH`, `SOURCE_REPOSITORY_EXISTS`, `SOURCE_REPOSITORY_NOT_PRIVATE`, `SOURCE_REPOSITORY_PUBLIC`, `SOURCE_COMMIT_NOT_FOUND`, `SOURCE_SECRET_DETECTED`, `SOURCE_CONFLICT` and `SOURCE_GITHUB_REFUSED` beside P6b's. **`503` is `SOURCE_UNREACHABLE`.** Use `refusal()` from `api/testing.ts`.
- **A REFUSAL TEST NEEDS A POSITIVE CONTROL IN THE SAME FILE**, and so does a negative claim. *"Refuses an unsigned delivery"* is true of a receiver that refuses everything, and that is exactly the shape of the SLO defect found on 2026-09-24: every test fired garbage, asserted a refusal, and passed against a route that refused everything.
- **Never accept a check you have not watched fail.** Every task ends by breaking what it built, **after committing the task**, and naming the test that goes red with its assertion quoted. `git checkout <path>` restores from the index, so an uncommitted task is destroyed by the restore rather than by the experiment. **Predict what turns red before you run it.** A wrong prediction is itself a finding (P5b sitting 9, F1). **A control that stays green is a question to chase, not a result** (P6a F6).
- **Every task names its CALLER.** A module with no call site is not built, and this project has shipped one five times.
- **NO TOKEN IN AN ARGUMENT, A URL, A MESSAGE, A ROW, AN EVENT OR A LOG LINE** (*Read this first* 3; Decision 4). A token goes to git only through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`/`GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n`/`GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_n`, with `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, an empty `credential.helper` and `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` (measured 2026-09-24: Apple git's `osxkeychain` helper comes from `/Applications/Xcode.app/Contents/Developer/usr/share/git-core/gitconfig`, and `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` removes it). **Every test that holds a token uses a CANARY** and asserts its absence from what it can see.
- **Nothing in this plan installs an external package.** `pnpm install --offline` links the new workspace package (Task 1's `[M18]`). If a task believes it needs a package, record a finding and raise it.
- **Never make a real GitHub repository public, and never install `Manifest (local dev)` anywhere but its own organisation** (Decision 21; *What Rich does* 2). **Every call to real GitHub is at Rich's yes, with the network on.**
- **Ask before `sudo`.** No task in this plan needs it. **Never edit the spec.** This plan's three spec actions were approved and applied on 2026-09-24; **anything further — including reverting Spec action 3 if Task 6's real leg contradicts it — goes to Rich first.** **Never touch Laravel Valet.** These four containers must survive: `docker-simple-saml-saml-idp-1`, `qdrant-local-dev`, `mongodb` and `mongo-express`. **`caddy-data` must never be destroyed.**
- **macOS ships bash 3.2 and a BSD userland**: no associative arrays, no `mapfile`, no `xargs -r`, no `readlink -f`, and `sed -i ''`. **And the tool shell is zsh, which does not word-split an unquoted `$VAR`**: `G="git -c …"; $G commit` failed with *command not found* while this plan was written. Run multi-line measurement scripts with `bash <file>`.
- **`request.log` writes nothing** under `Fastify({ logger: false })`. Use `console.error`, and never with a secret, a token, a cookie or a signature in the message. **A swallowed `.catch(() => undefined)` is this codebase's most productive defect.** **`.map(fn)` passes the ARRAY INDEX as its second argument.**
- **Tests that run `git` set their own identity and ignore the machine's config**: `-c user.name=… -c user.email=… -c commit.gpgsign=false`, as `local-driver.ts`'s `GIT_IDENTITY` does, and `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` wherever a credential is involved.
- **COMMIT ON `main`, AND STAGE YOUR OWN PATHS BY NAME**, meaning files, never directories. **Where a commit step says `<each path in Files, by name>`, it means exactly that**: every file the task's **Files** list names, plus the generated migration and the `drizzle/meta/` files `git status` shows for it, plus `pnpm-lock.yaml` when a task links a workspace package. **Never `git add -A`, `git add .`, `git commit -a` or `git checkout .`.** Other sessions commit on `main` while you work, so run `git status` before each commit and account for every path. End every commit message with the attribution lines the session's own system reminder gives.
- **`$SCRATCH` IS YOUR SESSION'S SCRATCHPAD AND YOU MUST SET IT.** An unset `$SCRATCH` turns `> "$SCRATCH/x"` into `> /x`, silently.
  ```bash
  export SCRATCH=<your session's scratchpad directory>
  [ -d "$SCRATCH" ] || { echo 'SCRATCH is not a directory'; exit 1; }
  ```
- **Leave the machine as you found it.** Run `./scripts/snapshot-machine.sh` at the start of a sitting and at the end, and `diff` the two. **At every sitting's close, run `bash scripts/dead-app-resources.sh` and `bash scripts/litellm-orphans.sh` bare, then TRY each with `--apply` yourself**; hand the output to Rich only if you are refused. **From Task 5 onwards the fake's container and volume are platform state**: a sitting that started it and did not find it running stops it (`make github-down`) and says so.

---

## Review Focus

**The five failure modes the spec implies and no task's happy path exercises, most likely first.** Each has its test added to the task that owns the code.

1. **A webhook with no `X-Hub-Signature-256` at all** — including one that carries only the legacy SHA-1 `X-Hub-Signature`. The expected behaviour is **`401 WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MISSING`, no row in `webhook_deliveries`, and no sync queued**, beside a correctly signed delivery that IS accepted, in the same file. Owned by Task 9's *refuses an absent signature and records nothing*.
2. **A git failure while a live token is in hand** — the fake stopped mid-operation, a repository the token cannot see. The expected behaviour is **a `SourceError` whose message, whose wire answer and whose operator line contain no token**, measured with a canary. Owned by Task 7's *never puts a token where a person can read it*.
3. **GitHub unreachable** (the fake stopped). The expected behaviour: **building a commit already in the mirror still succeeds**; reading `HEAD` answers **`503 SOURCE_UNREACHABLE`**, never the mirror's stale head. Owned by Task 7's *offline* cases and Task 8's Docker test.
4. **A repository left behind** by `pnpm test`, `make reset` or a failed creation, and a project created with the same name. The expected behaviour is **`409 SOURCE_REPOSITORY_EXISTS`, the new project row deleted, the old repository untouched** — never adopted. Owned by Task 7's *never adopts an orphan*.
5. **An upstream `main` rewritten** — possible on a free organisation, where `main` cannot be protected (*Read this first* 8). The expected behaviour: **the mirror refuses the rewrite and keeps the commit an approved release names buildable**, and `repository.history_rewritten` names the branch and both commits. Owned by Task 9's *a rewritten main is refused by the mirror and reported*.

## The fixtures and helpers every snippet below uses

**Test users** (`identity/testing.ts`'s `TEST_USERS`): `bio_prof` (a member who owns the fixture project), `unrelated_user` and `platform_admin`. **Demo users** (the Manifest IdP): `instructor` / `instructor` (`ins000001`) and `operator` / `operator` (`opr000001`).

**In the control plane:**

- `resetDatabase()` (`db/testing.ts`); `testDeps()`, `loginAs(deps, puid, { steppedUp? })`, `mutationHeaders(deps)`, `withProjectServer(fn)`, `projectFor(puid, slug)`, `builtProject(slug)`, `commitManifest(ctx, lines, message)` and **`refusal(res)`**, which returns `{ status, code }` together (`api/testing.ts`). `commitManifest` commits through `deps.source.commitFiles`, which is why it works unchanged on both drivers.
- **Added by Task 7 to `api/testing.ts`: `githubTestDeps(fake)`** — `testDeps()` with `source` replaced by a GitHub driver pointed at an in-process fake, and a recording `SourceObserver`. Task 9 adds `sourceSync`. **A test that replaces `source` must replace `builds` too, or its builds run on the other driver's paths** — the same rule `testDeps` states for `driver`.

**In the fake (`@manifest/github-fake/testing`, Task 4):**

```ts
export interface StartedFake {
  url: string                 // http://127.0.0.1:<port>
  apiUrl: string              // `${url}/api/v3`   — GitHub Enterprise Server's layout
  gitUrl: string              // `${url}`          — clone URLs are `${gitUrl}/<org>/<repo>.git`
  org: string                 // 'manifest-apps'
  appId: string               // '1000001'
  installationId: string      // '2000001'
  appKeyPem: string           // the App's PRIVATE key, generated per fake — what the control plane holds
  webhookSecret: string       // what both sides hold (Task 9)
  developerToken: string      // a classic `ghp_` PAT for `faculty-dev`, an org member with admin — a PERSON
  plan: 'free' | 'team'       // Decision 13: `free` refuses protection on a private repository, as GitHub does
  deliveries(): Delivery[]    // (Task 9) every webhook it sent, signed, with the answer it got
  setWebhookUrl(url: string): void
  stop(): Promise<void>
}
export function startFake(options?: {
  dataDir?: string; plan?: 'free' | 'team'; webhookUrl?: string
  /** TEST-ONLY misbehaviour, never set by main.ts: added by Task 7 (createPublic) and Task 10 (refusePrivatize). */
  quirks?: { createPublic?: boolean; refusePrivatize?: boolean }
}): Promise<StartedFake>
```

**In the demos and scripts:** `idp_login` (`infra/lib/idp-login.sh`); `scripts/lib/api.sh` (`api`, `field`, `clear_orphan_repository`, `wait_for_build`); `scripts/lib/check.sh`. **`scripts/lib/api.sh` cannot send a bodyless POST** (ORIENTATION §4), so the webhook probes in Task 15 are sent from TypeScript. **A driver-2 repository is pushed to at `http://127.0.0.1:7110/manifest-apps/<slug>.git`** with `faculty-dev`'s token from `infra/secrets/github-fake-developer.token`, passed through `GIT_CONFIG_*` exactly as the driver passes its own.

**THE TWO TRAPS THAT WILL COST A SITTING IF NOT DISARMED:**

1. **A GitHub-mode control plane refuses every driver-1 project** (Decision 3), and `launch-app` is one. Restarting on driver 2 therefore puts `make demo-releases` and `make demo-production` red by design. **Restart on driver 1 before running either**, and never treat that red as a regression.
2. **The fake's repositories outlive `pnpm test` and `make reset` unless the volume goes too**, and the next create of the same slug is then `409 SOURCE_REPOSITORY_EXISTS` (Decision 16). Task 5 puts the volume in `make reset`. Tests use a fresh in-process fake. Demos call `/_fake/reset`.

## Task 1: Measure what this plan rests on — before any of it is built

**ALONE, AND FIRST.** This task writes no feature code. Its output is a findings directory. Wherever a measurement contradicts this plan, its output is also an **`[M<n>]` correction block at the top of the task it contradicts**, and, **if a boundary moves, a message to Rich before sitting 2** (his instruction: never re-cut the sittings silently).

**Nineteen measurements, `[M1]` to `[M19]`.** Fifteen of them — `[M3]` to `[M17]`, each marked *(T1: M<n>)* in *Read this first* — re-run or re-read what this plan measured or read while it was written, **on the committed tree, with the output kept this time**, because the writing session's scratchpad dies with it. Four are new: `[M1]` and `[M2]` (the state and the gates), `[M18]` (an offline workspace link) and `[M19]` (what only the real App can answer). Two halves are new inside old ones: `[M13]`'s `down`, and `[M17]`'s prediction of which task first moves the contract. Two need the network (`[M3]`, `[M10]`). **Ask Rich once, at the start, for both**, and if he says no, record them as *read 2026-09-24, see the plan* and move the extraction to sitting 3's network window (Task 4, Step 1). `[M15]` needs Ollama and LiteLLM up and `ministral-3` warm.

**Files:**
- Create: `docs/superpowers/spikes/d5-baseline/README.md` — one section per measurement: what was asked, the command, the RAW answer, what it means for this plan, and (where it moves something) the task and the correction
- Create: `docs/superpowers/spikes/d5-baseline/results-task1-<date>.txt` — every command and its untrimmed output
- Create: `docs/superpowers/spikes/d5-baseline/extract-schemas.mjs` and `github-schemas.json` (`[M10]`; Task 4 `git mv`s both into `packages/github-fake/conformance/`)
- Create: `docs/superpowers/spikes/d5-baseline/probes/` — the throwaway scripts below, kept as the record of how each answer was got
- Modify: this plan — `[M<n>]` correction blocks; the sittings table only if Rich re-cuts it
- Create, then DELETE in the same step: `packages/m18-probe/` (a throwaway workspace package) and the lockfile change it makes

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing.
- Produces: the four gate numbers as they stand, a yes or no on each measurement, GitHub's schemas for the fake, and the prediction of which task first moves `openapi.json`.

- [ ] **Step 1: `[M1]` — the state this plan starts from, queried BEFORE anything truncates it**

```bash
cd /Users/rich/Developer/manifest
export SCRATCH=<your session's scratchpad directory>
[ -d "$SCRATCH" ] || { echo 'SCRATCH is not a directory'; exit 1; }
mkdir -p docs/superpowers/spikes/d5-baseline/probes
```

- [ ] **Step 4: `[M4]` — git over HTTP with core git alone**

Write `probes/smart-http.mjs` — the 40-line server this plan's writer measured, reproduced here so the record is on disk:

```js
// probes/smart-http.mjs — `node smart-http.mjs <bare-repo> <port>`. Basic auth required, any user.
import { createServer } from 'node:http'
import { spawn } from 'node:child_process'
import { createGunzip } from 'node:zlib'
const [REPO, PORT] = process.argv.slice(2)
const pkt = (s) => (s.length + 4).toString(16).padStart(4, '0') + s
createServer((req, res) => {
  const url = new URL(req.url, 'http://x')
  if (!(req.headers.authorization ?? '').startsWith('Basic ')) {
    res.writeHead(401, { 'WWW-Authenticate': 'Basic realm="probe"' }); return res.end()
  }
  if (req.method === 'GET' && url.pathname.endsWith('/info/refs')) {
    const service = url.searchParams.get('service')
    if (service !== 'git-upload-pack' && service !== 'git-receive-pack') { res.writeHead(403); return res.end() }
    res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': `application/x-${service}-advertisement`, 'Cache-Control': 'no-cache' })
    res.write(pkt(`# service=${service}\n`) + '0000')
    spawn('git', [service.slice(4), '--stateless-rpc', '--advertise-refs', REPO]).stdout.pipe(res)
    return
  }
  const m = url.pathname.match(/\/(git-upload-pack|git-receive-pack)$/)
  if (req.method === 'POST' && m) {
    res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': `application/x-${m[1]}-result` })
    const p = spawn('git', [m[1].slice(4), '--stateless-rpc', REPO])
    ;(req.headers['content-encoding'] === 'gzip' ? req.pipe(createGunzip()) : req).pipe(p.stdin)
    p.stdout.pipe(res); return
  }
  res.writeHead(404); res.end()
}).listen(Number(PORT), '127.0.0.1')
```

Drive it with a bare repository, a small push, a 3 MB push and a clone, **under bash**, with the Basic header passed in the environment (`GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=1 GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0=http.extraHeader GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0="Authorization: Basic $(printf 'x-access-token:probe' | base64)"`). Then check Alpine's git in a throwaway container (the network is needed for `apk`; if it is off, record it as measured 2026-09-24 and leave it to Task 5):

```bash
docker run --rm alpine:3.22 sh -c 'apk add --no-cache git >/dev/null 2>&1; git --version; git init -q --bare /r.git;
  git upload-pack --stateless-rpc --advertise-refs /r.git | head -c 40; echo; ls /usr/libexec/git-core/git-http-backend 2>&1' | tee -a "$R"
```

**Predict:** push, big push and clone exit 0; Alpine's git `2.49.1` advertises; `git-http-backend` absent.

```bash
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs'

// ACROSS LINES: four of the seven entries put `pattern:` on the line after `name:`.

  const line = `const token = "${token}"`
  console.log(label, token.length, pats.filter((p) => p.re.test(line)).map((p) => p.name))
}
JS
```

- [ ] **Step 10: `[M10]` and `[M11]` — GitHub's own description (network)**

Write `extract-schemas.mjs` (below), download the description, and run it. **Commit both the script and its output**: the output is dated evidence of what GitHub said on the day, and the fake's schema test reads it.

```js
// docs/superpowers/spikes/d5-baseline/extract-schemas.mjs — node extract-schemas.mjs <api.github.com.json> <out.json>
// Pulls, out of GitHub's own REST description, the response schemas of the operations the fake
// serves and the payload schemas of the webhooks it sends, with every component they reference.
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs'
const d = JSON.parse(readFileSync(process.argv[2], 'utf8'))
const OPS = [
  ['/app', 'get', '200'], ['/orgs/{org}/installation', 'get', '200'],
  ['/app/installations/{installation_id}/access_tokens', 'post', '201'],
  ['/orgs/{org}/repos', 'post', '201'], ['/repos/{owner}/{repo}', 'get', '200'],
  ['/repos/{owner}/{repo}', 'patch', '200'],
  ['/repos/{owner}/{repo}/branches/{branch}/protection', 'put', '200'],
]
const HOOKS = ['push', 'repository-publicized', 'ping']
const need = new Set()
const walk = (n) => {
  if (!n || typeof n !== 'object') return
  if (typeof n.$ref === 'string') {
    const k = n.$ref.split('/').pop()
    if (!need.has(k)) { need.add(k); walk(d.components.schemas[k]) }
  }
  for (const v of Object.values(n)) walk(v)
}
const roots = {}
for (const [p, m, s] of OPS) {
  const schema = d.paths[p][m].responses[s].content['application/json'].schema
  roots[`${m.toUpperCase()} ${p} ${s}`] = schema; walk(schema)
}
for (const h of HOOKS) {
  const schema = d['x-webhooks'][h].post.requestBody.content['application/json'].schema
  roots[`webhook ${h}`] = schema; walk(schema)
}
const facts = {
  'POST /user/repos enabledForGitHubApps': d.paths['/user/repos'].post['x-github'].enabledForGitHubApps,
  'POST /orgs/{org}/repos enabledForGitHubApps': d.paths['/orgs/{org}/repos'].post['x-github'].enabledForGitHubApps,
  'branch protection availability': d.paths['/repos/{owner}/{repo}/branches/{branch}/protection'].put.description.split('\n')[0],
  'installation token description': d.paths['/app/installations/{installation_id}/access_tokens'].post.description,
}
const schemas = Object.fromEntries([...need].filter((k) => d.components.schemas[k]).map((k) => [k, d.components.schemas[k]]))
writeFileSync(process.argv[3], JSON.stringify({ source: `github/rest-api-description ${d.info.version}`, extracted: new Date().toISOString(), facts, roots, components: { schemas } }, null, 1))
console.log(d.info.version, 'schemas', Object.keys(schemas).length)
```

```bash
jq -r '.facts | to_entries[] | "\(.key): \(.value|tostring|.[0:300])"' docs/superpowers/spikes/d5-baseline/github-schemas.json | tee -a "$R"
for s in full-repository installation integration installation-token webhook-push webhook-repository-publicized; do
  echo "$s required=$(jq --arg s $s '.components.schemas[$s].required | length' docs/superpowers/spikes/d5-baseline/github-schemas.json)"
done | tee -a "$R"
```
