- [ ] **Step 11: `[M12]` — the resolve hook in `node:22-alpine`**

Reproduce *Read this first* 10 in `$SCRATCH/strip/`: `src/b.ts` exporting a typed function, `src/main.ts` importing it as `./b.js`, and the hook (it is Task 4's `resolve-ts.mjs`, verbatim):

```js
// resolve-ts.mjs — a relative `./x.js` that does not exist resolves to `./x.ts` beside it.
import { registerHooks } from 'node:module'
import { existsSync } from 'node:fs'
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url'
registerHooks({
  resolve(specifier, context, next) {
    if (specifier.startsWith('.') && specifier.endsWith('.js') && context.parentURL) {
      const asJs = new URL(specifier, context.parentURL)
      const asTs = new URL(specifier.slice(0, -3) + '.ts', context.parentURL)
      if (!existsSync(fileURLToPath(asJs)) && existsSync(fileURLToPath(asTs))) return next(asTs.href, context)
    }
    return next(specifier, context)
  },
})
```


```ts
// source/git-driver.ts
export type SourceProvider = 'local' | 'github'

/** A repository this platform owns. NO PATH AND NO URL: where it lives is the driver's business. */
export interface RepoRef {
  projectSlug: string
  provider: SourceProvider
}

/** A commit, present in a bare repository on THIS machine — what `git --git-dir=` is handed. */
export interface LocalGitDir {
  gitDir: string
  commitSha: string
}

export interface SourceDriver {
  readonly name: SourceProvider
  createRepository(projectSlug: string, seed: SeedFiles): Promise<RepoRef>
  repositoryFor(projectSlug: string): RepoRef
  commitFiles(repo: RepoRef, files: SeedFiles, message: string): Promise<string>
  headCommit(repo: RepoRef, ref?: string): Promise<string>
  readFile(repo: RepoRef, commitSha: string, path: string): Promise<string | null>
  listBranches(repo: RepoRef): Promise<string[]>
  /**
   * THE BUILD PATH (roadmap finding 2). The commit, present locally — fetched first if this
   * driver keeps a mirror and lacks it. Refuses `SOURCE_COMMIT_NOT_FOUND` for a commit the
   * repository does not have. The builder and the code reviewer call this and nothing else.
   */
  localGitDir(repo: RepoRef, commitSha: string): Promise<LocalGitDir>
  destroyRepository(repo: RepoRef): Promise<void>
}

// source/driver-contract.ts
export interface SourceDriverHarness {
  driver: SourceDriver
  /** Pushes a commit to `main` the way a PERSON would, outside the driver. Returns its sha. */
  pushAsPerson(slug: string, files: Record<string, string>, message: string): Promise<string>
  cleanup(): Promise<void>
}
export function describeSourceDriver(name: string, make: () => Promise<SourceDriverHarness>): void
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the contract suite, and run it against driver 1 — it cannot compile yet**

```ts
// source/driver-contract.ts
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process'
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import { SourceError, type SourceDriverHarness } from './index.js'

const SEED = {
  'manifest.yaml': 'manifest: 1\nname: chem-labs\nblueprint: fixture-node@1\nruntime:\n  port: 3000\n',
  'src/index.js': "console.log('hello')\n",
}

async function code(p: Promise<unknown>): Promise<string | undefined> {
  try { await p; return undefined } catch (e) { return e instanceof SourceError ? e.code : `not a SourceError: ${String(e)}` }
}

/**
 * THE D5 SEAM'S CONTRACT (this plan's Task 2). Every source driver runs it — driver 1 in
 * `local-driver.test.ts`, driver 2 in `github/driver.test.ts` against the in-process fake.
 * A case here is a promise the CONTROL PLANE relies on; a driver-specific promise goes in
 * that driver's own file.
 */
export function describeSourceDriver(name: string, make: () => Promise<SourceDriverHarness>): void {
  describe(`the ${name} source driver keeps D5's contract`, () => {
    let h: SourceDriverHarness
    beforeEach(async () => { h = await make() })
    afterEach(async () => { await h.cleanup() })

    it('creates a repository seeded with the files it was given, and names it by slug and provider', async () => {
      const repo = await h.driver.createRepository('chem-labs', SEED)
      expect(repo).toEqual({ projectSlug: 'chem-labs', provider: h.driver.name })
      const sha = await h.driver.headCommit(repo)
      expect(sha).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{40}$/)
      expect(await h.driver.readFile(repo, sha, 'manifest.yaml')).toContain('name: chem-labs')
      expect(await h.driver.readFile(repo, sha, 'not-there.yaml')).toBeNull()
    })

    it('reads a file AT a commit, not at HEAD', async () => {
      const repo = await h.driver.createRepository('chem-labs', SEED)
      const first = await h.driver.headCommit(repo)
      const second = await h.driver.commitFiles(repo, { 'manifest.yaml': 'manifest: 1\nname: renamed\n' }, 'edit')
      expect(second).not.toBe(first)
      expect(await h.driver.readFile(repo, first, 'manifest.yaml')).toContain('name: chem-labs')
      expect(await h.driver.readFile(repo, second, 'manifest.yaml')).toContain('name: renamed')
    })

    it('sees a commit a PERSON pushed outside the driver', async () => {
      const repo = await h.driver.createRepository('chem-labs', SEED)
      const pushed = await h.pushAsPerson('chem-labs', { 'README.md': 'by hand\n' }, 'a person pushed this')
      expect(await h.driver.headCommit(repo)).toBe(pushed)
      expect(await h.driver.readFile(repo, pushed, 'README.md')).toBe('by hand\n')
    })

    it('hands the builder a local bare repository that holds the commit', async () => {
      const repo = await h.driver.createRepository('chem-labs', SEED)
      const sha = await h.driver.headCommit(repo)
      const local = await h.driver.localGitDir(repo, sha)
      expect(local.commitSha).toBe(sha)
      // What the builder runs (build/context.ts): it must succeed against this directory.
      expect(execFileSync('git', ['--git-dir', local.gitDir, 'cat-file', '-t', sha]).toString().trim()).toBe('commit')
    })

    it('refuses a commit the repository does not have, with its code', async () => {
      const repo = await h.driver.createRepository('chem-labs', SEED)
      expect(await code(h.driver.localGitDir(repo, 'f'.repeat(40)))).toBe('SOURCE_COMMIT_NOT_FOUND')
    })

    it('lists the default branch', async () => {
      const repo = await h.driver.createRepository('chem-labs', SEED)
      expect(await h.driver.listBranches(repo)).toEqual(['main'])
    })

    it('refuses a slug that could escape, and a path that could', async () => {
      for (const slug of ['../escape', 'a/b', '..', '.', 'Chem', 'has_underscore', ''])
        expect(await code(h.driver.createRepository(slug, SEED))).toBe('SOURCE_INVALID_SLUG')
      const repo = await h.driver.createRepository('chem-labs', SEED)
      expect(await code(h.driver.commitFiles(repo, { '../outside.txt': 'x' }, 'escape'))).toBe('SOURCE_PATH_ESCAPE')
    })

    it('refuses a reference another provider made', async () => {
      const other = h.driver.name === 'local' ? 'github' : 'local'
      expect(await code(h.driver.headCommit({ projectSlug: 'chem-labs', provider: other }))).toBe('SOURCE_PROVIDER_MISMATCH')
    })

    it('destroys a repository, after which it has no head', async () => {
      const repo = await h.driver.createRepository('chem-labs', SEED)
      await h.driver.destroyRepository(repo)
      expect(await code(h.driver.headCommit(repo))).toBeDefined()
    })
  })
}
```

In `local-driver.test.ts`, run it beside the file's own cases. The harness's `pushAsPerson` clones the bare repository, commits with a fixed identity and pushes, which is exactly what `scripts/demo-releases.sh:105-107` does:

```ts
describeSourceDriver('local', async () => {
  const root = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'manifest-source-'))
  const driver = createLocalSourceDriver(root)
  return {
    driver,
    pushAsPerson: async (slug, files, message) => pushByHand(join(root, `${slug}.git`), files, message),
    cleanup: () => rm(root, { recursive: true, force: true }),
  }
})
```

`pushByHand(bare, files, message)` is a 15-line helper at the bottom of the same file: `git clone` into a temp directory, write the files, `git -c user.name=person -c user.email=person@example.org -c commit.gpgsign=false commit -qam`, `git push -q origin HEAD:main`, and return `git rev-parse HEAD`. **Its identity is not Manifest's**, so the commit reads as a person's.

- [ ] **Step 3: Change the interface and driver 1**

`git-driver.ts` becomes the **Interfaces** block above; `SourceError` is unchanged. In `local-driver.ts`:

```ts
export function createLocalSourceDriver(root: string): SourceDriver {
  const repoRoot = resolve(root)
  // pathFor(slug) is unchanged: the slug rule, then the startsWith(repoRoot + sep) check.

  /** A reference is this driver's when it names THIS provider and a slug this driver could have made. */
  function assertOwned(repo: RepoRef): string {
    if (repo.provider !== 'local') {
      throw new SourceError('SOURCE_PROVIDER_MISMATCH',
        `'${repo.projectSlug}' is a ${repo.provider} repository and this is the local driver`)
    }
    return pathFor(repo.projectSlug)
  }

  return {
    name: 'local',
    repositoryFor: (projectSlug) => { pathFor(projectSlug); return { projectSlug, provider: 'local' } },
    async createRepository(projectSlug, seed) {
      const path = pathFor(projectSlug)
      await mkdir(repoRoot, { recursive: true })
      await git(repoRoot, ['init', '--bare', '--initial-branch=main', path])
      await commitThroughWorktree(path, seed, 'chore: seed from blueprint skeleton', true)
      return { projectSlug, provider: 'local' }
    },
    // commitFiles, headCommit, readFile, listBranches, destroyRepository: unchanged bodies,
    // each starting `const path = assertOwned(repo)`.
    async localGitDir(repo, commitSha) {
      const path = assertOwned(repo)
      if (!/^[0-9a-f]{40}$/.test(commitSha)) {
        throw new SourceError('SOURCE_COMMIT_NOT_FOUND', `'${commitSha}' is not a commit id`)
      }
      try {
        await run('git', ['--git-dir', path, 'cat-file', '-e', `${commitSha}^{commit}`])
      } catch {
        throw new SourceError('SOURCE_COMMIT_NOT_FOUND', `${repo.projectSlug} has no commit ${commitSha.slice(0, 12)}`)
      }
      return { gitDir: path, commitSha }
    },
  }
}
```

**Why the id is checked before git sees it:** `cat-file -e` accepts any revision expression, including `main` and `HEAD~3`. A build names a COMMIT, and the builder archives what it was told. So a symbolic name would build whatever `main` points at when the build starts, rather than what was validated — the pairing P6b's *What this plan does not build* already names as loose.

## Task 3: The App key's custody — one owner-only rule for both keys, the App JWT, and the settings

§20: *"GitHub App private key held in the same custody class as the master key."* This task makes that sentence ONE rule with TWO callers (Decision 5). It writes the App JWT with `node:crypto` by GitHub's own rules. It gives the control plane its GitHub settings, defaulted to the fake, so switching drivers is one variable. And it mints the fake App's three credentials into `infra/secrets/` from `make up`, beside the master key.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/control-plane/src/secrets/custody.ts` — `assertOwnerOnly(path, code)`
- Create: `packages/control-plane/src/secrets/custody.test.ts`

- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/secrets/index.ts` — exports it

```ts
// secrets/custody.ts
/** Refuses a key file anyone but its owner can read or write, or that another user owns. */
export async function assertOwnerOnly(path: string, code: 'SECRET_MASTER_KEY_PERMISSIONS' | 'SECRET_GITHUB_APP_KEY_PERMISSIONS'): Promise<void>

// source/github/app-auth.ts
export async function loadAppKey(path: string): Promise<KeyObject>   // an RSA private key, or SourceError
export function appJwt(input: { appId: string; key: KeyObject; now?: Date }): string

// config.ts — Config gains:
sourceDriver: 'local' | 'github'
github: {
  apiUrl: string          // MANIFEST_GITHUB_API_URL,  default http://127.0.0.1:7110/api/v3 (the fake)
  gitUrl: string          // MANIFEST_GITHUB_GIT_URL,  default http://127.0.0.1:7110
  org: string             // MANIFEST_GITHUB_ORG,      default manifest-apps
  appId: string           // MANIFEST_GITHUB_APP_ID,   default 1000001
  installationId: string  // MANIFEST_GITHUB_INSTALLATION_ID, default 2000001
  appKeyPath: string      // MANIFEST_GITHUB_APP_KEY,  default infra/secrets/github-fake-app.pem (repo-root relative)
  webhookSecretPath: string // MANIFEST_GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET, default infra/secrets/github-fake-webhook.secret
}
```

```ts
// secrets/custody.test.ts
import { chmod, mkdtemp, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises'
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os'
import { join } from 'node:path'
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import { assertOwnerOnly } from './custody.js'

describe('the custody class the master key and the App key share (§20)', () => {
  let dir: string
  beforeEach(async () => { dir = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'custody-')) })
  afterEach(async () => { await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }) })

  const codeOf = async (p: Promise<void>) => p.then(() => 'accepted', (e: { code?: string }) => e.code)

  it('accepts a key only its owner can read — the positive control', async () => {
    const f = join(dir, 'k'); await writeFile(f, 'x'); await chmod(f, 0o600)
    expect(await codeOf(assertOwnerOnly(f, 'SECRET_GITHUB_APP_KEY_PERMISSIONS'))).toBe('accepted')
    await chmod(f, 0o400)
    expect(await codeOf(assertOwnerOnly(f, 'SECRET_GITHUB_APP_KEY_PERMISSIONS'))).toBe('accepted')
  })

  it.each([0o640, 0o604, 0o644, 0o660, 0o606])('refuses mode %o, naming the code it was given', async (mode) => {
    const f = join(dir, 'k'); await writeFile(f, 'x'); await chmod(f, mode)
    expect(await codeOf(assertOwnerOnly(f, 'SECRET_GITHUB_APP_KEY_PERMISSIONS'))).toBe('SECRET_GITHUB_APP_KEY_PERMISSIONS')
    expect(await codeOf(assertOwnerOnly(f, 'SECRET_MASTER_KEY_PERMISSIONS'))).toBe('SECRET_MASTER_KEY_PERMISSIONS')
  })
})
```

*Another owner* cannot be made without `sudo`, so it is asserted by reading the code, and the task's record says so — **not** by a test that pretends.

```ts
// source/github/app-auth.test.ts
import { createPublicKey, generateKeyPairSync, verify } from 'node:crypto'
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import { appJwt } from './app-auth.js'

describe("the App's JWT (GitHub: RS256, iss = App ID, exp within ten minutes)", () => {
  const { privateKey, publicKey } = generateKeyPairSync('rsa', { modulusLength: 2048 })
  const now = new Date('2026-09-24T12:00:00Z')

  it('is a compact RS256 JWS that the PUBLIC key verifies', () => {
    const [h, p, s] = appJwt({ appId: '1000001', key: privateKey, now }).split('.')
    expect(JSON.parse(Buffer.from(h!, 'base64url').toString())).toEqual({ alg: 'RS256', typ: 'JWT' })
    expect(verify('RSA-SHA256', Buffer.from(`${h}.${p}`), createPublicKey(publicKey), Buffer.from(s!, 'base64url'))).toBe(true)
  })

  it('backdates iat by 60 s for clock drift and expires in 9 minutes, under GitHub’s 10', () => {
    const [, p] = appJwt({ appId: '1000001', key: privateKey, now }).split('.')
    const claims = JSON.parse(Buffer.from(p!, 'base64url').toString())
    expect(claims).toEqual({ iss: '1000001', iat: now.getTime() / 1000 - 60, exp: now.getTime() / 1000 + 540 })
  })

  it('does not verify under a different key — the negative control', () => {
    const other = generateKeyPairSync('rsa', { modulusLength: 2048 }).publicKey
    const [h, p, s] = appJwt({ appId: '1000001', key: privateKey, now }).split('.')
    expect(verify('RSA-SHA256', Buffer.from(`${h}.${p}`), other, Buffer.from(s!, 'base64url'))).toBe(false)
  })
})
```

`config.test.ts` gains four cases: the driver defaults to `local`; `MANIFEST_SOURCE_DRIVER=github` alone yields the fake's seven defaults; **an `http:` API or git URL whose host is not `127.0.0.1` or `localhost` is refused `CONFIG_GITHUB_INSECURE_URL`**, beside `https://api.github.com`, which is accepted; and `MANIFEST_SOURCE_DRIVER=gitlab` is `CONFIG_INVALID`.

```ts
// secrets/custody.ts
import { stat } from 'node:fs/promises'
import { SecretError } from './envelope.js'

/**
 * §20: the App key is held "in the same custody class as the master key" — so this is the
 * ONE rule, and both loaders call it (this plan's Decision 5). A key its group or others can
 * read is a key any other account on the machine, and anything running as it, can copy.
 *
 * `SecretError` for both, carrying the code the caller names, so the boot line says WHICH
 * key — a refusal that names the wrong file sends the operator to the wrong file. Both are
 * boot-time refusals and never reach the wire: `SecretError` is not one of the classes
 * `toErrorResponse` answers as itself, so neither code is in `api/error-codes.ts`.
 */
export async function assertOwnerOnly(
  path: string,
  code: 'SECRET_MASTER_KEY_PERMISSIONS' | 'SECRET_GITHUB_APP_KEY_PERMISSIONS',
): Promise<void> {
  const s = await stat(path)
  const loose = s.mode & 0o077
  if (loose !== 0 || (process.getuid !== undefined && s.uid !== process.getuid())) {
    throw new SecretError(
      code,
      `'${path}' is mode ${(s.mode & 0o777).toString(8)}, owner ${s.uid}: a key must be readable by its owner alone ` +
        `(chmod 600 '${path}'), because anything that can read it can act as the platform (§20)`,
    )
  }
}
```

`SecretError` is `secrets/envelope.ts:66`, `(readonly code: string, message: string)` (read 2026-09-24). **Do not register the two `SECRET_*` codes**: the registry's scan does not see `SecretError`, and its reverse check (*registers nothing the source never throws*) would go red. `loadMasterKeypair` calls `await assertOwnerOnly(path, 'SECRET_MASTER_KEY_PERMISSIONS')` **after** its existing unreadable check, so a MISSING file still answers `SECRET_MASTER_KEY_UNREADABLE` with the `make up` hint rather than an `ENOENT` from `stat`.

```ts
// source/github/app-auth.ts
import { createPrivateKey, createSign, type KeyObject } from 'node:crypto'
import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises'
import { assertOwnerOnly } from '../../secrets/index.js'
import { SourceError } from '../git-driver.js'

export async function loadAppKey(path: string): Promise<KeyObject> {
  let pem: string
  try { pem = await readFile(path, 'utf8') } catch {
    throw new SourceError('SOURCE_GITHUB_KEY_UNREADABLE',
      `cannot read the GitHub App key at '${path}'. For the fake, \`make up\` mints it; for a real App, see the D5 plan's "What Rich does".`)
  }
  await assertOwnerOnly(path, 'SECRET_GITHUB_APP_KEY_PERMISSIONS')
  const key = createPrivateKey(pem)
  if (key.asymmetricKeyType !== 'rsa') {
    throw new SourceError('SOURCE_GITHUB_KEY_UNREADABLE', `'${path}' is a ${key.asymmetricKeyType} key; a GitHub App key is RSA`)
  }
  return key
}

const b64url = (v: unknown) => Buffer.from(typeof v === 'string' ? v : JSON.stringify(v)).toString('base64url')

/**
 * GitHub's App JWT: RS256, `iss` the App ID, `iat` 60 s in the past (GitHub's own advice for
 * clock drift), `exp` nine minutes out — GitHub refuses one more than ten. Minted per call and
 * never stored: it authenticates as the App, which can mint a token for every installation.
 */
export function appJwt({ appId, key, now = new Date() }: { appId: string; key: KeyObject; now?: Date }): string {
  const t = Math.floor(now.getTime() / 1000)
  const unsigned = `${b64url({ alg: 'RS256', typ: 'JWT' })}.${b64url({ iss: appId, iat: t - 60, exp: t + 540 })}`
  return `${unsigned}.${createSign('RSA-SHA256').update(unsigned).sign(key).toString('base64url')}`
}
```

**Module boundaries:** `source/github/app-auth.ts` imports `../../secrets/index.js`, which is a public entry, so `module-boundaries.test.ts` allows it. **Check first that `secrets/` does not already import `source/`**, which would make a cycle. `SOURCE_GITHUB_KEY_UNREADABLE` joins the registry.

`config.ts`: add the schema entries of the **Interfaces** block. Resolve `appKeyPath` and `webhookSecretPath` with the existing `fromRepoRoot`, exactly as `MANIFEST_SECRETS_MASTER_KEY` is. Then add one refinement, after the existing origin checks:

```ts
  // An installation token over plaintext is a token on the network (Decision 4). Loopback only.
  for (const [name, value] of [['MANIFEST_GITHUB_API_URL', raw.MANIFEST_GITHUB_API_URL], ['MANIFEST_GITHUB_GIT_URL', raw.MANIFEST_GITHUB_GIT_URL]] as const) {
    const u = new URL(value)
    if (u.protocol === 'http:' && u.hostname !== '127.0.0.1' && u.hostname !== 'localhost') {
      throw new ConfigError('CONFIG_GITHUB_INSECURE_URL',
        `${name} is '${value}': a GitHub installation token may cross only loopback in plaintext; use https`)
    }
  }
```

**`config.github` is built whatever the driver**, so Task 9's webhook route can read the secret path. It is **read** only when the driver is `github`. A driver-1 control plane must boot on a machine that has never minted the fake's files, and `config.test.ts` asserts that.

- [ ] **Step 4: The fake App's credentials, minted by `make up`**

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# infra/lib/ensure-github-fake.sh — the FAKE GitHub App's credentials (D5 plan, Task 3).
#
# Three files in infra/secrets/, the master key's custody (§20, Decision 5), minted once and
# never by `make reset` — the same rule as master.key:
#   github-fake-app.pem         the App's PRIVATE key. The control plane holds it; the fake
#                               NEVER sees it — GitHub holds only the public half.
#   github-fake-app.pub.pem     the public half, which the fake verifies App JWTs with.
#   github-fake-webhook.secret  the App's webhook secret: the fake signs with it, the control
#                               plane verifies with it (§20: "verified by HMAC before any processing").
#   github-fake-developer.token faculty-dev's classic PAT: a PERSON who pushes to GitHub
#                               directly, outside Manifest — what the acceptance's direct pushes use.
# Nothing here is a real credential. A REAL App's key is Rich's to place (the plan's
# "What Rich does"), at infra/secrets/github-app.pem, and this script never touches it.
set -euo pipefail
ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../.." && pwd)"
DIR="$ROOT/infra/secrets"
mkdir -p "$DIR" && chmod 700 "$DIR"
umask 077
if [ ! -f "$DIR/github-fake-app.pem" ]; then
  echo "  minting the fake GitHub App's key (RSA 2048)"
  openssl genpkey -algorithm RSA -pkeyopt rsa_keygen_bits:2048 -out "$DIR/github-fake-app.pem" 2>/dev/null
  chmod 600 "$DIR/github-fake-app.pem"
fi
# Re-derived every time: it is the private key's half, so it can never disagree with it.
openssl pkey -in "$DIR/github-fake-app.pem" -pubout -out "$DIR/github-fake-app.pub.pem" 2>/dev/null
chmod 600 "$DIR/github-fake-app.pub.pem"
[ -f "$DIR/github-fake-webhook.secret" ] || { openssl rand -hex 32 > "$DIR/github-fake-webhook.secret"; chmod 600 "$DIR/github-fake-webhook.secret"; }
if [ ! -f "$DIR/github-fake-developer.token" ]; then
  # A classic PAT's shape: ghp_ and 36 alphanumerics. `tr -dc` over /dev/urandom, BSD-safe.
  printf 'ghp_%s\n' "$(LC_ALL=C tr -dc 'A-Za-z0-9' </dev/urandom | head -c 36)" > "$DIR/github-fake-developer.token"
  chmod 600 "$DIR/github-fake-developer.token"
fi
```

**`chmod 600` on the public key and the token is not decoration.** Task 5 mounts both into the fake read-only, and a mounted file keeps its host mode. So the fake's own process must be able to read a `600` file owned by the developer's uid. **That is a Task 5 measurement, not an assumption**: the fake runs as a non-root user, and Docker Desktop's file sharing maps ownership.

In `Makefile`, `up` gains `@bash infra/lib/ensure-github-fake.sh` directly after `ensure-master-key.sh`.

## Task 4: The fake, in process — App JWTs, installation tokens, private org repositories and git over HTTP

> **`[M4]` AND `[M11]` — TWO CORRECTIONS (2026-09-24, F2 and F4).**
> **(1) Create every repository with `git init --bare -b main`** — or set `HEAD` to `refs/heads/main`
> explicitly. With `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` on the host, and in `alpine:3.22`'s git 2.49.1 by default, a bare
> repository's `HEAD` names `refs/heads/master`. A clone of it then **exits 0 with NOTHING checked out**
> (*"warning: remote HEAD refers to nonexistent ref"*) — Task 1's own `[M4]` probe did exactly that on its
> first run, and a test asserting only the clone's exit code would be green. Assert the clone's `main` and
> a file in it.
> **(2) `schemas.ts` must normalise GitHub's schemas at LOAD, or Ajv refuses to compile two of them.**
> Ajv 8.20.0 throws *`"nullable" cannot be used without "type"`* compiling `installation` (the answer to
> `GET /orgs/{org}/installation`, which THIS task validates) and `webhook-push` (Task 9's): GitHub writes
> `nullable: true` beside `anyOf`/`oneOf` with no `type`, at exactly two sites —
> `installation.properties.account` and `webhook-push.properties.repository.properties.pushed_at`. Rewrite
> each such node at load: drop `nullable`, append `{ "type": "null" }` to its `anyOf`/`oneOf`, and **throw
> if a node has neither** (so a new shape in a later description is a red test, not a silent pass).
> Keep `github-schemas.json` VERBATIM — it is dated evidence of what GitHub said. **And `ajv-formats` is
> not optional**: without it Ajv warned 838 times and enforced no `uri`, `date-time`, `int64`,
> `uri-template` or `email` at all. Measured with the normaliser plus `ajv-formats`: 10 of 10 roots compile,
> 0 format warnings, `null` accepted at both sites, and `"not a uri"` refused where a URI belongs
> (`spikes/d5-baseline/probes/normalise.mjs`). Add a `schemas.test.ts` case: the normaliser rewrites
> exactly two nodes of today's file.

**The thing Rich decided on, built so that its author cannot simply agree with himself** (Decision 7). Four behaviours: App JWTs by GitHub's rules; installation tokens scoped to repositories and permissions; organisation repositories that are private unless asked otherwise; git over HTTP with Basic auth, scope and permission. **Every JSON answer is validated against GitHub's own schemas in the same test file that exercises it.** Webhooks are Task 9's, visibility events Task 10's and protection Task 12's. Each later task grows this package and its tests for its own feature.

```ts
// app-auth.test.ts — GitHub's App-JWT rules, against the fake over real HTTP
import { generateKeyPairSync, createSign } from 'node:crypto'
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import { startFake, type StartedFake } from './testing.js'
import { expectGitHubShape } from './schemas.js'

const b64 = (v: unknown) => Buffer.from(JSON.stringify(v)).toString('base64url')
function jwt(key: string, claims: Record<string, unknown>): string {
  const u = `${b64({ alg: 'RS256', typ: 'JWT' })}.${b64(claims)}`
  return `${u}.${createSign('RSA-SHA256').update(u).sign(key).toString('base64url')}`
}

describe('the fake authenticates a GitHub App the way GitHub does', () => {
  let fake: StartedFake
  beforeEach(async () => { fake = await startFake() })
  afterEach(async () => { await fake.stop() })
  const now = () => Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000)
  const get = (path: string, auth?: string) =>
    fetch(`${fake.apiUrl}${path}`, { headers: { accept: 'application/vnd.github+json', ...(auth ? { authorization: auth } : {}) } })

  it('answers GET /app for a valid JWT, in GitHub’s shape — the positive control', async () => {
    const res = await get('/app', `Bearer ${jwt(fake.appKeyPem, { iss: fake.appId, iat: now() - 60, exp: now() + 540 })}`)
    expect(res.status).toBe(200)
    expectGitHubShape('GET /app 200', await res.json())
  })

  it.each([
    ['no Authorization header', undefined],
    ['a JWT signed by another key', `Bearer ${jwt(generateKeyPairSync('rsa', { modulusLength: 2048 }).privateKey.export({ type: 'pkcs8', format: 'pem' }).toString(), { iss: '1000001', iat: now() - 60, exp: now() + 540 })}`],
    ['an exp more than ten minutes out', 'FAR'],
    ['an expired JWT', 'EXPIRED'],
    ['another App’s iss', 'OTHER_ISS'],
  ])('refuses %s with 401 and a message', async (_label, auth) => {
    const t = now()
    const header = auth === 'FAR' ? `Bearer ${jwt(fake.appKeyPem, { iss: fake.appId, iat: t - 60, exp: t + 660 })}`
      : auth === 'EXPIRED' ? `Bearer ${jwt(fake.appKeyPem, { iss: fake.appId, iat: t - 700, exp: t - 60 })}`
      : auth === 'OTHER_ISS' ? `Bearer ${jwt(fake.appKeyPem, { iss: '999', iat: t - 60, exp: t + 540 })}`
      : auth
    const res = await get('/app', header)
    expect(res.status).toBe(401)
    expect((await res.json()).message).toMatch(/\S/)
  })

  it('mints an installation token scoped to named repositories and permissions', async () => {
    const res = await fetch(`${fake.apiUrl}/app/installations/${fake.installationId}/access_tokens`, {
      method: 'POST',
      headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${jwt(fake.appKeyPem, { iss: fake.appId, iat: now() - 60, exp: now() + 540 })}`, 'content-type': 'application/json' },
      body: JSON.stringify({ permissions: { administration: 'write' } }),
    })
    expect(res.status).toBe(201)
    const body = await res.json()
    expectGitHubShape('POST /app/installations/{installation_id}/access_tokens 201', body)
    // GitHub's stateless format since 2026-04-27 (Read this first 4): ghs_<APPID>_<JWT>, NOT 40 characters.
    expect(body.token).toMatch(new RegExp(`^ghs_${fake.appId}_eyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]+\\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]+\\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$`))
    expect(body.token.length).toBeGreaterThan(40)
    expect(Date.parse(body.expires_at) - Date.now()).toBeGreaterThan(59 * 60_000)
    expect(body.permissions).toEqual({ administration: 'write' })
  })
})
```

`repos.test.ts` holds five cases, each schema-checked where it returns JSON:
- an installation token holding `administration: write` creates `{ name, private: true }` → `201`, `private: true`, `visibility: 'private'`, `full_name: 'manifest-apps/<name>'`;
- creating the same name again → **`422`**, with a message naming the name;
- a token scoped to repository A reads B → **`404 {"message":"Not Found"}`**, never a `403`, because GitHub hides a private repository;
- `PATCH { private: true }` → `200`;
- `DELETE` → `204`, then `GET` → `404`.

`git-http.test.ts` holds six cases:
- a `contents: write` token scoped to the repository pushes a commit (git exits 0) and `ls-remote` shows it;
- the same token clones;
- a `contents: read` token pushing → **git fails**, its stderr naming *Permission*;
- a token scoped to another repository → **`Repository not found`**;
- no credential → git fails with `terminal prompts disabled` (the test sets `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`);
- `faculty-dev`'s PAT pushes.

**The tokens travel through `GIT_CONFIG_*` in these tests too**, because a test that puts a token in a URL teaches the next reader to.

Beside them, `boundary.test.ts` greps `packages/github-fake/src` for `from '.*control-plane` and expects **nothing**. This is Decision 7's first half, made mechanical.

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement — the security-relevant parts in full**

```ts
// app-auth.ts (the fake's) — GitHub's rules, as its documentation states them
import { createHmac, createVerify, timingSafeEqual, type KeyObject } from 'node:crypto'

const b64json = (s: string): unknown => JSON.parse(Buffer.from(s, 'base64url').toString('utf8'))

/** GitHub: RS256; `iss` the App ID; `exp` at most ten minutes out; not expired; `iat` not in the future. */
export function verifyAppJwt(header: string | undefined, publicKey: KeyObject, appId: string, now: Date): { ok: true } | { ok: false; message: string } {
  const m = /^Bearer ([A-Za-z0-9_-]+)\.([A-Za-z0-9_-]+)\.([A-Za-z0-9_-]+)$/.exec(header ?? '')
  if (m === null) return { ok: false, message: 'A JSON web token could not be decoded' }
  const [, h, p, s] = m as unknown as [string, string, string, string]
  const alg = (b64json(h) as { alg?: unknown }).alg
  if (alg !== 'RS256') return { ok: false, message: 'A JSON web token could not be decoded' }
  const ok = createVerify('RSA-SHA256').update(`${h}.${p}`).verify(publicKey, Buffer.from(s, 'base64url'))
  if (!ok) return { ok: false, message: 'A JSON web token could not be decoded' }
  const c = b64json(p) as { iss?: unknown; iat?: unknown; exp?: unknown }
  const t = Math.floor(now.getTime() / 1000)
  if (String(c.iss) !== appId) return { ok: false, message: 'Integration not found' }
  if (typeof c.exp !== 'number' || c.exp <= t) return { ok: false, message: "'Expiration time' claim ('exp') must be a numeric value representing the future time at which the assertion expires" }
  if (c.exp > t + 600) return { ok: false, message: "'Expiration time' claim ('exp') is too far in the future" }
  if (typeof c.iat !== 'number' || c.iat > t + 60) return { ok: false, message: "'Issued at' claim ('iat') must be an Integer representing the time that the assertion was issued" }
  return { ok: true }
}
```

**The messages are GitHub's as its documentation and community answers report them, NOT measured.** `golden.json` carries them with `"source": "documentation"`, and Task 6's real leg replaces them with what GitHub actually sends. When the two differ, **the fake changes to match GitHub**.

```ts
/**
 * GitHub's stateless installation token (Read this first 4): `ghs_<APPID>_<JWT>`. The fake
 * signs its own claims with an HMAC key only it holds, so a token is verified WITHOUT a
 * table — which is the property that makes GitHub's format stateless — and a forged or
 * altered one fails the MAC. The claims name the installation, the repositories and the
 * permissions, and expire after one hour, as GitHub's do.
 */
export function mintInstallationToken(i: { appId: string; installationId: string; tokenKey: Buffer; repositories: 'all' | string[]; permissions: Grant['permissions']; now: Date }): { token: string; expiresAt: string } {
  const exp = Math.floor(i.now.getTime() / 1000) + 3600
  const h = Buffer.from(JSON.stringify({ alg: 'HS256', typ: 'JWT' })).toString('base64url')
  const p = Buffer.from(JSON.stringify({ inst: i.installationId, repos: i.repositories, perms: i.permissions, exp })).toString('base64url')
  const s = createHmac('sha256', i.tokenKey).update(`${h}.${p}`).digest('base64url')
  return { token: `ghs_${i.appId}_${h}.${p}.${s}`, expiresAt: new Date(exp * 1000).toISOString().replace('.000', '') }
}

/** Who a request is: an installation (its token), `faculty-dev` (the PAT), or nobody. */
export function grantFor(authorization: string | undefined, ctx: { tokenKey: Buffer; developerToken: string; now: Date }): Grant | undefined {
  const credential = credentialOf(authorization)          // `token X`, `Bearer X`, or Basic's password
  if (credential === undefined) return undefined
  if (equalStrings(credential, ctx.developerToken)) {
    return { kind: 'person', login: 'faculty-dev', repositories: 'all', permissions: { administration: 'write', contents: 'write', metadata: 'read' } }
  }
  const m = /^ghs_\d+_([A-Za-z0-9_-]+)\.([A-Za-z0-9_-]+)\.([A-Za-z0-9_-]+)$/.exec(credential)
  if (m === null) return undefined
  const [, h, p, s] = m as unknown as [string, string, string, string]
  const want = createHmac('sha256', ctx.tokenKey).update(`${h}.${p}`).digest()
  const given = Buffer.from(s, 'base64url')
  if (given.length !== want.length || !timingSafeEqual(given, want)) return undefined
  const c = JSON.parse(Buffer.from(p, 'base64url').toString()) as { repos: Grant['repositories']; perms: Grant['permissions']; exp: number }
  if (c.exp * 1000 <= ctx.now.getTime()) return undefined
  return { kind: 'installation', login: 'manifest-local-dev[bot]', repositories: c.repos, permissions: { metadata: 'read', ...c.perms } }
}
```

`credentialOf` accepts `token X`, `Bearer X` and `Basic base64(user:X)`, and answers `undefined` for anything else. `equalStrings(a, b)` is the codebase's constant-time comparison: equal lengths first, then `timingSafeEqual` (*Read this first* 6).

**The authorisation rule, one function in `server.ts`, used by every route:**

```ts
/** GitHub's answer to a private repository you may not see is 404, never 403 — it hides existence. */
function canSee(g: Grant | undefined, repo: string): boolean {
  return g !== undefined && (g.repositories === 'all' || g.repositories.includes(repo))
}
function can(g: Grant | undefined, repo: string, perm: 'administration' | 'contents', level: Permission): boolean {
  if (!canSee(g, repo)) return false
  const held = g!.permissions[perm]
  return held === 'write' || (level === 'read' && held === 'read')
}
```

`POST /orgs/{org}/repos` needs `administration: write` **and `repositories: 'all'`**, because a token scoped to named repositories cannot name the one being created. That is the fake's conservative guess at `[M19](a)`. The real leg measures it, and golden decides.

**Git over HTTP** (`git-http.ts`) is Task 1 Step 4's server with four additions. It resolves `/<org>/<repo>.git` against `state.repos`, answering `404` with GitHub's text for anything it cannot see. It needs `contents: read` for `upload-pack` and `contents: write` for `receive-pack`. On a `401` it sends `WWW-Authenticate: Basic realm="GitHub"`. And it spawns git with `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` and the repository's directory under `<dataDir>/<org>/<repo>.git`. **It records nothing about a credential except its login.**

`repos.ts`'s `fullRepository(repo, base)` builds the repository object from explicit values: `id`, `node_id`, `name`, `full_name`, `private`, `visibility`, `owner` (a `simple-user` for the organisation), `html_url`, `clone_url` (`${gitUrl}/${org}/${name}.git`), `default_branch: 'main'`, the timestamps, `permissions` and the booleans. It computes every `*_url` template from GitHub's pattern (`${apiUrl}/repos/${full}/<suffix>{/placeholder}`). **The schema test is the authority on which 75 keys exist**: run it, read Ajv's `must have required property` list, and add exactly those. Do not guess further.

`testing.ts`'s `startFake()` generates the App keypair and the token key (`generateKeyPairSync('rsa', { modulusLength: 2048 })`, `randomBytes(32)`) and a `ghp_` developer token. It creates a temporary `dataDir` unless given one, listens on `127.0.0.1:0`, and returns *The fixtures*' `StartedFake`. `main.ts` reads the same values from the environment (Task 5) and a `/data` volume.

```bash
  packages/github-fake/src/state.ts packages/github-fake/src/app-auth.ts packages/github-fake/src/repos.ts \
```

## Task 5: The fake's container — `--profile github`, `127.0.0.1:7110`, built by `make seed`

> **`[M13]` — MEASURED (2026-09-24, F5): A BARE `compose down` EXITS 0 AND LEAVES THE PROFILED SERVICE
> RUNNING.** With `always` and `opt` (profile `github`) up, a bare `docker compose down` stopped `always`,
> reported the shared network *"Resource is still in use"*, **and exited 0**; `opt` kept running and the
> network stayed. `docker compose --profile github down` then removed both. (docker compose 5.4.0.) So this
> task's conditional resolves **YES**, and it reaches further than `make down`: **both `down` AND `reset`'s
> own `$(COMPOSE) down` (`Makefile:70` today) become `$(COMPOSE) --profile github down`** — `reset`'s next
> line is `docker volume rm -f … 2>/dev/null || true`, which against a still-running fake fails exactly as
> silently. The exit code cannot be the check: assert that no `manifest-github-fake` container exists
> after `make down`. The probe is `spikes/d5-baseline/probes/profiles.sh`.

Rich decided the fake runs **in a container**. This task puts Task 4's server there: behind a profile, so driver 1's developer never runs it; on the platform network, so it can call `host.docker.internal:7100` for webhooks (*Read this first* 5); and published on loopback only. **It needs the network once, for `apk add git`** — ask Rich (*What Rich does* 1).

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/github-fake/Dockerfile`
- Modify: `infra/compose.yaml` — the `github-fake` service and the `github-fake-data` volume
- Modify: `infra/seed/seed.sh:14` — `$COMPOSE --profile github build`
- Modify: `Makefile` — `github-up`, `github-down`; `down` and `reset` per `[M13]`; the volume in `reset`
- Modify: `scripts/doctor.sh` — the image exists; **7110 is the fake's**, in the port map that learned 7104 was the console's (P5c sitting 3)
- Modify: `scripts/verify.sh` — two static checks: the fake's credentials are owner-only, and compose mounts no App PRIVATE key into the fake and publishes it on `127.0.0.1` only
- Create: `packages/control-plane/src/source/github/fake-container.docker.test.ts`
- Modify: `docs/superpowers/RUNBOOK.md` — *The GitHub fake*: start, stop, reset, what it is not
- Modify: `docs/superpowers/ORIENTATION.md` §4 *Numbers* — 7110

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 3's four `infra/secrets/github-fake-*` files; Task 4's `main.ts` environment:
  `FAKE_PORT` (7110), `FAKE_DATA_DIR` (`/data`), `FAKE_ORG`, `FAKE_APP_ID`, `FAKE_INSTALLATION_ID`, `FAKE_PLAN`, `FAKE_API_URL` and `FAKE_GIT_URL` (**the HOST's view**, `http://127.0.0.1:7110/…`, because the host is who reads `clone_url`), `FAKE_WEBHOOK_URL` (Task 9), and `FAKE_APP_PUBLIC_KEY_FILE`, `FAKE_WEBHOOK_SECRET_FILE`, `FAKE_DEVELOPER_TOKEN_FILE`.
- Produces: `manifest-github-fake:local`; `make github-up` / `make github-down`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Measure the one thing Task 3 left open — can a non-root process in the container read a `600` bind-mounted file?**

```bash
bash infra/lib/ensure-github-fake.sh
docker run --rm --user node -v "$PWD/infra/secrets/github-fake-webhook.secret:/run/fake/webhook.secret:ro" node:22-alpine \
  sh -c 'id; ls -ln /run/fake/webhook.secret; wc -c < /run/fake/webhook.secret' 2>&1
```

**Predict: readable** — Docker Desktop's file sharing presents host files to the VM without POSIX uid enforcement. **If it is NOT readable**, the fake keeps `USER node` and its entrypoint cannot read its credentials. Then choose between (i) running the fake as root (it is a test double on loopback, and the reason must be written in the Dockerfile) and (ii) `chmod 644` on the PUBLIC key alone, which is not a secret, plus the secret and the token. **Never loosen the App's private key, which is not mounted at all.** Record the measurement and the choice.

- [ ] **Step 2: The Dockerfile**

```dockerfile
# packages/github-fake/Dockerfile — the GitHub FAKE (D5 plan, Task 5). NOT GitHub.
FROM node:22-alpine
# git at IMAGE BUILD, which is `make seed`'s step 2 — the one step with the network (C1).
# There is no apk mirror (ORIENTATION §8), so this line cannot run offline, and a fake with
# no git cannot be repaired offline either. upload-pack/receive-pack --stateless-rpc are
# core git; Alpine's package has no git-http-backend and the fake needs none (Task 1, [M4]).
RUN apk add --no-cache git
WORKDIR /app
COPY package.json resolve-ts.mjs ./
COPY src ./src
RUN mkdir -p /data && chown node:node /data
USER node
ENV FAKE_PORT=7110 FAKE_DATA_DIR=/data
EXPOSE 7110
# busybox wget is in the image (NOT curl — LiteLLM's healthcheck trap, compose.yaml).
HEALTHCHECK --interval=5s --timeout=3s --retries=12 CMD wget -qO- http://127.0.0.1:7110/_fake/health || exit 1
CMD ["node", "--import", "./resolve-ts.mjs", "src/main.ts"]
```

- [ ] **Step 3: The compose service**

```yaml
  # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  # D5's driver 2, locally: a GitHub-compatible FAKE (the D5 plan, Decision 6).
  # BEHIND A PROFILE — `make up` never starts it, so a developer on driver 1 runs
  # nine containers, as §21 says. `make github-up` starts it; `make seed` builds it
  # (`--profile github build`, because a plain `compose build` skips a profiled
  # service — measured 2026-09-24). NO HOSTNAME: nothing in a container calls it.
  # The App's PRIVATE key is NOT mounted — GitHub holds only the public half, and
  # `make verify` asserts this list.
  # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  github-fake:
    build: { context: ../packages/github-fake }
    image: manifest-github-fake:local
    container_name: manifest-github-fake
    profiles: [github]
    restart: unless-stopped
    networks: [platform]
    extra_hosts:
      # Its webhooks go where the edge's own traffic goes: the control plane on the
      # host, 127.0.0.1:7100 (the plan's Read this first 5). Never through the edge.
      - "host.docker.internal:host-gateway"
    environment:
      FAKE_ORG: manifest-apps
      FAKE_APP_ID: "1000001"
      FAKE_INSTALLATION_ID: "2000001"
      FAKE_PLAN: team
      FAKE_API_URL: http://127.0.0.1:7110/api/v3
      FAKE_GIT_URL: http://127.0.0.1:7110
      FAKE_WEBHOOK_URL: http://host.docker.internal:7100/webhooks/github
      FAKE_APP_PUBLIC_KEY_FILE: /run/fake/app.pub.pem
      FAKE_WEBHOOK_SECRET_FILE: /run/fake/webhook.secret
      FAKE_DEVELOPER_TOKEN_FILE: /run/fake/developer.token
    volumes:
      - github-fake-data:/data
      - ./secrets/github-fake-app.pub.pem:/run/fake/app.pub.pem:ro
      - ./secrets/github-fake-webhook.secret:/run/fake/webhook.secret:ro
      - ./secrets/github-fake-developer.token:/run/fake/developer.token:ro
    ports:
      - "127.0.0.1:7110:7110"
```

…and under `volumes:`, `github-fake-data: { name: manifest-github-fake-data }`. **`make reset` removes that volume** (the fake's repositories are project state), and `make down` stops the service per `[M13]`. **If a bare `compose down` leaves a profiled container attached to `manifest-platform`, `make down` becomes `$(COMPOSE) --profile github down`**, and `make verify`'s *nothing of ours is left running after down* check (if it has one) is re-read. New targets:

```makefile
github-up: up  ## D5 driver 2's GitHub fake (profile github), on 127.0.0.1:7110.
	@$(COMPOSE) --profile github up -d --wait github-fake
	@echo "  GitHub fake up on http://127.0.0.1:7110 — start the control plane with MANIFEST_SOURCE_DRIVER=github to use it"

github-down:  ## Stop the GitHub fake. Its repositories survive until `make reset`.
	@$(COMPOSE) --profile github stop github-fake
```

```bash
docker image inspect manifest-github-fake:local --format '{{.Id}} {{.Created}}'
make github-up && curl -sS http://127.0.0.1:7110/_fake/health && curl -sS -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' http://127.0.0.1:7110/api/v3/app
docker exec manifest-github-fake sh -c 'id -un; git --version; node --version'
```

- [ ] **Step 5: `make doctor` and `make verify`**

`doctor.sh` gains ONE check, *the GitHub fake's image is built*, which answers with the `make seed` remedy when absent. It also marks 7110 as the fake's in its port-claim map, so a running fake is not reported as *CLAIMED BY SOMETHING ELSE*. **Predict doctor 19 → 20**, and read it twice: once with the fake stopped and once with it running.

`verify.sh` gains TWO static checks, which run whether or not the fake is up, so the count does not depend on the profile. (1) *the fake's four credential files are owner-only*. (2) *compose gives the fake no App private key and publishes it on 127.0.0.1 only*: read `infra/compose.yaml` and assert that the service's volumes name no `github-fake-app.pem` without `.pub`, and that its only port begins `127.0.0.1:`. **Predict verify 55 → 57.** Update `scripts/ci-acceptance.sh`'s `EXPECT_DOCTOR` and `EXPECT_VERIFY` in this commit.

- [ ] **Step 6: The Docker-tier test — the image, not the in-process copy**

`fake-container.docker.test.ts` starts a THROWAWAY container from `manifest-github-fake:local`, on `127.0.0.1::7110` (a random host port), with a generated keypair, secret and token in a temp directory. **It is not the profile's service**, so the test owns what it creates and removes it in `afterAll` (`docker rm -f -v`). It asserts four things:
- **the control plane's own `appJwt` (Task 3) is accepted by the IMAGE** — the first cross-check between the two sides that was not written in one file;
- a token minted that way pushes over git, and a clone gets the commit back;
- the process is not root (`docker exec … id -u` is not `0`);
- restarting the container keeps the repository in its volume.

```bash
  scripts/ci-acceptance.sh packages/control-plane/src/source/github/fake-container.docker.test.ts \
  docs/superpowers/RUNBOOK.md docs/superpowers/ORIENTATION.md
git commit -m "feat(infra): the GitHub fake as a container — profile github, 127.0.0.1:7110, built by make seed"
```

## Task 6: The conformance run — the fake against GitHub's recorded answers, and a real App at Rich's yes

**Rich's decision's second half:** *a short opt-in conformance run against a real GitHub App checks that the fake answers what GitHub answers.* One script, `conformance.ts`, runs fourteen steps against a **target** and records a normalised answer for each. Against the in-process fake it runs in `pnpm test` on every run, compared with `conformance/golden.json`. Against real GitHub it runs from `make github-conformance`, at Rich's yes, and writes what GitHub said beside the golden for a person to compare. **It is placed in this sitting because it is the fake's check; if sitting 3 has run long, it opens sitting 4.**

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/github-fake/src/conformance.ts` — `runConformance(target)` and `normalise`
- Create: `packages/github-fake/conformance/golden.json` — the expected answers, `"source": "documentation"`
- Create: `packages/github-fake/src/conformance.test.ts` — the in-process fake against golden
- Create: `scripts/github-conformance.sh`; modify `Makefile` — `github-conformance`
- Modify: `docs/superpowers/RUNBOOK.md` — *The conformance run*, with *What Rich does* 2's settings

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `startFake` (Task 4), and nothing from the control plane — **the script signs its JWT with its own twelve lines**, so a defect in Task 3's `appJwt` cannot be reproduced here and agree with itself.
- Produces:

```ts
export interface ConformanceTarget { apiUrl: string; gitUrl: string; org: string; appId: string; installationId: string; appKeyPem: string; label: string }
export interface StepAnswer { step: string; status: number; facts: Record<string, string | number | boolean | null> }
export function runConformance(t: ConformanceTarget): Promise<StepAnswer[]>
```

- [ ] **Step 1: The fourteen steps, and what each records**

| Step | Request | Facts recorded (never a token, never an id that changes per run) |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | `GET /app`, valid JWT | status; `slug` present; the `permissions` keys, sorted |
| C2 | `GET /app`, JWT with `exp` = now + 11 min | status; `message` |
| C3 | `GET /app`, JWT signed by a fresh key | status; `message` |
| C4 | `GET /orgs/{org}/installation` | status; `repository_selection` |
| C5 | installation token, `permissions: { administration: 'write' }`, no repositories | status; token class (`stateless` for `^ghs_\d+_eyJ…`, `classic` for `^ghs_` and 36 characters, else `other`); minutes to `expires_at`, rounded to 5; `permissions` |
| C6 | `POST /orgs/{org}/repos` `{ name: mf-conformance-<ts>-a, private: true, auto_init: false }`, with C5's token | status; `private`; `visibility`; `default_branch` |
| C7 | the same for `-b` | status |
| C8 | installation token, `repositories: [<-a>]`, `permissions: { contents: 'write' }`, **one second after C6** — `[M19](a)` | status (the answer to *can a just-created repository be named*) |
| C9 | `git push` of one commit to `-a` with C8's token, then `ls-remote` | git's exit; whether `ls-remote` shows the commit |
| C10 | `GET /repos/{org}/<-b>` with C8's token | status (`[M19](d)`: 404 or 403) |
| C11 | a `contents: read` token for `-a`, then `git push` | git's exit; the first `remote:` or `fatal:` line with the org and repository names replaced by `<org>` and `<repo>` |
| C12 | `PATCH /repos/{org}/<-a>` `{ private: true }` (no-op) | status; `private` |
| C13 | `PUT /repos/{org}/<-a>/branches/main/protection`, `{ required_status_checks: null, enforce_admins: false, required_pull_request_reviews: null, restrictions: null, allow_force_pushes: false, allow_deletions: false }` | status; `message` when refused (`[M19](b)`) |
| C14 | `DELETE` both repositories | statuses |

**C13 on the FAKE runs with `plan: 'free'` AND with `'team'`**, so golden holds GitHub's documented answer for each. The real run's organisation is free, so its C13 is the free-plan answer. **Delete in a `finally`**, so a failing step still removes both real repositories, and print their names if the delete itself fails, so Rich can remove them by hand.

- [ ] **Step 2: `golden.json` from GitHub's documentation — the fake must meet it before any real run**

Each step's expected facts come from the description (`github-schemas.json`'s `facts` and schemas) and GitHub's docs, with a `"why"` beside each. Examples: C5's token class `stateless` (the April 2026 note); C6 `private: true`, `visibility: 'private'`; C10 `404` (GitHub hides a private repository); C13 on a free plan `403`, with the documented upgrade message; C2 `401`. **Where the documentation is silent** — C8 in particular — the entry says `"unknown": true`, and the test ASSERTS NOTHING for it and says so in its name. That case is exactly what the real run exists for.

- [ ] **Step 3: The unit test, and watch it fail first**

```ts
// conformance.test.ts
import golden from '../conformance/golden.json' with { type: 'json' }
it.each([['team'], ['free']] as const)('the fake answers what the golden record says GitHub answers (plan: %s)', async (plan) => {
  const fake = await startFake({ plan })
  try {
    const answers = await runConformance({ ...fake, label: `fake/${plan}`, appKeyPem: fake.appKeyPem })
    for (const a of answers) {
      const want = (golden.steps as Record<string, GoldenStep>)[a.step]
      if (want === undefined) throw new Error(`golden has no ${a.step}`)
      if (want.unknown === true) continue          // stated in golden; the real run decides
      const expected = want.byPlan?.[plan] ?? want
      expect({ step: a.step, status: a.status, ...pick(a.facts, Object.keys(expected.facts ?? {})) })
        .toEqual({ step: a.step, status: expected.status, ...(expected.facts ?? {}) })
    }
  } finally { await fake.stop() }
})
```

**`golden.json` is written first, and the test fails against Task 4's fake wherever the fake is wrong** — the free-plan C13 at least, since protection is Task 12's. **A C13 the fake cannot answer yet is `"pendingTask": 12`** in golden. The test SKIPS it by name, and Task 12 removes the marker. **No other step may be skipped.**

- [ ] **Step 4: `make github-conformance` — skippable, never a failure offline**

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# scripts/github-conformance.sh — the fake's answers against REAL GitHub (Rich's decision, 2026-09-24).
# OPT-IN: needs infra/secrets/github-conformance.json and infra/secrets/github-app.pem (the plan's
# "What Rich does" 2), the network, and Rich's yes — it creates and deletes two repositories.
# SKIPPED, exit 0, when any of those is missing: the offline acceptance must never fail on it.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
CONF=infra/secrets/github-conformance.json KEY=infra/secrets/github-app.pem
[ -f "$CONF" ] && [ -f "$KEY" ] || { echo "SKIPPED — no real App configured (see RUNBOOK, The conformance run)"; exit 0; }
curl -sS -m 5 -o /dev/null https://api.github.com/zen || { echo "SKIPPED — api.github.com is unreachable (the network is off)"; exit 0; }
node --import ./packages/github-fake/resolve-ts.mjs packages/github-fake/src/conformance-main.ts "$CONF" "$KEY"
```

`conformance-main.ts` (four lines over `runConformance`) writes `packages/github-fake/conformance/github.com-<date>.json` and prints each step whose answer differs from golden. **The file is committed as evidence.** Then a person replaces golden's `documentation` entries with GitHub's answers, sets `"source": "github.com <date>, App <id>"`, and **the fake is fixed until the unit test is green again**. No token and no private key appears in the output: `normalise` keeps only the facts column above, and the step's test asserts that no string in the output starts `ghs_`, `ghp_` or `-----BEGIN`.

## Task 7: The GitHub driver — a mirror, tokens that never leave the process, and the same contract suite

> **`[M5]` AND `[M14]` — TWO CORRECTIONS (2026-09-24, F3 and F6). Read `spikes/d5-baseline/README.md`'s
> `[M14]` section before `sync`.**
> **(1) F3 — force `GIT_TRACE_REDACT: '1'` AFTER the `...process.env` spread in `gitWithToken`.** Form C is
> safe from argv and the URL (re-measured), and the message is built from stderr and redacted — but the
> environment is also where git's trace variables live. With an inherited `GIT_TRACE_CURL=<file>` and
> `GIT_TRACE_REDACT=0`, **the token's base64 is written to that FILE**, where no message redactor reaches;
> forcing `GIT_TRACE_REDACT=1` after the spread writes `<redacted>` instead (`probes/leak-trace-file.mjs`).
> Add it to `git.test.ts`: with those two variables in `process.env`, the trace file holds no canary form.
> **(2) F6 — `sync` fetches with TWO refspecs, and every READ of "what GitHub has now" uses the second.**
> Measured: after ONE rewrite of upstream `main`, EVERY later non-forced fetch reports `!` for `main`, exit 1
> — **a perfectly normal push Y on top of the rewritten history is reported as another rewrite and never as
> an update**, and Y lands in the mirror's object store unreferenced. As written, that makes `headCommit`
> answer the frozen `refs/heads/main` for ever (**a stale head presented as current — exactly what Decision
> 18 forbids**), `listBranches` stale, and `commitFiles` clone the frozen `main` and be refused
> `SOURCE_CONFLICT` for ever, however often the caller retries. **The fix, measured**: one fetch,
> `refs/heads/*:refs/heads/*` (non-forced: the history Decision 1 keeps) **and**
> `+refs/heads/*:refs/manifest/upstream/*` (forced: GitHub now). The shadow's porcelain says `+` once for
> the rewrite, a plain fast-forward for Y, and nothing on a quiet sync (`probes/mirror-shadow.sh`).
> **`headCommit`, `listBranches` and `commitFiles`' clone base read `refs/manifest/upstream/*`**;
> `refs/heads/*` is only the history keeper, beside `refs/manifest/kept/<sha>` (Task 9). A `!` on
> `refs/heads/*` with the shadow's `+` on the same branch is the expected, already-reported consequence —
> in THIS task still a throw, as written; Task 9 parses it. Add a contract-suite case now: after a rewrite
> and a normal push, `headCommit` answers the push, not the mirror's frozen `main`.

**D5's driver 2.** It passes Task 2's contract suite **unchanged** against the in-process fake, and then the promises only a remote driver has to keep: Decisions 1, 4, 12 (at creation), 16 and 18. **The build path does not change**: `localGitDir` returns the mirror.

```ts
// source/github/driver.ts
export interface GithubDriverOptions {
  mirrorRoot: string          // config.reposRoot — the mirror lives where driver 1's repositories do (Decision 1)
  apiUrl: string; gitUrl: string; org: string; appId: string; installationId: string
  appKey: KeyObject
  fetch?: typeof fetch        // a test's spy; production uses the global
  now?: () => Date
}
export function createGithubSourceDriver(o: GithubDriverOptions): SourceDriver   // name: 'github'

// source/github/tokens.ts
export type TokenPermissions = Partial<Record<'contents' | 'administration', 'read' | 'write'>>
export interface TokenCache {
  forRepository(repository: string, permissions: TokenPermissions): Promise<string>   // cached until 5 min before expiry
  installationWide(permissions: { administration: 'write' }): Promise<string>        // NEVER cached — Decision 4's one exception
  forget(repository: string): void                                                    // after a 401: re-mint once
}

// source/github/git.ts
export function gitWithToken(args: readonly string[], o: { cwd: string; token?: string; timeoutMs?: number }): Promise<string>
export function redactToken(text: string, token: string | undefined): string

// api/testing.ts
export async function githubTestDeps(fake: StartedFake): Promise<ServerDeps>   // testDeps() with source = GitHub over `fake`
```

The control plane resolves `NodeNext` with `rootDir: src`, and it has never imported a workspace package before (`grep '"@manifest/' packages/control-plane/package.json` finds none). `@manifest/mock` and `@manifest/journey` consume `@manifest/contract` through a `types` export that points at `.ts` source, so the pattern is known to work — **in their configurations, not yet in this one**.
```ts
// source/github/driver.test.ts
import { mkdir, mkdtemp, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises'
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os'
import { dirname, join } from 'node:path'
import { createPrivateKey } from 'node:crypto'
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import { startFake, type StartedFake } from '@manifest/github-fake/testing'
import { describeSourceDriver } from '../driver-contract.js'
import { SourceError } from '../index.js'
import { createGithubSourceDriver } from './driver.js'
import { gitWithToken } from './git.js'

async function harness(options: Parameters<typeof startFake>[0] = {}) {
  const fake = await startFake(options)
  const mirrorRoot = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'manifest-mirror-'))
  const minted: string[] = []
  // A spy on the wire: every installation token GitHub hands the driver, so a test can look
  // for it anywhere a person could read it. It never changes an answer.
  const spyFetch: typeof fetch = async (input, init) => {
    const res = await fetch(input, init)
    if (String(input).endsWith('/access_tokens') && res.status === 201) {
      const body = await res.clone().json() as { token: string }
      minted.push(body.token)
    }
    return res
  }
  const driver = createGithubSourceDriver({
    mirrorRoot, apiUrl: fake.apiUrl, gitUrl: fake.gitUrl, org: fake.org,
    appId: fake.appId, installationId: fake.installationId, appKey: createPrivateKey(fake.appKeyPem), fetch: spyFetch,
  })
  return { fake, mirrorRoot, minted, driver, cleanup: async () => { await fake.stop(); await rm(mirrorRoot, { recursive: true, force: true }) } }
}

/** A person pushing straight to GitHub with their own token — outside Manifest. */
async function pushAsPerson(fake: StartedFake, slug: string, files: Record<string, string>, message: string): Promise<string> {
  const work = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'person-'))
  const remote = `${fake.gitUrl}/${fake.org}/${slug}.git`
  const as = { cwd: work, token: fake.developerToken }
  try {
    await gitWithToken(['clone', '-q', remote, '.'], as)
    for (const [path, content] of Object.entries(files)) {
      await mkdir(dirname(join(work, path)), { recursive: true })
      await writeFile(join(work, path), content)
    }
    await gitWithToken(['add', '-A'], as)
    await gitWithToken(['-c', 'user.name=person', '-c', 'user.email=person@example.org', '-c', 'commit.gpgsign=false', 'commit', '-qm', message], as)
    await gitWithToken(['push', '-q', 'origin', 'HEAD:main'], as)
    return (await gitWithToken(['rev-parse', 'HEAD'], as)).trim()
  } finally { await rm(work, { recursive: true, force: true }) }
}

describeSourceDriver('github', async () => {
  const h = await harness()
  return { driver: h.driver, pushAsPerson: (slug, files, m) => pushAsPerson(h.fake, slug, files, m), cleanup: h.cleanup }
})

describe('the GitHub driver keeps what only a REMOTE driver has to promise', () => {
  const SEED = { 'manifest.yaml': 'manifest: 1\nname: chem-labs\n' }
  const codeOf = (p: Promise<unknown>) => p.then(() => 'resolved', (e) => (e instanceof SourceError ? e.code : String(e)))

  it('never puts a token where a person can read it — the canary', async () => {
    const h = await harness()
    try {
      const repo = await h.driver.createRepository('chem-labs', SEED)
      expect(h.minted.length).toBeGreaterThan(0)                          // the positive control: tokens WERE minted
      await h.fake.stop()                                                 // GitHub goes away mid-life
      const failure = await h.driver.headCommit(repo).catch((e: unknown) => e)
      expect(failure).toBeInstanceOf(SourceError)
      expect((failure as SourceError).code).toBe('SOURCE_UNREACHABLE')
      const visible = `${(failure as Error).message}\n${String(failure)}\n${JSON.stringify(failure)}`
      for (const t of h.minted) {
        expect(visible).not.toContain(t)
        expect(visible).not.toContain(Buffer.from(`x-access-token:${t}`).toString('base64'))
      }
      expect(visible).not.toMatch(/ghs_\d+_/)
    } finally { await h.cleanup() }

  it('scopes every token to ONE repository and the least permission, except the one that creates it', async () => {

  it('offline: a mirrored commit still builds, and HEAD is 503, never stale', async () => {
    const h = await harness()
```

**Three test-only helpers belong beside `pushAsPerson`**: `createAsPerson`, `getAsPerson` and `lsRemoteMain`, each fifteen lines or fewer over `fetch`/`gitWithToken` with the developer's token. **`startFake`'s `quirks.createPublic` is added to Task 4's fake in this task**, as a test-only option that `main.ts` never sets. The two cases above that are only described in comments are written out in full when this task runs, in the shape of their neighbours. Their assertions are stated exactly, and none of them is optional.

`git.test.ts` holds three cases. (1) `redactToken` removes a token, its `x-access-token:` base64 and a bare `Authorization: Basic …` value. (2) `gitWithToken(['ls-remote', 'http://127.0.0.1:7196/o/r.git'], { token: CANARY })` fails with a `SourceError` whose message has no canary, and whose code is `SOURCE_UNREACHABLE`. (3) The same call against a listening server authenticates, which is Task 1 `[M5]`'s form C, now in the unit tier.

The contract suite gains: **`readFile` at a commit the repository does not have → `SOURCE_COMMIT_NOT_FOUND`**. Today driver 1 answers `null` for that, as it does for a path not in the tree. On driver 2 a `null` would read as *there is no `manifest.yaml`*, and the validate route would record an invalid spec for a commit it never read. **Measure git's two stderr messages first** — `git show <missing-sha>:x` and `git show <sha>:missing` — and split driver 1 on them.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement — `git.ts` and `tokens.ts` in full, because they are where a token lives**

```ts
// source/github/git.ts
import { execFile } from 'node:child_process'
import { promisify } from 'node:util'
import { SourceError } from '../git-driver.js'
const run = promisify(execFile)

/** Every form a token can take on the way to git. Applied to EVERY message this module builds. */
export function redactToken(text: string, token: string | undefined): string {
  if (token === undefined || token.length === 0) return text
  const basic = Buffer.from(`x-access-token:${token}`).toString('base64')
  return text.split(token).join('[token]').split(basic).join('[token]')
}

const UNREACHABLE = /Could not resolve host|Failed to connect|Connection refused|Couldn't connect|timed out|Empty reply/i

/**
 * git with a GitHub token, the only way this codebase hands git one (Decision 4; Task 1 [M5]):
 * through GIT_CONFIG_* in the ENVIRONMENT, never argv and never the URL — both of which reach
 * `String(error)` and from there a `SourceError` message on the wire. System AND global config
 * are ignored, so macOS's osxkeychain helper (Xcode's system gitconfig) never stores or asks
 * for a credential, and a developer's ~/.gitconfig cannot change what the platform does.
 *
 * The message is built from git's STDERR, never from `String(error)`, which begins with every
 * argument — and then redacted anyway, in case a remote echoes something back.
 */
export async function gitWithToken(args: readonly string[], o: { cwd: string; token?: string; timeoutMs?: number }): Promise<string> {
  const env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = {
    ...process.env,
    GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT: '0', GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM: '1', GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL: '/dev/null', GIT_ASKPASS: '', SSH_ASKPASS: '',
  }
  const config: [string, string][] = [['credential.helper', '']]
  if (o.token !== undefined) {
    config.push(['http.extraHeader', `Authorization: Basic ${Buffer.from(`x-access-token:${o.token}`).toString('base64')}`])
  }
  config.forEach(([k, v], i) => { env[`GIT_CONFIG_KEY_${i}`] = k; env[`GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_${i}`] = v })
  env.GIT_CONFIG_COUNT = String(config.length)
  try {
    const { stdout } = await run('git', [...args], { cwd: o.cwd, env, maxBuffer: 32 * 1024 * 1024, timeout: o.timeoutMs ?? 120_000 })
    return stdout
  } catch (error) {
    const stderr = String((error as { stderr?: unknown }).stderr ?? '').trim().split('\n').slice(-5).join(' | ')
    const code = UNREACHABLE.test(stderr) || (error as { killed?: boolean }).killed === true ? 'SOURCE_UNREACHABLE' : 'SOURCE_GIT_FAILED'
    throw code === 'SOURCE_UNREACHABLE'
      ? new SourceError('SOURCE_UNREACHABLE', redactToken(`GitHub could not be reached (git ${args[0]}): ${stderr}`, o.token))
      : new SourceError('SOURCE_GIT_FAILED', redactToken(`git ${args[0]} failed: ${stderr}`, o.token))
  }
}
```

**Two literal `new SourceError('…'` constructions, not one with a variable code**, because the registry finds codes only as literals (*Read this first* 16).

```ts
// source/github/tokens.ts
export function createTokenCache(i: { client: GithubClient; installationId: string; now: () => Date }): TokenCache {
  const cache = new Map<string, { token: string; expiresAt: number }>()
  const key = (repo: string, p: TokenPermissions) => `${repo} ${JSON.stringify(Object.entries(p).sort())}`
  async function mint(body: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<{ token: string; expiresAt: number }> {
    const res = await i.client.asApp('POST', `/app/installations/${i.installationId}/access_tokens`, body)
    if (res.status !== 201) throw i.client.refusal('mint an installation token', res)
    const b = res.json as { token: string; expires_at: string }
    return { token: b.token, expiresAt: Date.parse(b.expires_at) }
  }
  return {
    async forRepository(repository, permissions) {
      const k = key(repository, permissions)
      const hit = cache.get(k)
      if (hit !== undefined && hit.expiresAt - i.now().getTime() > 5 * 60_000) return hit.token
      const fresh = await mint({ repositories: [repository], permissions })
      cache.set(k, fresh)
      return fresh.token
    },
    // Decision 4's one exception: a token cannot name a repository that does not exist yet.
    // Administration only, used for one call, and never kept.
    async installationWide(permissions) { return (await mint({ permissions })).token },
    forget(repository) { for (const k of cache.keys()) if (k.startsWith(`${repository} `)) cache.delete(k) },
  }
}
```

`client.ts` is a thin `fetch` wrapper. `asApp(method, path, body?)` signs with `appJwt`. `asToken(token, method, path, body?)` sends `Authorization: token <t>`, `Accept: application/vnd.github+json` and `X-GitHub-Api-Version: 2022-11-28`. Both return `{ status, json }`. **A `fetch` that rejects** (`ECONNREFUSED`, a DNS failure, a timeout from `AbortSignal.timeout(10_000)`) becomes `SourceError('SOURCE_UNREACHABLE', …)`. `refusal(operation, res)` builds `SourceError('SOURCE_GITHUB_REFUSED', `GitHub answered ${status} to ${operation}: ${message}`)` from GitHub's `message` field alone, truncated to 200 characters.

**The driver** (`driver.ts`) implements the eight methods over a mirror at `join(mirrorRoot, `${slug}.git`)`:

- `repositoryFor(slug)` → `{ projectSlug: slug, provider: 'github' }` after §7's slug rule, **re-stated here as driver 1 does** (the traversal defence must not depend on another module).
- `createRepository(slug, seed)`:
  1. a mirror directory already there → `SOURCE_REPOSITORY_EXISTS`;
  2. `installationWide({ administration: 'write' })` → `POST /orgs/{org}/repos { name, private: true, auto_init: false }`. `422` → `SOURCE_REPOSITORY_EXISTS`; any other non-`201` → `refusal`;
  3. the answer must read `private: true` and `visibility: 'private'`, or the driver deletes the repository and throws `SOURCE_REPOSITORY_NOT_PRIVATE` (Decision 12);
  4. the seed commit in a throwaway worktree, with `GIT_IDENTITY`, pushed `HEAD:main` with `forRepository(slug, { contents: 'write' })`;
  5. `git init --bare` the mirror, write its `pre-receive` (the refusing hook of *Read this first* 12), and `git config manifest.visibility private`;
  6. `sync`.
  **If any step after the POST fails**, `DELETE` the repository with `forRepository(slug, { administration: 'write' })` and remove the mirror. Then **re-throw the original error**. If the delete itself fails, write a `console.error` naming the repository, so an orphan is never silent.
- `sync(slug)` (private until Task 9 exposes it) → `gitWithToken(['fetch', '--porcelain', remote, 'refs/heads/*:refs/heads/*'], { cwd: mirror, token: forRepository(slug, { contents: 'read' }) })`, where `remote` is `${gitUrl}/${org}/${slug}.git`. **Non-forced, and never `--prune`**: a branch deleted on GitHub keeps its ref in the mirror, so nothing a release built from becomes unreachable and is lost to `gc` (deleting such a ref is what `--prune` is documented to do — read, not measured). In this task a rejected ref makes `sync` throw `SOURCE_GIT_FAILED`; Task 9 parses the porcelain output instead and reports it. **A `401` / `Authentication failed`** calls `forget(slug)` and retries once.
- `headCommit(repo, ref)`: `sync`, then `rev-parse refs/heads/<ref>` in the mirror.
- `readFile(repo, sha, path)`: the commit in the mirror → `git show`, where a path not in the tree is `null`. Not in the mirror → `sync` → retry → still not there → `SOURCE_COMMIT_NOT_FOUND`.
- `listBranches`: `sync`, then `for-each-ref` in the mirror.
- `localGitDir(repo, sha)`: the 40-hex check (Task 2); in the mirror → return it; else `sync`, and still absent → `SOURCE_COMMIT_NOT_FOUND`. **A `sync` that is `SOURCE_UNREACHABLE` propagates as that code**, never as *not found*: a commit that may exist on GitHub is not reported as one that does not.
- `commitFiles(repo, files, message)`: `sync`; clone the MIRROR into a throwaway worktree (local and fast); write the files with Task 2's path-escape check; commit; push `HEAD:main` to GitHub, **non-forced**. `rejected … (fetch first)` / `non-fast-forward` → `SOURCE_CONFLICT`: GitHub moved since the sync, and the caller retries. Then `sync`, and return the new sha.
- `destroyRepository(repo)`: `DELETE /repos/{org}/{slug}` with `forRepository(slug, { administration: 'write' })`, then remove the mirror.
- **Every method starts with the provider check** of Task 2's contract (`SOURCE_PROVIDER_MISMATCH` for a `local` reference).

`githubTestDeps(fake)` is `testDeps()` with `source` replaced by a driver over `fake` and a temp mirror root, and **`builds` rebuilt over the same driver and bus**, as `testDeps`' own comment requires of anything that replaces a collaborator.

```ts
  // A git host that cannot be reached is not a state conflict (D5 plan, Decision 18): a client
  // retries a 503 and does not "fix" a 409. The message names the provider and never a token.
  if (error instanceof SourceError && error.code === 'SOURCE_UNREACHABLE') {
    return { status: 503, body: { error: { code: error.code, message: error.message, hint: 'The git host did not answer. A commit already mirrored still builds; try again when it is reachable.' } } }
  }
```

```ts
// source/github/build.docker.test.ts — a REAL BuildKit build from a repository in the fake's IMAGE
it('builds from the mirror, and builds the same commit again with GitHub gone', async () => {
  const fake = await startFakeContainer()                               // Task 5's throwaway container
  const driver = createGithubSourceDriver({ mirrorRoot: await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'mf-mirror-')), ...fake.options })
  try {
    const repo = await driver.createRepository('gh-build', readFixtureTree('fixture-node'))
    const sha = await driver.headCommit(repo)
    const input = { projectSlug: 'gh-build', commitSha: sha, blueprintRef: 'fixture-node@1', repoPath: (await driver.localGitDir(repo, sha)).gitDir }
    expect((await buildToEnd(deps, await withProjectRows(input))).status).toBe('succeeded')
    await fake.stop()                                                   // docker stop — GitHub is gone
    expect((await buildToEnd(deps, await withProjectRows({ ...input, repoPath: (await driver.localGitDir(repo, sha)).gitDir }))).status).toBe('succeeded')
    await expect(driver.headCommit(repo)).rejects.toMatchObject({ code: 'SOURCE_UNREACHABLE' })
  } finally { await fake.remove() }
})
```

`readFixtureTree` reads `fixtures/fixture-node` (or whatever `fixtureBareRepo` seeds from — **read `runtime/docker/testing.ts:295` and seed from the same source**) into a `SeedFiles`. `withProjectRows` inserts the project, spec and build rows that `buildToEnd`'s `StartBuildInput` references, the way the other Docker files do. **Read one of them before writing this.**

## Task 9: Webhooks — the fake delivers signed events, the receiver refuses an ABSENT signature by its own code, and every mirror advance is reported once

> **`[M14]` — THE PORCELAIN IS READ FROM THE SHADOW REFS (2026-09-24, F6; Task 7's correction (2)).**
> After a rewrite, `refs/heads/main` reports `!` on EVERY later sync, so the parsing below — `!` →
> `rewritten`, ` `/`*` → `updated` — would publish `repository.history_rewritten` on every push for ever and
> never report, validate or scan another push to `main`. **Parse the `refs/manifest/upstream/*` lines
> instead**: `+` (forced) → `rewritten` **and** its new commits into what is scanned; ` ` and `*` →
> `updated`; `=` skipped. A `!` on `refs/heads/<b>` is expected whenever `refs/manifest/upstream/<b>`
> differs from it, and is not reported again. `process()`'s *"a push whose `refs/heads/main` moved"* means
> the SHADOW's `main`. **Add to *a rewritten main is refused by the mirror and reported*: a normal push
> AFTER the rewrite is reported `repository.pushed`, validated, and the rewrite event exists exactly
> once** — predicted red against the parsing as first written, which is the control that it is in force.
> **And `[M9]` (F12):** a delivery sent as `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` — a content type a GitHub
> App's webhook can be configured with — is refused `415` by Fastify before HMAC runs, as is one with no
> content type. The fake sends JSON; **a real App must be registered with content type
> `application/json`**, which belongs in the RUNBOOK's registration steps beside *What Rich does* 2's.

**§20: *"Webhook payloads verified by HMAC before any processing."*** And ORIENTATION §7e's lesson from 2026-09-24, which is this task's design constraint: **node-saml verified a signature that was PRESENT and accepted one that was ABSENT, while every test fired garbage and passed.** So this task's tests send a real signed delivery, an unsigned one, a SHA-1-only one, a malformed one and a wrongly-signed one, and assert each refusal's CODE beside the accepted one. It also makes the mirror's sync report instead of fail: a rewritten branch is refused and named, a fast-forward lands, and both reach the one observer (Decision 11).

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/github-fake/src/webhooks.ts`, `webhooks.test.ts` — sign, deliver, record, redeliver
- Modify: `packages/github-fake/src/git-http.ts`, `server.ts` — a `push` after every successful receive-pack; `/_fake/deliveries`, `/_fake/deliveries/{id}/redeliver`
- Create: `packages/control-plane/src/api/webhook-signature.ts`, `webhook-signature.test.ts`
- Create: `packages/control-plane/src/api/routes/webhooks.ts`
