```ts
// source/git-driver.ts
export interface MirrorAdvance {
  projectSlug: string
  /** Branches that moved (or appeared), as the mirror now has them. */
  updated: { ref: string; from: string | null; to: string }[]
  /** Branches GitHub rewrote, which the mirror REFUSED — it keeps `mirror`, GitHub has `upstream`. */
  rewritten: { ref: string; mirror: string; upstream: string }[]
}
export interface SourceObserver { advanced(advance: MirrorAdvance): Promise<void> }
// SourceDriver gains:
  /** Bring the local copy up to date with the provider, and report what moved (Decision 11). */
  sync(repo: RepoRef): Promise<MirrorAdvance>

// GithubDriverOptions gains, REQUIRED — so `tsc` names every place that builds a driver without one:
// src/index.ts (the real observer), api/testing.ts's githubTestDeps (a RECORDING observer the tests read),
// source/github/driver.test.ts's harness, and build.docker.test.ts / webhook.docker.test.ts. Update each.
  observer: SourceObserver

// api/webhook-signature.ts
export type SignatureVerdict = 'valid' | 'missing' | 'malformed' | 'invalid'
export function verifySignature(secret: Buffer, body: Buffer, header: string | string[] | undefined): SignatureVerdict

// source/queue.ts
export interface SerialQueue { enqueue(label: string, job: () => Promise<void>): void; idle(): Promise<void> }
export function createSerialQueue(): SerialQueue

// api/spec-validation.ts — the validate route's body, now with two callers
export async function validateAndRecord(deps: ServerDeps, project: { id: string; slug: string; quota: unknown }, commitSha: string): Promise<SpecValidationResult>
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Measure first, on the committed tree — what the server's error handler does to a `401`, and to a missing content type**

Read `api/server.ts`'s `setErrorHandler`: **any error whose `statusCode` is `401` is answered as the generic `UNAUTHENTICATED`**. So a webhook refusal THROWN as a 401 would lose its code — exactly the collapse Decision 9 exists to prevent. **The route therefore REPLIES its four refusals rather than throwing them** (`reply.status(401).send({ error: { code, message, hint } })`). Each is a literal `code: 'WEBHOOK_…'` in `api/`, which the registry's scan reads (`error-codes.test.ts:86`). Then measure, with `app.inject` against `buildServer(await testDeps())` once the route exists, what a POST with no `content-type` answers. **Assert whatever envelope it is, and that it is not a `500`.**

- [ ] **Step 2: The failing tests — the receiver**

```ts
// api/webhook-signature.test.ts
import { createHmac } from 'node:crypto'
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import { verifySignature } from './webhook-signature.js'

describe('the webhook signature (§20: verified by HMAC before any processing)', () => {
  // GitHub's OWN test vector (docs.github.com, validating webhook deliveries; Read this first 6).
  const VECTOR = { secret: "It's a Secret to Everybody", body: 'Hello, World!', header: 'sha256=757107ea0eb2509fc211221cce984b8a37570b6d7586c22c46f4379c8b043e17' }
  const secret = Buffer.from(VECTOR.secret)
  const body = Buffer.from(VECTOR.body)

  it('accepts GitHub’s published vector — the positive control', () => {
    expect(verifySignature(secret, body, VECTOR.header)).toBe('valid')
  })
  it('refuses an ABSENT header as missing — and does not throw (timingSafeEqual would)', () => {
    expect(verifySignature(secret, body, undefined)).toBe('missing')
  })
  it.each([
    ['empty', ''], ['no prefix', '757107ea0eb2509fc211221cce984b8a37570b6d7586c22c46f4379c8b043e17'],
    ['sha1', 'sha1=' + 'a'.repeat(40)], ['short', 'sha256=abc'], ['uppercase', VECTOR.header.toUpperCase()],
    ['sent twice', [VECTOR.header, VECTOR.header]],
  ])('refuses %s as malformed', (_l, h) => { expect(verifySignature(secret, body, h)).toBe('malformed') })
  it('refuses a well-formed signature over different bytes as invalid', () => {
    expect(verifySignature(secret, Buffer.from('Hello, World?'), VECTOR.header)).toBe('invalid')
  })
  it('refuses the right body under another secret as invalid', () => {
    expect(verifySignature(Buffer.from('another'), body, VECTOR.header)).toBe('invalid')
  })
})
```

```ts
// api/webhooks.test.ts — against a driver-2 server over an in-process fake
// helpers: `deliver(ctx, { event, body, signature?: 'good' | 'none' | 'sha1-only' | string, id? })` injects
// POST /webhooks/github with content-type application/json, X-GitHub-Event, X-GitHub-Delivery (a uuid
// unless given) and — per `signature` — X-Hub-Signature-256 computed with the fake's secret, nothing,
// only a CORRECT X-Hub-Signature (sha1), or the literal string. `pushPayload(fake, slug, before, after)`
// is the fake's own builder (Task 9's webhooks.ts), so the body is GitHub-shaped.
describe('POST /webhooks/github', () => {
  it('accepts a signed push, queues ONE sync, and the new commit is validated — the positive control', async () => {
    // create `hook-app` on driver 2; a person pushes a manifest change to the fake (pushAsPerson);
    // deliver(good) → 202 { queued: true }; await deps.sourceSync.idle();
    // the mirror has the commit (localGitDir resolves); ONE webhook_deliveries row;
    // a `repository.pushed` event { ref: 'refs/heads/main', from, to }; an app_specs row for `to`.
  })
  it('refuses an absent signature and records NOTHING', async () => {
    // deliver(none) → refusal(res) toEqual { status: 401, code: 'WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MISSING' };
    // webhook_deliveries count 0; after idle(), no repository.pushed event and no new app_specs row.
  })
  it('refuses a delivery carrying only the legacy SHA-1 signature as MISSING', async () => { /* sha1-only → 401 MISSING */ })
  it('refuses a malformed signature, and a wrong one, each by its own code', async () => {
    // 'sha256=zz' → 401 WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MALFORMED; a well-formed signature of other bytes → 401 WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_INVALID.
  })
  it('verifies BEFORE it parses: a wrongly signed body that is not JSON is INVALID, not a parse error', async () => {
    // body 'not json', signature over other bytes → 401 WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_INVALID (not 400).
    // And a CORRECTLY signed 'not json' → 400 WEBHOOK_PAYLOAD_INVALID — the ordering's own positive control.

  it('answers a delivery it has seen with 200 duplicate, and changes nothing', async () => {
```

- every `push` payload validates as `webhook push` in `github-schemas.json` (Task 4's `expectGitHubShape`);

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement — the signature and the route in full**

```ts
// api/webhook-signature.ts
import { createHmac, timingSafeEqual } from 'node:crypto'

/**
 * GitHub's X-Hub-Signature-256: `sha256=` and 64 lowercase hex characters, the HMAC-SHA256 of the
 * RAW body under the App's webhook secret (docs.github.com; its own test vector is in the test).
 *
 * FOUR ANSWERS, NOT TWO (Decision 9). An ABSENT header is its own answer — the 2026-09-24 SLO
 * defect was a verifier that checked a signature when one was there and accepted a message
 * without one. The legacy SHA-1 `X-Hub-Signature` is never read: a delivery carrying only that
 * is `missing`. Two headers is `malformed`, because an ambiguity resolved silently is the shape
 * of a confused deputy (`api/server.ts`, CREDENTIAL_AMBIGUOUS).
 *
 * Constant time, and only between EQUAL LENGTHS: `timingSafeEqual` throws on a length mismatch
 * (Read this first 6), and the regex below fixes the length before it is called.
 */
export function verifySignature(secret: Buffer, body: Buffer, header: string | string[] | undefined): 'valid' | 'missing' | 'malformed' | 'invalid' {
  if (header === undefined) return 'missing'
  if (Array.isArray(header)) return 'malformed'
  const m = /^sha256=([0-9a-f]{64})$/.exec(header)
  if (m === null) return 'malformed'
  const given = Buffer.from(m[1]!, 'hex')
  const want = createHmac('sha256', secret).update(body).digest()
  return given.length === want.length && timingSafeEqual(given, want) ? 'valid' : 'invalid'
}
```

```ts
// api/routes/webhooks.ts — POST /webhooks/github (unversioned; api/unversioned.ts says why)
import type { FastifyPluginAsync, FastifyReply } from 'fastify'
import { verifySignature } from '../webhook-signature.js'

const REFUSALS = {
  missing: { code: 'WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MISSING', message: 'the delivery carries no X-Hub-Signature-256', hint: 'GitHub signs every delivery when the App has a webhook secret; an unsigned request is not from GitHub.' },
  malformed: { code: 'WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MALFORMED', message: 'X-Hub-Signature-256 is not one sha256= and 64 lowercase hex characters' },
  invalid: { code: 'WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_INVALID', message: 'X-Hub-Signature-256 does not match this body under the App’s webhook secret' },
} as const

export const webhookRoutes = (deps: WebhookDeps): FastifyPluginAsync => async (app) => {
  // A RAW body, in THIS plugin only (Read this first 7): the HMAC is over the bytes GitHub sent,
  // and a re-serialised object is not those bytes.
  app.removeContentTypeParser('application/json')
  app.addContentTypeParser('application/json', { parseAs: 'buffer' }, (_r, body, done) => done(null, body))

  app.post('/webhooks/github', {
    bodyLimit: 5 * 1024 * 1024,                               // Decision 8: the receiver reads four fields
    config: { csrf: 'exempt', idempotency: 'exempt' },        // Read this first 19: GitHub sends neither
  }, async (request, reply) => {
    // REPLIED, NOT THROWN: server.ts's error handler flattens any 401 into UNAUTHENTICATED (Step 1).
    const refuse = (status: number, e: { code: string; message: string; hint?: string }) =>
      (reply as FastifyReply).status(status).send({ error: e })
    if (deps.secret === undefined) {
      return refuse(404, { code: 'WEBHOOKS_NOT_CONFIGURED', message: 'this control plane runs the local source driver, which receives no webhooks' })
    }
    const verdict = verifySignature(deps.secret, request.body as Buffer, request.headers['x-hub-signature-256'])
    if (verdict !== 'valid') return refuse(401, REFUSALS[verdict])
    // ── nothing above this line reads the body's CONTENT ──
    let payload: GithubPayload
    try { payload = JSON.parse((request.body as Buffer).toString('utf8')) as GithubPayload } catch {
      return refuse(400, { code: 'WEBHOOK_PAYLOAD_INVALID', message: 'the signed body is not JSON' })
    }
    const deliveryId = request.headers['x-github-delivery']
    const event = request.headers['x-github-event']
    if (typeof deliveryId !== 'string' || !/^[0-9a-f-]{36}$/.test(deliveryId) || typeof event !== 'string') {
      return refuse(400, { code: 'WEBHOOK_PAYLOAD_INVALID', message: 'X-GitHub-Delivery and X-GitHub-Event are required' })
    }
    if (!(await deps.recordDelivery(deliveryId, event))) return reply.status(200).send({ duplicate: true })
    if (event === 'ping') return reply.status(200).send({ pong: true })
    const slug = deps.slugOf(payload.repository?.full_name)     // `${org}/<slug>` → slug, anything else → undefined
    if (slug === undefined || !(await deps.holds(slug))) return reply.status(202).send({ ignored: 'unknown repository' })
    if (event === 'push' || (event === 'repository' && payload.action === 'publicized')) {
      deps.queue.enqueue(`webhook ${event} ${slug} ${deliveryId}`, () => deps.process(slug, event))
      return reply.status(202).send({ queued: true })
    }
    return reply.status(202).send({ ignored: event })
  })
}
```

`WebhookDeps` is built in `buildServer`, as `registryTokenRoutes`' deps are. It holds:
- `secret`: `undefined` on driver 1. On driver 2 it is read from `config.github.webhookSecretPath` after `assertOwnerOnly(path, 'SECRET_GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET_PERMISSIONS')` — **the same custody rule, a third caller**.
- `recordDelivery`: `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING`, answering whether a row was written.
- `slugOf`, `holds`: a `projects` row with a `github` repository row.
- `queue`: `deps.sourceSync`.
- `process(slug, event)`: `repositoryOf` → `deps.source.sync(repo)` → and, for a `push` whose `refs/heads/main` moved, `validateAndRecord(deps, project, to)`.

**The observer runs inside `sync`**, so the webhook does not publish the `pushed` event itself. It would publish it twice.

The sync in the GitHub driver parses `git fetch --porcelain` (measured while this plan was written, host git 2.50.1):
- `!` — **rejected** (a rewrite): `rewritten`, the mirror's ref untouched;
- ` ` — fast-forward, `*` — a new ref, `+` — forced (never, because the refspec has no `+`): all three go into `updated`;
- `=` — up to date: skipped.

**Exit 1 with at least one `!` line is a report, not a failure.** Exit 1 with none is `SOURCE_GIT_FAILED`. Then `await observer.advanced(advance)`, and only then any bookkeeping ref. **`localGitDir` pins every commit it hands out** (`git update-ref refs/manifest/kept/<sha> <sha>`), so neither a rewrite nor a deleted branch can ever make a built commit unreachable to `gc`. Driver 1's `sync` answers `{ updated: [], rewritten: [] }`: its repository IS the source.

`createSourceObserver({ db, bus })` resolves the slug to a project and publishes `repository.pushed` `{ ref, from, to }` for each `updated` entry, and `repository.history_rewritten` `{ ref, mirror, upstream }` for each `rewritten` one, with its `humanMessage` saying *"GitHub's history for main was rewritten; Manifest kept the commits it had"*. **Commit ids only: no author, no message text**, because a commit message is an app author's free text and §14's redaction applies to an event.

`createSerialQueue()`: one promise chain. A job that throws writes `console.error(`[source-sync] ${label} failed: ${message}`)` and the chain continues. **It is not swallowed, and it does not stop the next job.** `idle()` resolves when the chain is empty. Built once in `index.ts`, and once per `testDeps()`.

`api/unversioned.ts` gains the entry:

```ts
  {
    method: 'POST',
    path: '/webhooks/github',
    why: "GitHub's deliveries (D5 driver 2): its caller is GitHub, never a Manifest client, and its credential is the delivery's HMAC signature rather than a session or a token (§20). Reached at 127.0.0.1:7100 directly, as the edge itself reaches the control plane — never through the edge, which forwards only /v1/* and /auth/*.",
  },
```

`versioning.test.ts`'s title says *"the five it names"* while the list has seven. **Fix the title to derive the count from the list**, rather than to *eight*: a restated number drifts (ORIENTATION §9).

The fake's `webhooks.ts`:
- **When a delivery happens.** `git-http.ts` snapshots every ref before a `receive-pack` and diffs the refs after it. For each ref that changed, it sends one `push`, as GitHub does per ref.
- **What a `push` carries.** `before`, `after`, `forced` (true when `before` is not an ancestor of `after`), `created`, `deleted`, `base_ref: null`, `compare`, up to 20 `commits` (from `git log`), `head_commit`, `repository` (the webhook `Repository` shape), `pusher`, `sender` and `installation`.
- **How it is signed.** `X-Hub-Signature-256`, plus the legacy `X-Hub-Signature` (SHA-1), both over the exact bytes sent.
- **How it is sent.** POSTed to the webhook URL with a 10-second timeout, and recorded with the answer's status. `GET /_fake/deliveries` lists the log, and `POST /_fake/deliveries/{id}/redeliver` re-sends a delivery with the same id.

- [ ] **Step 5: The delivery across the real boundary — the Docker tier**

`webhook.docker.test.ts` starts the fake's IMAGE (Task 8's `startFakeContainer`, now with `FAKE_WEBHOOK_URL=http://host.docker.internal:<port>/webhooks/github`). It then starts a driver-2 control-plane SERVER on the host with `app.listen({ host: '127.0.0.1', port: 0 })`, and pushes as a person. **It asserts the delivery reached the host from the container** — the fake's log shows `202`, and the mirror has the commit. **This is Task 1 `[M7]`'s measurement made permanent**, and the only test in the repository that crosses from a platform container to the control plane's loopback port.

## Task 10: Enforced private — a repository found public is made private again, reported, and not built while public

§20: *"Repositories are private by default and enforced private. A public repository containing a course application leaks student data immediately and irreversibly."* Task 7 made repositories private at creation and refuses GitHub's answer if it is not. **This task makes the "enforced" half real** (Decision 12): on every sync, and at once on a `publicized` webhook.

**Files:**
- Modify: `packages/github-fake/src/repos.ts`, `webhooks.ts` — `PATCH` to public or private sends a `repository` event, `publicized` or `privatized`; the quirk `refusePrivatize` for tests
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/source/github/driver.ts` — `GET` the repository on each sync; revert; `manifest.visibility`; `localGitDir`'s refusal
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/source/git-driver.ts` — `MirrorAdvance.visibility`

- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/api/error-codes.ts` — `SOURCE_REPOSITORY_PUBLIC`
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/source/github/driver.test.ts`, `api/webhooks.test.ts`

- Consumes: Task 9's sync, observer and webhook route (which already queues `repository`/`publicized`).
- Produces: `MirrorAdvance.visibility: { observed: 'private' | 'public'; enforced: boolean; result: 'private' | 'still-public' } | null` (`null` when it was not read, for example offline); the event `repository.visibility_enforced { observed: 'public', result, detail }`.

- **`faculty-dev` makes the repository public** (`PATCH { private: false }` with the PAT, as a person could on GitHub), and the fake delivers `repository`/`publicized`. After `idle()`: the fake reads `private: true` again; one `repository.visibility_enforced { observed: 'public', result: 'private' }` event exists; and a build is accepted afterwards. **In the same file, the positive control is a repository nobody touched**: no event, and the sync's `GET` read `private`.
- **The same with the fake's `refusePrivatize` quirk** (the revert is refused, as a GitHub organisation policy could refuse it). The event reads `result: 'still-public'`. **`POST /v1/projects/{id}/builds` answers `409 SOURCE_REPOSITORY_PUBLIC`.** Then lift the quirk and sync again, and the same build is accepted.
- **Offline with the mirror marked `public`**: stop the fake, then `localGitDir` → `SOURCE_REPOSITORY_PUBLIC`. The last known state holds, because the build does not need the network to refuse.
- **With no webhook at all**, as for a laptop's real App: publicise, then call `headCommit`, whose sync finds and reverts it.

- [ ] **Step 2: Watch them fail. Step 3: Implement** — in `sync`, after the fetch: `GET /repos/{org}/{slug}` with `forRepository(slug, { contents: 'read' })`. `private === false` → `PATCH { private: true }` with `forRepository(slug, { administration: 'write' })` → re-read → set `manifest.visibility` in the mirror's config to what was read LAST → put the result in `advance.visibility`. **A `GET` that fails as unreachable leaves `visibility: null` and the mirror's config as it was.** `localGitDir` reads `git config manifest.visibility` and refuses `public` with `new SourceError('SOURCE_REPOSITORY_PUBLIC', …)`.

## Task 11: Push-time secret scanning — every path Manifest can see, one list of rules, and the token format the gate misses

> **`[M14]` (2026-09-24, F6): scan what moved on the SHADOW refs.** `updated` comes from
> `refs/manifest/upstream/*` (Task 9's correction), and a `+` (forced) update's new commits are scanned
> too — a rewritten history is new content. Without it, one force-push to `main` on a free organisation
> (where `main` cannot be protected, *Read this first* 8) would switch push-time scanning off for `main`
> for good. `refs/manifest/scanned/<branch>` is compared against the shadow's head. **Add the case: a
> secret pushed to `main` after a rewrite is reported.**

§20, as applied on 2026-09-24 (Spec action 1, option (a)): *a push Manifest makes is scanned before it leaves and refused; a push to a driver-1 repository is refused by that repository's own hook; a push made directly to GitHub cannot be blocked by Manifest, and its commits are scanned as soon as Manifest learns of them, a detection raising an Event naming the commit, the path and the rule — never the value — and the commit not built.* **This task builds exactly that sentence** (Decision 14). GitHub.com runs no custom pre-receive hooks, so a push made straight to GitHub cannot be blocked from here, only found. And it closes *Read this first* 4: **the stateless installation token this plan puts into circulation passes the build's own secret gate today.**

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/control-plane/src/build/secret-patterns.ts` — `SECRET_PATTERNS` (moved), two new entries, `scanText`
- Create: `packages/control-plane/src/build/secret-patterns.test.ts`
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/build/gates.ts`, `build/index.ts` — `scanForSecrets` over `scanText`
- Create: `packages/control-plane/src/source/scan-commits.ts`, `scan-commits.test.ts` — the added lines of new commits
- Create: `packages/control-plane/src/source/pre-receive.ts`, `pre-receive.test.ts` — driver 1's hook, rendered from the list; the corpus test
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/source/git-driver.ts` — `SourceDriver.prepare()`; `MirrorAdvance.findings`
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/source/local-driver.ts` — the hook at creation and at `prepare()`; the pre-push scan in `commitFiles` and `createRepository`
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/source/github/driver.ts` — the pre-push scan; scanning on every advance; `refs/manifest/scanned/<branch>`; `prepare()` re-writes each mirror's refusing hook
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/source/driver-contract.ts` — *refuses to commit a secret, and the head does not move*
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/projects/source-events.ts` — `repository.secret_detected`
- Modify: `observability/events.ts`, `event-schemas.ts`, `db/schema.ts`; create `drizzle/0027_*.sql` — the event type
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/index.ts` — `await source.prepare()` before listening; the count on the boot line
- Modify: `packages/control-plane/src/api/error-codes.ts`, `api/routes/projects.ts` (`errors:`) — `SOURCE_SECRET_DETECTED`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 9's sync and observer.
- Produces:

```ts
// build/secret-patterns.ts
export interface SecretPattern { name: string; pattern: RegExp }
export const SECRET_PATTERNS: readonly SecretPattern[]
export interface SecretFinding { path: string; line: number; rule: string }     // NEVER the matched text
export function scanText(text: string, path: string): SecretFinding[]

// source/scan-commits.ts
export interface CommitFinding extends SecretFinding { commit: string }
export async function scanNewCommits(gitDir: string, heads: readonly string[], exclude: readonly string[]): Promise<{ findings: CommitFinding[]; commits: number; truncated: boolean }>

// source/git-driver.ts — SourceDriver gains:
  /** Boot: make every repository this driver owns what it must be (hooks, protection). Idempotent. */
  prepare(): Promise<{ repositories: number }>
// MirrorAdvance gains:
  findings: CommitFinding[]
```

- [ ] **Step 1: The failing tests**

`secret-patterns.test.ts` checks four things:
- **the corpus**: every existing rule still catches its sample, and a clean file finds nothing;
- **the stateless installation token `ghs_1234567_eyJ…` IS caught**, named *a GitHub App installation token*, and so is a fine-grained PAT `github_pat_…`;
- **`scanText` never returns the matched text**: a canary secret appears in no field of any finding;
- `scanForSecrets(dir)` (the build gate) finds exactly what `scanText` finds over the same files. There is one list, and this test is what makes that true.

`pre-receive.test.ts` is **the corpus test**. It makes a real bare repository with the RENDERED hook installed. Then, for each corpus entry, it pushes a commit adding the file with real `git push` and asserts two things:
- **the push is refused exactly when `scanText` finds something**;
- **the hook's stderr names the same `path:line` and rule set**, and never the text.

It has three further cases:
- a secret **added in one commit and deleted in the next, in the same push** → refused, which is the history case a tree scan misses;
- a clean push → accepted, the positive control;
- **the hook without `node` on its PATH** refuses the push, fail-closed, and says why.

`scan-commits.test.ts` checks the scanner over git history:
- only commits not reachable from `exclude` are scanned;
- line numbers are the file's real ones after a hunk offset;
- a binary file is skipped;
- a merge commit's added lines are scanned once;
- more than 1000 new commits → `truncated: true` and the first 1000 scanned.

The contract suite gains *refuses to commit a secret, and the head does not move*: `commitFiles` with a canary AWS key → `SOURCE_SECRET_DETECTED`, the message naming `path:line` and the rule and **not** the key, and `headCommit` unchanged. `createRepository` with such a seed is refused the same way.

The GitHub driver's tests gain five cases:
- **a person pushes a commit adding a key straight to the fake** → after `sync`, `advance.findings` names it;
- the observer publishes one `repository.secret_detected` event whose payload has `commit`, `path`, `line` and `rule`, and **no field containing the key**;
- a second `sync` reports nothing new;
- the build of that commit still fails at the build gate, so a secret that GitHub has cannot be deployed;
- **the finding is reported even when no webhook arrived**, because `headCommit`'s sync found it.

- [ ] **Step 2: Watch them fail.**

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

```ts
// build/secret-patterns.ts — THE list. build/gates.ts, source/'s pre-push scan, the mirror's scan
// and driver 1's rendered hook all read it; the hook gets it serialised (source/pre-receive.ts).
export const SECRET_PATTERNS: readonly SecretPattern[] = [
  // …the seven from gates.ts, moved verbatim, in the same order…
  // D5 plan (Read this first 4): GitHub's stateless installation token, ghs_<APPID>_<JWT>, since
  // 2026-04-27. Neither rule above catches it — the GitHub rule needs 36 alphanumerics after
  // `ghs_` and meets `_`; the JWT rule's `\beyJ` has no word boundary after `_`. Measured.
  { name: 'a GitHub App installation token', pattern: /\bghs_\d+_[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}/ },
  { name: 'a GitHub fine-grained token', pattern: /\bgithub_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{22,}/ },
]

export function scanText(text: string, path: string): SecretFinding[] {
  const out: SecretFinding[] = []
  for (const [i, line] of text.split('\n').entries()) {
    for (const { name, pattern } of SECRET_PATTERNS) {
      if (!pattern.test(line)) continue
      out.push({ path, line: i + 1, rule: name })          // the match itself is NEVER kept (§14)
      break
    }
  }
  return out
}
```

**Order matters, and is kept:** `scanForSecrets` stops at the first rule per line, as `gates.ts` does today. So the two new rules go LAST, and a line holding a classic token still reads *a GitHub token*.

`scan-commits.ts`:
1. `git rev-list <heads…> --not <exclude…>` (with `--stdin` when the lists are long), capped at 1000 commits;
2. `git log -p -U0 --no-color --no-ext-diff --no-renames --diff-filter=AM --format=%x00%H --stdin` over those commits;
3. parse: `\0<sha>` starts a commit, `+++ b/<path>` a file, `@@ -a,b +c,d @@` sets the line counter to `c`, and each `+` line (never `+++`) is scanned with `scanText(line, path)` at the counter;
4. `Binary files … differ` is skipped;
5. a 20 MB cap on `maxBuffer`; exceeding it marks the scan `truncated` and scans what arrived.

`pre-receive.ts`'s `renderPreReceiveHook()` returns a `#!/usr/bin/env node` script. It holds `JSON.stringify(SECRET_PATTERNS.map(p => ({ name: p.name, source: p.pattern.source, flags: p.pattern.flags })))` inline, and about 50 lines of plain JavaScript:
1. read stdin's `<old> <new> <ref>` lines;
2. skip deletions (`new` all zeros);
3. `git rev-list <new…> --not --all`, which lists exactly the incoming commits, because git's quarantine keeps them out of `--all`;
4. the same `git log -p -U0` walk as `scan-commits.ts`;
5. on findings, print `Manifest refused this push (§20): <path>:<line> looks like <rule> in <sha12>` and one sentence of remedy — *remove it from every commit in the push, and treat the secret as exposed* — then exit 1.

**The loop exists twice, and the corpus test is what holds the two to one answer** (Decision 14). The driver writes it to `hooks/pre-receive` with mode `0755` at `createRepository`. `prepare()` re-renders it in every `<slug>.git` under the root, so a pattern added later reaches every repository at the next boot.

**The pre-push scan**, in both drivers' `commitFiles` and `createRepository`: `scanText` over every file's content **before anything is written**. Findings → `new SourceError('SOURCE_SECRET_DETECTED', <path:line — rule, for each>)`. On driver 1 the hook would refuse the push anyway. **The early scan is what makes the refusal a `409` with a code rather than a `SOURCE_GIT_FAILED` carrying git's stderr**, and it is the same on both drivers.

**The mirror's scan**, in the GitHub driver's `sync`, after the fetch: `scanNewCommits(mirror, updated.map(u => u.to), <every refs/manifest/scanned/* value>)` → `advance.findings`. **After `observer.advanced(advance)` resolves**, each `refs/manifest/scanned/<branch>` is set to that branch's head. If the observer throws, the refs do not move and the next sync reports again. **At least once, and never skipped** (Decision 11).

`repository.secret_detected`'s payload: `{ commit, findings: [{ path, line, rule }] }`, at most 50 findings with a `truncated` flag. Its `humanMessage`: *"A secret-shaped value was pushed to GitHub in <sha12> (<path>:<line>, <rule>). It is on GitHub now: treat it as exposed and rotate it. Manifest will not build a commit that carries it."* **Asserted to contain no finding's text.**

- [ ] **Step 4: Gates, owed tier, restart the control plane, and read the boot line's `prepare` count** — every repository under `.manifest/repos/` given the hook. `ls -l .manifest/repos/*/hooks/pre-receive` shows each one, mode 755. **Then a real push by hand to `launch-app`'s bare repository**, adding `AKIA` + 16 characters in a throwaway commit, is refused with the hook's sentence. Push nothing else, and leave `launch-app` as it was.

- [ ] **Step 5: Commit** (every path in **Files**, plus the migration, its `meta/` files, and the regenerated contract):

```bash
git commit -m "feat(source): push-time secret scanning on every path Manifest sees — and the stateless token the build gate missed"
```

| # | Break | Predict |
|---|---|---|
| (a) | delete the stateless-token rule | *IS caught* red, and the corpus test's stateless entry is ACCEPTED by the hook — **the measured miss, back** |
| (b) | the hook's `rev-list` becomes `<new> -1` (the tip only) | *added and deleted in the same push* red: the push is accepted |
| (c) | the hook's loop drops its `break` (all rules per line) | the corpus test red: the hook names two rules where `scanText` names one — **the two-loops-one-answer control, firing** |
| (d) | `refs/manifest/scanned/*` moved BEFORE the observer | inject an observer that throws once. Predict: the finding is lost, and *reported at least once* red |
| (e) | the pre-push scan removed from driver 1 | the contract case red with **`SOURCE_GIT_FAILED`** (the hook still refuses) — the code, not the refusal, is what the early scan buys |
| (f) | `prepare()` not called at boot | no unit test red. **Held by Step 4's `ls` and the boot line**; predict a repository created before this task has no hook |

> **`[M15]` — RICH CHOSE STRUCTURED OUTPUT, 2026-09-24 (F7; Decisions 19 and 22). THIS TASK WAS REWRITTEN FOR

**P6b sitting 7's F9, with its mechanism measured** (*Read this first* 13; Decision 19): all the failures came after the instruction to state the reviewer's verdict, over a free-text answer. A GitHub push is exactly the path that will put an unreviewed `manifest.yaml` change in front of this summary, which is why the inheritance is this plan's. **F13** is a sentence in `launch/readiness.ts` that names the wrong source. **F14** is a PUID where a person's name belongs.

**THE MODEL IS GIVEN FACTS AND FILLS A SCHEMA; THE PLATFORM WRITES EVERYTHING ELSE.** The user message is JSON: the changes (`path`, `from`, `to`, `describeDiff`'s own `summary`) and the security notes. **No verdict, no coverage sentence.** The answer is `{ changes: [{ path, exposure }] }`, `path` an **enum of this diff's own paths** and the array exactly as long as the diff. There is no field a verdict could go in, so F9 stops being catchable and becomes impossible to express in the shape — the per-sentence check below is the backstop, not the control.

```ts
// releases/summary.ts
export type SummarySource = 'llm' | 'unavailable' | 'no-previous-release' | 'no-changes' | 'withheld'
export interface Exposure { path: string; sentence: string }
export interface ChangeSummary {
  summary: string | null                 // the sentences joined, in the diff's order — for every reader that wants one string
  summarySource: SummarySource
  exposures?: Exposure[] | null          // what the console lays out; null unless summarySource is 'llm'
  summaryWithheldBecause?: string
}
/** The answer's shape for THIS diff: exactly one `{ path, exposure }` per change, `path` from the diff. */
export function exposureSchema(changes: readonly SpecChange[]): z.ZodType<{ changes: { path: string; exposure: string }[] }>
/** The rule one sentence broke, or null. Deterministic; applied before anything is stored. */
export function checkExposure(sentence: string): string | null
// releases/approval.ts
export async function latestReviewFor(db: Db, releaseId: string): Promise<{ review: DiffSnapshot['review']; from: 'preview' | 'approval'; at: Date } | undefined>
```

`F9.inventedVerdict` is kept from P6b's measured sentences (*Read this first* 13). `modelAnswering(text)` and `captureRequests()` are `LiteLlmClient` fakes of the shape `approval.test.ts` already uses — **read that file and reuse its helper rather than writing a third** (a helper copied three times drifts three ways). F13, in `readiness.test.ts`: a verdict that came from a PREVIEW reads *"…recorded when this release was previewed"*, one from a decision *"…when an administrator decided on this release"* — **both cases, in the same test**. F14, in `approval.test.ts`: the `release.approved` event's `humanMessage` begins with the approver's **display name** and does not contain their PUID. A rejection's does the same.

- [ ] **Step 3: Watch them fail.**

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement**

```ts
// releases/summary.ts
/**
 * THE MODEL DESCRIBES ONE THING; THE RECORD SAYS EVERYTHING ELSE (the D5 plan's Decisions 19 and 22,
 * on P6b's F9). Measured 2026-09-24: asked for prose and told to "state the code reviewer's verdict
 * exactly as given", the model invented or misattributed a verdict 6 times in 20; a regex over the
 * prose then withheld 14 good answers in 20. So the model is handed FACTS as JSON and fills a schema
 * with one `exposure` sentence per change — there is no field a verdict could go in. The verdict and
 * the coverage limit are the snapshot's own fields, which the console shows beside this. R4(d) (Rich)
 * is unchanged: the SNAPSHOT surfaces the verdict.
 */
const SYSTEM_PROMPT =
  'You explain configuration changes to a platform administrator. For EACH change in the input, write one ' +
  'plain-English sentence saying what that change could expose: personal information, where data can go, or ' +
  'what the app can reach. Use only the facts in the input. Answer with JSON matching the schema.'

export function exposureSchema(changes: readonly SpecChange[]) {
  const paths = changes.map((c) => c.path) as [string, ...string[]]   // describeDiff never returns an empty list here
  return z.strictObject({
    changes: z
      .array(z.strictObject({ path: z.enum(paths), exposure: z.string().min(10).max(300) }))
      .length(paths.length),
  })
}

/** Withheld, not repaired: the answer's own words decided something, which is never the model's to do. */
export function checkExposure(sentence: string): string | null {
  // NOT "blocked" (F7: it is the word for egress that was denied) and NOT Markdown (stripped below).
  return /\b(verdict|approv\w*|reject\w*|decid\w*|decision|recommend\w*)\b/i.test(sentence)
    ? 'a sentence states or suggests a decision, a verdict or an approval, which is the administrator’s and the record’s, never the model’s'
    : null
}
```

In `summariseChanges`: the body gains `response_format: { type: 'json_schema', json_schema: { name: 'change_exposures', strict: true, schema: z.toJSONSchema(exposureSchema(changes)) } }` (adjusted by Step 1), and `max_tokens` becomes `80 + 90 * changes.length` — a truncated answer is invalid JSON, and the old flat 200 would cut a large diff short. The user message is `JSON.stringify({ changes, securityNotes: context.security.map(({ field, note }) => ({ path: field, note })) })`. **It drops `Code review:` and `context.coverage`.** Then, in order: `JSON.parse` → `exposureSchema(changes).safeParse` → **each path exactly once** (a schema cannot say "unique" for objects) → `checkExposure` on every sentence → strip `` ` `` and `*` from each sentence (safe now: a decision word was refused first, so stripping cannot turn `**Verdict:**` into a verdict that passes) → `{ summarySource: 'llm', exposures: in the diff's order, summary: sentences.join(' ') }`. **Any failure is `{ summary: null, summarySource: 'withheld', exposures: null, summaryWithheldBecause }`**, the reason naming the rule and, for a schema failure, Zod's first issue path — **never the model's text**, because an app's own words can be in it. The operator gets one `console.error` line with the same reason. **A model or provider that ignores `response_format`** — LiteLLM's `drop_params: true` drops what a provider cannot take — answers prose, which fails the parse and is withheld: **the failure is loud and safe, never a summary nobody constrained.** `SummaryContext` keeps `review` and `coverage`, **because the snapshot still stores them**; only the model stops receiving them.

`latestReviewFor` already reads both sources (`releases/approval.ts:389-401`), and now also returns which one was newest and when. `readiness.ts:288` builds its sentence from `from`. **`approval.ts:99`'s `humanMessage`** reads `users.display_name` for `input.actor.userId` (P6b's Decision 18 already joins it for `decidedByName`, and the event uses the same read), falling back to the PUID only if the name is empty. So the feed reads *"Test Operator approved this release for production."*

`approvals.tsx` (its change list is at line ~290, `path: from → to — summary`): under each change line, that path's `sentence`, labelled once above the list as **"What each change could expose — written by a language model; the change lines are the record."** The deterministic line stays FIRST (the measured residual is a paraphrase that gets a fact wrong — *"the full name attribute"* for `sn`). A `withheld` summary renders **"The model's summary was withheld: <because>. What changed, the security notes and the reviewer's verdict below are the record."** in the same place, so an administrator never meets an empty space. A record written before this task has no `exposures`: render its `summary` string as today. The mock gains `summaryExposures` on its previews and one withheld preview, and `validate.test.ts` holds them to the document.

- [ ] **Step 5: The real model, re-measured.** Run `probes/structured.mjs 20` against the schema `summary.ts` now builds (Step 1's variant), **and a third change** (a `resources` change) so the enum and the count are not always two. **Record how many parse, match, pass `checkExposure`, and how long each took.** **If more than 5 of 20 are withheld, stop before committing and bring it to Rich** — the threshold Task 1 set, carried over: a summary withheld more often than shown is a decision about whether to keep the feature, and that is his.

**P6b sitting 7's console findings**, each small, each a person's confusion rather than a leak. **There is no DOM test tier** (P5c decided, with its cost stated), so each behaviour is a **pure function the screen calls**, tested in the `packages` project, and the screen is clicked in sitting 7 and again in sitting 8.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/console/src/approval-state.ts`, `approval-state.test.ts` — `canDecide`, `reasonDraft`, `approvalLinkWanted`
- Modify: `packages/console/src/screens/approvals.tsx` — F11 and F12
- Modify: `packages/console/src/screens/launch.tsx:140-148` — F15
- Modify: `packages/mock/src/fixtures.ts` — a launched, self-serve checklist, if none exists

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Schemas['Approval']` (with `previewId`), `Schemas['ApprovalPreview']`, `Schemas['LaunchReadiness']` (`launched`, `reescalated`, the `admin-approval` item).
- Produces:

```ts
/** F11: once this screen has recorded a decision, the buttons wait for a NEW preview. */
export function canDecide(s: { preview: { id: string } | undefined; decidedWithPreview: string | undefined; busy: boolean }): boolean
/** F12: a rejection reason typed before a step-up, kept in sessionStorage by release — never in the URL. */
export function reasonDraft(storage: Pick<Storage, 'getItem' | 'setItem' | 'removeItem'> | undefined, releaseId: string): { read(): string; write(v: string): void; clear(): void }
/** F15: link to the approval only when one is required, or exists. */

```

- **`false` once a decision was recorded with that preview**;
- `true` again with a NEW preview;
- `false` while `busy`.

`reasonDraft`:
- `write` then `read` round-trips under the key `manifest.approval-reason:<releaseId>`;
- `clear` removes it;
- **a storage that throws** — a private window, blocked site data — reads `''` and writes nothing, without throwing;
- `undefined` storage behaves the same.

`approvalLinkWanted`:

- `true` for a launched app with `reescalated: true`;
- **`false` for a launched app whose `admin-approval` is met with no approval**, which is exactly F15's case;

## Task 15: The acceptance — `make demo-github`, against a control plane on driver 2, offline

**ALONE, AND LAST.** It drives driver 2's whole delivery path through `@manifest/contract`: a project whose code is on (fake) GitHub, private; a person's push that reaches Manifest by a signed webhook, is validated and is built from the mirror; a build with GitHub gone; a repository made public and made private again; a secret pushed straight to GitHub and found; a force-push refused; and the webhook's four refusals. It is **green three times**, it is a step of the offline acceptance and of `ci-acceptance`, and it is **clicked by a person**. Then `make github-conformance` runs at Rich's yes, and **`make demo` runs on driver 1 with no GitHub at all** (C1).

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/journey/src/github.ts` — the phases, through `@manifest/contract` plus raw `fetch` for the two things outside the contract (the webhook route, and the fake itself)
- Create: `scripts/demo-github.sh` — sign-ins (`idp_login`), git pushes as `faculty-dev` (through `GIT_CONFIG_*`, never a URL), `docker stop`/`start` of the fake
- Modify: `scripts/lib/api.sh` — `clear_orphan_repository` also removes an orphan's repository on the fake (with `faculty-dev`'s token, `DELETE /repos/{org}/{slug}`, as a person can) and its mirror
- Modify: `Makefile` — `demo-github: github-up`
- Modify: `scripts/offline-acceptance.sh` — step 13; `scripts/ci-acceptance.sh` — the step, reporting `NOT RUN` on driver 1
- Modify: `docs/superpowers/RUNBOOK.md` — *`make demo-github`*, with the restart commands for both drivers; `docs/superpowers/WALKTHROUGH.md` — what to open, and what the repository line says

**Interfaces:** consumes everything above. Produces nothing later tasks use: this is the last one.

- [ ] **Step 1: The phases, and what each asserts — the shape of every answer, and every refusal's code**

| Step | What happens | Asserted |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | An UNSIGNED `POST /webhooks/github` (from TypeScript: `api.sh` cannot send a bodyless POST, §4); the fake's `/_fake/health` | `401 WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MISSING` = driver 2, proceed. **`404 WEBHOOKS_NOT_CONFIGURED` = driver 1: print the restart command and stop, CREATING NOTHING** (Decision 17). Anything else: stop, naming it |
| 1 | Sign in as `instructor` (bash, `idp_login`) | the session reads `ins000001` |
| 2 | `clear_orphan_repository github-app` | a slug no project holds loses its fake repository and its mirror; a held one is left |
| 3 | Create `github-app` from a starter, or re-use it | `repository` is `{ provider: 'github', fullName: 'manifest-apps/github-app', webUrl: 'http://127.0.0.1:7110/manifest-apps/github-app', mainProtected: true, protectionDetail: null }`; **the fake, asked with `faculty-dev`'s token, says `private: true`** |
| 4 | `faculty-dev` pushes a commit changing the app's page text straight to the fake | within 30 s: a `repository.pushed` event for that commit on the project's stream, and the project's newest validation is of that commit (read through the contract) |
| 5 | Build that commit; release; deploy to staging | `succeeded`; `healthy`; the app's URL answers with `X-Manifest-Instance` equal to the new instance, **and the body is the pushed page text** — the shape, not a `200` (the edge's wildcard answers `200` for anything) |
| 6 | `docker stop manifest-github-fake`; build the SAME commit again; `POST …/spec` with no commit; `docker start` | the build `succeeded`, from the mirror (C1); the validation **`503 SOURCE_UNREACHABLE`**; health back within 30 s |
| 7 | `faculty-dev` makes the repository public (`PATCH { private: false }`) | within 30 s the fake reads `private: true` again, and a `repository.visibility_enforced { observed: 'public', result: 'private' }` event exists |
| 8 | `faculty-dev` pushes a file holding an AWS-key-shaped value, and a second holding a stateless-format installation token (built at run time, never a real one) | one `repository.secret_detected` event naming both paths and the rules *an AWS access key id* and *a GitHub App installation token*, **its JSON containing neither value**; then a build of that commit **`failed`**, its reason naming the gate. **Then `faculty-dev` pushes their removal** |
| 9 | `faculty-dev` force-pushes a rewritten `main` | git refuses, printing `GH006` (team plan) |
| 10 | The webhook's refusals, each beside step 4's accepted one: SHA-1 only; malformed; wrong; the fake's REDELIVERY of step 4's delivery | `401 WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MISSING`; `401 WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_MALFORMED`; `401 WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_INVALID`; `200 { duplicate: true }` **and still exactly one `repository.pushed` event for step 4's commit** |

**The re-use path** (`github-app` exists) skips step 3's creation and asserts the same `repository` read. Every other step runs again, and each makes a new commit, so no step depends on the last run. **The control plane's own commit carrying a secret is NOT in this demo**: nothing in the contract commits files before the authoring API. It is held by the contract suite on both drivers (Task 11) — **named here as the breadth this acceptance does not cover**.

Each run's time and check count go in the record.

## What this plan does not build

**Named so the next plan inherits a list rather than a surprise.**

- **The authoring API's write surface** (the next plan). This plan gives `commitFiles` its second implementation and its pre-push secret scan. **Nothing in the contract commits a file yet**, so the pre-push scan's only callers are project creation's seed and the tests. The authoring API is what makes it load-bearing, and it should add the Event a refused commit raises (`repository.secret_refused`), which this plan deliberately does not define with no publisher.
- **D14's sandbox git credential** (§20's fifth bullet). A sandbox is S5's. What this plan makes true is that an installation token never leaves the control plane (Decision 20). A branch-scoped credential must be issued by the control plane and forwarded under its own token, since GitHub cannot scope an installation token to a branch.
- **Production's webhook ingress.** GitHub delivers to a PUBLIC URL. Locally the fake calls `host.docker.internal:7100`, and the laptop's real App has no webhook at all. How UBC's edge exposes `/webhooks/github`, and only that path, is Phase 5's topology question (§19). **The route is written so that the only thing it trusts is the signature.**
- **Retention of `webhook_deliveries`.** One row per delivery, never pruned. Harmless on a laptop, and a table that grows for ever on a server.
- **GitHub push protection on UBC's organisation** — the only thing that can BLOCK a direct push on GitHub.com — and **the organisation's own policies**: members may not make repositories public or create public ones. Both are UBC's, and both are **item 10 of the external track** (`docs/external-track.md`), added on 2026-09-24 when Spec action 1 was applied as option (a).
- **Webhook conformance against real GitHub.** A laptop has no public URL. The signing is checked by GitHub's published test vector, and the payload shapes by GitHub's own schemas. **Whether GitHub re-uses a delivery id on redelivery is unmeasured** (Decision 10).
- **A production launch on driver 2.** The acceptance deploys to staging (Decision 17's stated trade). `releases/`, `launch/` and `spec/` import nothing from `source/`.
- **Every other demo on driver 2.** `make demo-releases` and `make demo-production` run on a driver-1 project (`launch-app`), and a driver-2 control plane refuses it by design (Decision 3).
- **Moving a project between drivers.** A project keeps the provider it was created with. A migration from laptop repositories to an organisation is a tool nobody has asked for.
- **Master-key and App-key custody on UBC infrastructure** (Vault/KMS, §20). The loaders are the one place it changes.
- **P6b's list, still open:** binding the manifest's `blueprint:` to the project's pin; `startBuild`'s pairing of `body.commitSha` with the newest spec (**sharper now**, because a webhook records a validation for every push to `main`); D16's *every environment*; database-level non-repudiation for `approvals` and `approval_previews`; a real `Reviewer`; a run against real UBC Shibboleth for a changed registration; a rollback operation; per-instance egress proxies; `make verify`'s runtime-route meter on the public listener; a route that deletes a project; IAM change-request generation (P8); certificate expiry alerts (D20).

---

## Spec actions

**THREE — ALL APPROVED BY RICH AND APPLIED ON 2026-09-24**, the day this plan was written, each in the exact wording below, which he read first: action 1 as option (a), action 2 as written, and action 3 *"unless the conformance run says otherwise"*. **Found while applying:** action 1's approved wording ends *"which the production organisation enables (§19)"*, and §19 has no row for the production GitHub organisation, so the pointer points at nothing. That was this plan's drafting miss. **The wording was applied as approved**, and a one-row §19 addition is raised with Rich separately (ORIENTATION §8, *Open*) rather than applied unread. Every spec change this project has made was approved by Rich first, and applied only after he had read the words. **A spec action is not finished when the spec changes**: the four shared HTML pages restate §20 and §21 in plain language (`manifest-decisions.html` above all) and are swept with it.

### 1. §20 *Git driver*, bullet 4 — what "a detected secret blocks the push" can mean on GitHub.com — ✅ **RICH CHOSE (a), 2026-09-24; APPLIED**

It currently reads:

> - Push-time secret scanning on both drivers; a detected secret blocks the push and raises an Event.

**Why it cannot stay as written:** GitHub.com runs no custom pre-receive hooks (they are GitHub Enterprise Server's), and a webhook fires after a push has landed. So on driver 2 nothing Manifest runs can block a push made directly to GitHub. The only thing that can is **GitHub's own push protection**. On a private repository that needs **GitHub Secret Protection**, a paid product bought on a Team or Enterprise plan (GitHub's documentation, read 2026-09-24), and custom patterns — Manifest's rules — need the same.

| Option | What it means | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| **(a) Recommended: say what each path does** | The wording below. Manifest blocks every push that passes through it, blocks every push to driver 1, and FINDS every commit pushed straight to GitHub, raising an Event and refusing to build it | The sentence stops promising a block that GitHub.com cannot give Manifest. **Blocking a direct push becomes an external-track item** — push protection on UBC's organisation |
| (b) Keep the sentence, and make it true | Driver 2 requires GitHub Secret Protection on the organisation, with Manifest's rules as custom patterns kept in step with `build/secret-patterns.ts`, and the platform refuses to run driver 2 without it | A paid licence per active committer as a precondition of driver 2, a second copy of the rules in GitHub's format, and **no way to test it on a free organisation** — so the laptop's acceptance could never prove it |

**Proposed wording for (a):**

> - Push-time secret scanning on both drivers, from one set of rules. **A push Manifest makes** — its own commits, and in time a sandbox's — is scanned before it leaves and refused if it carries a secret. **A push to a driver-1 repository** is refused by that repository's own hook. **A push made directly to GitHub cannot be blocked by Manifest** (GitHub.com runs no custom pre-receive hooks): its commits are scanned as soon as Manifest learns of them, by webhook or fetch, a detection raises an Event naming the commit, the path and the rule — never the value — and the commit is not built. The secret is then on GitHub and must be treated as exposed. Blocking such a push at GitHub is GitHub's push protection, which the production organisation enables (§19).

*Had Rich chosen (b), Task 11 would be unchanged, with a precondition check in Task 8's boot and a custom-pattern export in Task 11, both untestable locally. He chose (a), so neither is built.*

### 2. §21 *Platform inventory* — the GitHub fake, in one row — ✅ **APPROVED AND APPLIED 2026-09-24**

The inventory says *"Nine long-running containers"* and *"Git uses the local driver (bare repositories on disk), so it needs no container."* **Both stay true for the default platform**, because the fake is behind a profile. But the inventory is where a reader looks for what runs on 7110. **Proposed** — one row after *Egress proxy*, and one sentence after the table's *"Git uses the local driver…"*:

> | GitHub fake | 7110 | **Opt-in** (`make github-up`, compose profile `github`) — a GitHub-compatible test double for D5's driver 2: App installation tokens, private repositories, git over HTTP and HMAC-signed webhooks. **It is not GitHub**: its answers are held to GitHub's own published schemas and test vector, and checked against a real GitHub App by an opt-in conformance run. |

> …so it needs no container. **Driver 2 runs against the GitHub fake above**, which only the driver-2 acceptance starts.

### 3. §20 *Git driver*, bullet 1 — the one installation token that cannot be scoped to a repository — ✅ **APPROVED 2026-09-24 "unless the conformance run says otherwise"; APPLIED**

It reads *"installation tokens are short-lived and scoped per repository."* **Creating a repository needs a token that names no repository**, because the repository does not exist yet. GitHub's API takes `repositories` only for repositories the installation already has (description 1.1.4). **Proposed:**

> - GitHub App private key held in the same custody class as the master key; installation tokens are short-lived and scoped per repository — **except the one that creates a repository, which cannot name it yet: it carries the administration permission alone, is used for that one call, and is never kept.**

*Rich's approval is CONDITIONAL on Task 6's real leg (steps C5–C8). If it measures that GitHub accepts a not-yet-existing name, or scopes creation some other way, the applied sentence is wrong: **bring the measurement to Rich with the reverting edit, and do not revert it unasked.** Decision 4's exception then goes with it.*

**Not proposed, deliberately:**

- **Enforced private** (§20, bullet 3). Created private, verified private, made private again and never built public (Decision 12) is what *"enforced"* can mean from outside GitHub. The window between a person's flip and the revert is already what §20's *"immediately and irreversibly"* describes. The preventive control is the organisation's policy, which goes on the external track rather than into the spec.
- **`source_repositories` and `webhook_deliveries`.** §6 lists key fields, and P6a and P6b added tables without a §6 action.
- **The webhook's route outside the edge** (Decision 8). §21 already says the control plane is reached on the console's origin through the edge *by clients*; GitHub is not a client, and the route is on the unversioned list with that reason.
- **D5's row.** *"Driver 2 is a UBC GitHub org"* is the production mapping, and it stays true. The laptop's App is on a developer's own organisation by the roadmap's finding 3, which D5 does not contradict.

---

## What the self-review caught

*Run against the spec and the code with fresh eyes after the plan was written, as `superpowers:writing-plans` prescribes, and by checking claims rather than re-reading prose (ORIENTATION §6). The items are listed where they were fixed.*

1. **Task 3 would have registered two codes the registry refuses.** `SecretError` lives in `secrets/envelope.ts:66`, not `store.ts`, and is NOT one of `error-codes.test.ts`'s wire classes. That test's reverse check (*registers nothing the source never throws*) would have gone red on both `SECRET_*` codes. Fixed: they are boot-time refusals, unregistered, and the App key's code is `SECRET_GITHUB_APP_KEY_PERMISSIONS` rather than a `CONFIG_*` code thrown by the wrong class.
2. **Task 1's pattern probe would have read four patterns out of seven** — four of `gates.ts`'s entries put `pattern:` on the line after `name:`. Its regex was rewritten to match across lines, **run against the real file** (7 of 7), and the probe now refuses to report unless it reads seven.
3. **Task 9's refusals would all have reached the wire as `UNAUTHENTICATED`.** `api/server.ts`'s error handler rewrites any error with `statusCode` 401 into the generic body, so the route REPLIES its four refusals (Task 9, Step 1, and control (d) witnesses it).
4. **This plan's own mirror probe measured the wrong thing first.** A rewrite the mirror had never fetched is a fast-forward from the mirror's side, and git correctly accepted it. The probe was re-run with the mirror synced before the rewrite, and that is the measurement *Read this first* 12 and Task 9 cite.
5. **Task 7's first `sync` used `--prune`**, which would drop the ref of a branch deleted on GitHub and let `gc` take a built commit. Removed, and Task 9 pins every commit it hands out (`refs/manifest/kept/<sha>`). **And the sentence first written to justify it claimed a measurement that was never made**; it now says *read, not measured*.
6. **Task 13's check, shipped alone, would withhold every summary.** Verified by running it over the 2026-09-24 sample: under the current prompt every answer states the verdict, as it was told to. The prompt and the check are one change, and Task 13 says so at its head.
7. **Task 3's control (a) would have loosened Rich's real master key** to watch a boot refuse. It now uses a copy in the scratchpad.
8. **The conformance run was called "thirteen steps" in three places, and its table has fourteen.** Recounted from the table and fixed everywhere.
9. **Task 7's `pushAsPerson` was a comment**, with the contract suite depending on it. It is written out, with its imports.
10. **Task 9's Files list and commit omitted `secrets/custody.ts`**, whose code union the webhook secret's custody check extends. Added to both.
11. **Five line pointers were wrong, by one to three lines**: `Caddyfile:67` (the `@api` matcher is line 68), `seed.sh:13` (the build is line 14), `error-codes.test.ts:83` and `:64-70` (86, and 66–71), and `api/errors.ts:640-648` (639–650). **Each was re-opened with `grep -n` and corrected.** A wrong pointer in a plan is inherited and multiplied (ORIENTATION §6).
12. **Four names disagreed across tasks.** `startFake`'s `quirks` option, added by Tasks 7 and 10, was missing from *The fixtures*' signature. Task 4's Files omitted `boundary.test.ts`, which its commit staged. `startFakeContainer` was called by Tasks 8 and 9 and never typed (Task 8's Interfaces now define `FakeContainer`). And Task 9's REQUIRED observer breaks every place a driver is built, which Task 9 now lists.
13. **Spec coverage.**
    - §20 *Git driver*: bullet 1 (custody, scoped short-lived tokens) is Tasks 3 and 7, with Spec action 3 for its one exception. Bullet 2 (HMAC before any processing) is Task 9, its order asserted by a test. Bullet 3 (private by default and enforced) is Tasks 7 and 10. Bullet 4 (push-time scanning) is Task 11, with Spec action 1. **Bullet 5 (the sandbox's credential) has no task**, named in *What this plan does not build*, with Decision 20 building what makes it possible.
    - D5 is Tasks 2, 7 and 8. D14 is Decision 20.
    - §12's offline secret gate is kept (Task 11 adds rules to the same list). §13's *a release's commit stays buildable* is Decisions 1, 13 and 18 and the `kept` refs. §21's inventory is Task 5, with Spec action 2. C1 is Decision 18, Task 8's Docker test and Task 15's `make demo` on driver 1.
14. **Review Focus**: each of its five lines has a named test in the task that owns the code — Tasks 7, 8 and 9.

## What executing this plan found

**THE FINDINGS COUNT FOR EVERY SITTING LIVES IN ONE PLACE: the roadmap's defect-rate table**, where it is used for arithmetic (Rich, 2026-09-20). These headings deliberately state no number. To count a sitting's findings at its CLOSE, after the post-sweep check and the final gate run:

```bash
# The alternation is load-bearing: a sitting that numbers its findings in a list (`1. **F1 …`)
# reads as ZERO to a bare `^\*\*F` grep.
awk '/^### Sitting 2 —/,/^### Sitting 3 —/' docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-24-d5-github-source-driver.md \
  | grep -cE '^[[:space:]]*([0-9]+\. )?\*\*F[0-9]+ '
```

**The honest prior, from the defect-rate table: 9.8 findings per task (P6a), 8.6 (P5a), 8.9 (P5b), 7.6 (P5c). It has never fallen with practice.** At fifteen tasks that is **roughly 115–150 findings.** This plan runs a second source driver, a webhook and a git server for the first time on this platform. **This project's worst discoveries have all arrived at a first.** Treat this plan as a hypothesis.

*One dated section per sitting, added as it runs.*

### Sitting 1 — Task 1, the measurements, alone and first — 2026-09-24

**EVERY PREMISE THIS PLAN WAS WRITTEN AGAINST HOLDS, AND DRIVING THEM FOUND THAT ITS OWN HISTORY-KEEPING
RULE FREEZES `main` AFTER ONE REWRITE.** `[M3]`, `[M6]`, `[M8]`, `[M9]`, `[M10]`, `[M12]`, `[M14]`,
`[M16]` and `[M18]` measured exactly as *Read this first* predicted; `[M4]`, `[M5]`, `[M7]`, `[M11]`,
`[M13]` and `[M17]` held in substance with a correction each; `[M15]` reproduced F9 and crossed Task 13's
own threshold; `[M19]` is written down as unmeasured, by design. **The one the plan did not know is F6**:
after a single rewrite of GitHub's `main`, every later non-forced fetch reports the same refusal, so a
normal push is never an update — never scanned, validated or reported — and `headCommit` answers a stale
head for ever. The fix is measured and lands in Tasks 7, 9 and 11. **No task boundary moved; Rich's eight
sittings stand.** Every raw answer is in [`spikes/d5-baseline/`](../spikes/d5-baseline/README.md), one
section per measurement, with the untrimmed output beside it; the `[M<n>]` blocks at the top of Tasks 2, 4,
5, 7, 8, 9, 11 and 13 carry the corrections.

#### The decisions this sitting made

**1. The network was used, at Rich's yes, asked once at the start** — for `[M3]`, `[M10]` and `[M4]`'s
`apk add`. So `github-schemas.json` is committed now, and Task 4 needs no network window for it.

**2. F6's fix is two refspecs in one fetch, not one forced refspec.** `refs/heads/*` stays non-forced — the
history Decision 1 keeps, belt and braces beside Task 9's `refs/manifest/kept/<sha>` — and
`+refs/heads/*:refs/manifest/upstream/*` carries what GitHub has now, which every read of "now" uses.
*Rejected:* **forcing `refs/heads/*` and relying on the kept pins alone**, which is simpler but reverses
Decision 1 on the strength of one sitting's measurement; **refetching by sha on demand**, which leaves the
scan and the events reading a frozen ref. *Changing course* is one refspec.

**3. `[M18]` linked Task 4's EXACT devDependencies** — `ajv` and `ajv-formats` — not the plan's `ajv`
alone, because F4 made `ajv-formats` load-bearing and it is the one a real link would add.

**4. F7 is not decided here.** The plan's own rule sends it to Rich in sitting 7, before Task 13 is built;
the options and a recommendation are at the top of Task 13, measured over twenty answers per prompt.

**5. `[M7]`'s edge claim got a control the plan did not have** — a throwaway listener on 7104 for two
requests (F9) — because the container test the plan wrote cannot fail.

**6. The model warmed for `[M15]` was unloaded at close** (`keep_alive: 0`): §7e handed this sitting a
machine with no model resident.

#### The findings

**F1 — FASTIFY IS 5.12.3, NOT THE PLAN'S "5.12.2 (INSTALLED)".** The only `fastify@` in the store, what
the control plane links, and what its `package.json` asks for (`^5.12.3`); the lockfile names no 5.12.2.
The plan's number has a source: fastify 5.12.3's own `fastify.js` says `const VERSION = '5.12.2'`, so
`app.version` reads 5.12.2. And the plan's `[M2]` version command fails `node: bad option: --prefix`
(exit 9); only its fallback answers. **The header is corrected.**

**F2 — A BARE REPOSITORY'S `HEAD` IS `master`, AND A CLONE OF IT "SUCCEEDS" EMPTY.** `[M4]`'s first run:
under `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` no `init.defaultBranch` applies; the push to `main` landed and the clone
exited 0 with *"remote HEAD refers to nonexistent ref"* — 0 commits. Alpine's git (the fake's) does the
same by default. **Task 4: `git init --bare -b main`, and assert the clone's content, not its exit code.**

**F3 — AN INHERITED TRACE VARIABLE WRITES THE TOKEN TO A FILE.** Task 7's `gitWithToken` spreads
`process.env`. With `GIT_TRACE_CURL=<file>` and `GIT_TRACE_REDACT=0` inherited, the token's base64 is
written to that file — no message redactor reaches it; with `GIT_TRACE_REDACT=0` and a stderr trace it
reaches stderr too, where Task 7's redactor does catch it. Forcing `GIT_TRACE_REDACT=1` after the spread
writes `<redacted>` (`probes/leak-trace-file.mjs`). **Task 7, correction (1).**

**F4 — AJV CANNOT COMPILE TWO OF GITHUB'S SCHEMAS, AND ENFORCES NO FORMAT WITHOUT `ajv-formats`.**
*"nullable" cannot be used without "type"* for `installation` and `webhook-push`: GitHub writes `nullable`
beside `anyOf`/`oneOf` at exactly two nodes. Task 4 validates the first and Task 9 the second, so
`expectGitHubShape` would have thrown on first use. 838 *unknown format* warnings without `ajv-formats`.
A load-time normaliser plus `ajv-formats`: 10 of 10 compile, 0 warnings, `null` and formats enforced
(`probes/normalise.mjs`). **Task 4, correction (2).**

**F5 — A BARE `compose down` EXITS 0 AND LEAVES THE PROFILED SERVICE RUNNING.** The network is reported
*"Resource is still in use"*, and the profiled container keeps running (docker compose 5.4.0). **Task 5:
`make down` AND `make reset`'s own `$(COMPOSE) down` name the profile**, since `reset`'s `docker volume rm
-f … || true` would fail just as silently; assert on the container, never the exit code.

**F6 — ONE REWRITE OF GITHUB'S `main` FREEZES THE MIRROR'S `main`, AND EVERYTHING DOWNSTREAM OF IT.**
`[M14]` measured one fetch after a rewrite; its addendum measured the next. `! <c1> <Y> refs/heads/main`,
exit 1, for a NORMAL push Y — and on every sync after. Read against the plan: Task 9 would publish
`history_rewritten` on every push and never `pushed`, and never validate `manifest.yaml` again; Task 11
would never scan another commit on `main` (one force-push on a free organisation, where `main` cannot be
protected, would switch scanning off for good); Task 7's `headCommit` would answer the frozen `main` as
current (Decision 18's forbidden stale head) and `commitFiles` would be refused `SOURCE_CONFLICT` for
ever; and Y sits in the object store unreferenced. **Fixed by a forced shadow refspec, measured**
(`probes/mirror-shadow.sh`: `+` once for the rewrite, a fast-forward for Y, nothing when quiet). **Tasks
7 (2), 9 and 11**, each with a case red against the plan as first written.

**F7 — TASK 13'S CHECK WITHHOLDS 7 SUMMARIES IN 10 UNDER TASK 13'S OWN PROMPT, AND NONE OF THEM IS A
VERDICT.** Twenty answers per prompt, every input read from source. Current prompt: 20 of 20 state "the
verdict", **6 of 20 attribute one to the coverage sentence or to an administrator** — F9 reproduced, 3 in
10 twice, as the plan measured. Task 13's prompt: **0 of 20 attribute a verdict**, and `checkSummary`
withholds **14 of 20** — 11 for backticks round a hostname or `sn`, 3 for *"previously blocked"*
describing egress. Without those two rules: 0 of 20 withheld, and all six inventions still caught. Over
the plan's own 5-in-10 line. **Rich's, in sitting 7, with the options at the top of Task 13.** *(The
plan's "4 of 10 with Markdown" is now backticks in 20 of 20.)*

**F8 — THE CONTRACT FIRST MOVES IN TASK 2, NOT TASK 8.** Every route's `errors:` is printed into its
operation's `default` description (`document.ts:169`), and Task 2 adds `SOURCE_COMMIT_NOT_FOUND` to
`startBuild`'s. **Task 2 takes the `1.2.0` bump** — its own note provides for it — and must also stage
`document.ts` and `packages/contract/package.json`, which its commit step does not name. **Tasks 2 and
8 and Decision 15.**

**F9 — `[M7]`'s EDGE CONTROL, AS THE PLAN WROTE IT, COULD NOT FAIL.** A container asking the console site
for `/webhooks/github` is refused by `@outside` before the path is read — and so is `/v1/me`, which IS
forwarded. The body proves the refusal, not the routing. **The routing was measured from the host with a
throwaway listener on 7104**: `/webhooks/*` reached the CONSOLE's catch-all (the listener logged the
POST), `/v1/*` the control plane. Decision 8 holds; the evidence for it is now the right evidence.

**F10 — AN APP NETWORK FAILS `curl` AT EXIT 28, NOT THE PREDICTED 6.** `Resolving timed out after 5002
ms`: the name never resolves, and that network's DNS times out rather than answering NXDOMAIN. Same
property, different code — a test that asserts exit 6 would be red for the wrong reason.

**F11 — SIX OF THIS SITTING'S OWN PROBES MEASURED THE WRONG THING ON THEIR FIRST RUN**: `[M4]`'s `HEAD`
(F2), `[M8]`'s positive control (a `||` fallback made it true whatever the guard said), `[M13]`'s exit
status (read through a pipe — `tail`'s, not compose's), `[M14]`'s addendum (the amend put `-c` after
`commit`, failed, and no rewrite happened), `[M15]`'s input reader (a regex matching an interface's
`reason: string`) and `[M11]`'s import (ESM resolves from the FILE, not the cwd). Every one was caught by
reading the raw output rather than the summary line; every first run is kept in the results. And at close
**`pnpm lint` was red on two probes** — ESLint reads `docs/superpowers/spikes/`: `hmac.cjs`'s `require()`
(now `hmac.mjs`, re-run, identical output) and an unused variable in `normalise.mjs` (re-run, identical).

**F12 — A FORM-ENCODED WEBHOOK IS REFUSED `415` BEFORE HMAC RUNS.** `[M9]`: GitHub Apps can be configured
to send `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`; the scoped parser takes only JSON. The fake sends JSON; **a
real App must be registered with content type `application/json`**. Task 9's block says so.

#### Negative controls

All of the task's own held: `[M6]`'s classic token caught before *stateless: none* was believed; `[M5]`'s
form C authenticated against a server that accepts ONLY the canary, and a wrong token was refused. **One
could not fail as written** — `[M7]`'s (F9) — and was replaced. The added ones are in the README's
table: the forced fetch that DOES move the mirror, the fast-forward that DOES land on a protected
upstream, the `enum` that does not run, the build context without which `build` skips both services, the
`merge-base` that proves a rewrite happened, and an installation token without `token` refused by
GitHub's schema.

  180.5 s).** `pnpm lint` clean at open, **red at close on two probes (F11), clean after**; `pnpm
  typecheck` and `pnpm format:check` clean. **`make doctor` 19 / 0 / 0 warnings, `make verify` 55 / 0**

- Snapshot diff (`scripts/snapshot-machine.sh`, 13:25 → 14:04): uptimes, free disk 104 → 120 GiB (not this
  sitting's — it wrote a few hundred KB), and the working tree's dirty count (this sitting's own files).
- `dead-app-resources.sh`: **none dead**; `litellm-orphans.sh`: **nothing to delete** — nothing to
  `--apply`. Images: `docker images -q` **192**, `sort -u` **184**, `127.0.0.1:7107/local/*` **145** —
  unchanged from §7e's hand-off.
- The database as `[M1]` found it before the tests truncated it; **after the close's `pnpm test` it holds

- Nothing listens on 7100, 7102, 7104 or 7110; three addresses on `lo0`; Ollama 0.34.4 with no model
  resident (`ministral-3` unloaded, decision 6).

#### After the sitting — Rich's two decisions, the same day (2026-09-24)

**1. F7 is decided: the approval summary becomes STRUCTURED OUTPUT.** Asked what the model is actually for,
the answer was one sentence per change about what it could expose; the change, its security note, the
reviewer's verdict and the coverage limit are data the platform already holds. **Task 13 is rewritten**
(its `[M15]` block, Decision 19 rewritten, **Decision 22 added**): the model is handed facts as JSON and fills
a schema with no field a verdict could go in. **Measured before rewriting it** (`probes/structured.mjs`,
through LiteLLM 1.98.0 on `qwen3.5:4b`): **10 of 10 parsed and matched, 0 decision words, 0 Markdown, ~3.3 s
each**; one paraphrase got a fact wrong (*"the full name attribute"* for `sn`), so the console shows the
deterministic change line first. **`probes/structured-control.mjs` proves the schema is what does it**: a field
renamed in the schema alone was used 3 of 3 times, and with no schema the model invented its own keys and
fenced them in Markdown 1 time in 3. LiteLLM's adapter (`llms/ollama/chat/transformation.py`) maps
`response_format: json_schema` to Ollama's `format: <schema>` — read, then measured.

**2. The chat model is `qwen3.5:4b`, with thinking OFF.** It is the model S3 measured as a thinking model and
rejected (Evidence 9). Measured again: with the mapping switched and nothing else, `default-chat` streamed
**260 frames, 258 of reasoning and 0 of content** — S3's failure exactly, and the negative control for the
fix. **`reasoning_effort: none` on both chat mappings** (LiteLLM 1.98.0 maps anything but
`low`/`medium`/`high` to Ollama's `think: false`): **7 frames, 5 of content, 0 of reasoning**, on both logical
names. `make verify`'s *a streamed completion returns non-empty content* is 55/0 with it; `infra/models.txt`,
the config's comments, `ai-path.docker.test.ts`'s comment and `offline-acceptance.sh` name the new model.
`ministral-3` (6.0 GB) is still on the machine and no longer used; removing it is Rich's to do. The gates
after the switch are in ORIENTATION §2's box.

**3. The same afternoon, the pin was made request-proof and the reasoning names were added** (Rich approved both,
and both spec actions, which were APPLIED after he read the exact wording — the roadmap's *Spec actions raised by
the chat-model switch*). **A request's own `reasoning_effort` overrode the mapping's `reasoning_effort: none`**:
`"low"` on one request gave 231 reasoning frames and 0 content. LiteLLM 1.98.0 maps a request's `reasoning_effort` to
Ollama's `think` first and copies a mapping's provider-specific params over it (`utils.py:4268`, then `:4508`), so the
default names now pin **`think: false`**: with it, no parameter, `"high"` and `"low"` all gave 5 content frames and 0
reasoning, on both names (`probes/think-pin.sh`). **The residual, measured: a request sending Ollama's own `think:
true` still wins.** **`default-chat-reasoning` (internal) and `default-chat-onprem-reasoning` (confidential)** are the
same model with thinking ON (`probes/reasoning-variant.sh`): 300 tokens → empty; no limit → the answer after 451–3,627
reasoning frames, 17–129 s; a request's `"none"` → the answer in about a second. `ai-path.docker.test.ts` gained two
tests — a request's `reasoning_effort` cannot turn the default names' thinking on, and the `-reasoning` names DO think —
and their controls were watched: (a) the old pin back → the first red while the old *streams NON-EMPTY content* stayed
GREEN, which is why the test was needed; (b) the reasoning names pinned off → the second red; (c) `reasoning_effort:
medium` removed from the reasoning names → still green, because Ollama thinks by default, so that line is documentation.
`pnpm test:docker` is now 202 in 31 (ORIENTATION §2).
